<meta>This is the title page of a work by Jacob Böhme, establishing the foundational themes of his mystical philosophy and the structure of the subsequent 177 questions.</meta>

# THEOSOPHICAL QUESTIONS,
->or<-
# Consideration of Divine Revelation,
### what GOD,
### Nature and Creature, as well as Heaven, Hell and World, together with all creatures are;
->From whence all things in nature have taken<-
->their origin, and for what purpose God has created them;<-
->what their use is;<-
### especially concerning Man,
# What Adam and Christ may be;
### guided through the entire process and course of the world until the end, and into eternity
# and set forth in 177 questions
### By a lover of Christ and his children, for further reflection on what man is.
### From the right and true Theosophical <term>Theosophy: A form of esoteric philosophy seeking direct knowledge of the divine through spiritual intuition</term> foundation
->begun to be answered, yet not completed,<-
->in the year 1624,<-
->by<-
# Jacob Böhme,
### otherwise called the Teutonic Philosopher. <note>original: "Teutonicus Philosophus." This was a common title for Böhme, emphasizing his role as a German-speaking mystic.</note>

---
->Printed in the year of the born-forth great Salvation 1730.<-

<summary>The title page of Jacob Böhme's "Theosophical Questions," outlining a comprehensive inquiry into the nature of God, creation, and the spiritual trajectory of humanity from Adam to Christ.</summary>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This is the opening page of the work; no previous page for continuity. The catchword "schrie" at the bottom indicates the next page begins with the continuation of the word "beschrieben" (described).</meta>

# XVIII. THEOSOPHICAL QUESTIONS
### Concerning Divine Revelation,
### arranged in 177 questions.

---

### The Author’s Preface to the Reader.

It is written: *The natural man perceives nothing of the Spirit of God; it is foolishness to him, and he cannot understand it.* (1 Corinthians 2:14.) And again, it is also written: *The Spirit searches all things, even the depths of the Godhead.* (1 Corinthians 2:10.)

If "Master Clever-wit" <note>original: "Meister Klügling" — a derisive term for a person who relies solely on intellectual pride or worldly logic without spiritual insight</note> should attempt to approach these questions and explain them without Divine Light, he would be unable to do so; he might even consider it a sin to ask such high questions, simply because he himself cannot understand them. To such a person we say: let him leave this to those to whom the Spirit of God wishes to give it (the Spirit which searches all things through the spirit of man), for to him it is still an incomprehensibility and seems to him to be impossible.

But to those who love Jesus, we say that these things are indeed well worth searching and understanding, and are by no means impossible. For in a true Christian dwells Christ, in whom all the treasures of hidden wisdom are revealed; such a person knows these things solely in the Spirit of Christ, and not by his own nature or ability. As we have then clearly set forth and descri— <note>The text cuts off mid-word; the catchword "schrie" suggests "beschrieben" (described).</note>

<summary>This preface introduces a series of 177 theosophical questions, asserting that spiritual truths can only be understood through the Spirit of Christ rather than human intellect. The author warns that those relying on worldly logic will find these deep mysteries incomprehensible or even offensive.</summary>
<keywords>Theosophy, Divine Revelation, Holy Spirit, Christ, spiritual vs. natural man, wisdom, mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory remarks and begins the first of the Theosophical Questions. The previous page likely concluded the general preface to the work.</meta>

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 1.

...have written, albeit briefly and summarily, yet in our other writings quite extensively and as necessity required: and we commend the reader of this to the revelation of our Lord Jesus Christ.

->The Theosophical Questions Follow.<-

### The 1st Question.

## What is God apart from nature and creature in Himself?

->Summaries.<-

God is the eternal Unity, §. 1. which flows out from itself, and thereby leads into willing and movements; 2. and has no place where He dwells separately, but is in, with, and through us, in the ground of the soul. 3.

->Answer.<-

God is the eternal Unity, as the immeasurable, single Good, which has nothing behind or before it that might give or contribute anything to Him, or that might move Him; without any inclinations or properties, which without origin of time is in itself only One, as a sheer purity, without contact, which nowhere has nor needs any place or position for His dwelling; but is simultaneously outside the world and in the world, and is deeper than a thought may soar; indeed, if one were to speak numbers for a hundred thousand years together of His greatness and depth, one would still not have begun to express His depth, for He is Infinity. Everything that can be counted and measured is natural and figurative: but the Unity of God cannot be expressed, for it is through everything simultaneously, and is named and recognized as "Good" <note>original: "Gut"; the author connects the word for "God" (Gott) with "Good" (Gut)</note> because it is the eternal gentleness and the highest beneficence in the sensitivity of nature and the creature, as the sensible, sweet Love.

2. For the Unity, as the Good, flows out from itself, and leads itself with the outflow into willing and movements; there the Unity loves or dwells through the willing...

<summary>This page introduces the first of Jacob Böhme's "Theosophical Questions," defining God as an eternal, infinite Unity that exists beyond time, space, and physical properties. It establishes the concept of the "outflow" of the divine will as the beginning of movement and creation.</summary>
<keywords>Theosophy, Divine Unity, Infinity, Outflow, Divine Will, Nature and Creature, The Good, Mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 2, which discussed the nature of the Divine Will and the initial movement of the Godhead from the "Nothing" into being.</meta>

# Fr. 2. Contemplation of Divine Revelation
<margin>A 2</margin>

Willing or surging <note>original: "Wollen oder Wallen"; a wordplay on the desire (will) and the boiling or surging movement of the spirit</note>, and this surging or willing perceives the gentleness of the Unity; this is the ground of Love within the Unity, of which Moses says: The Lord our God is one God, and none besides Him. <note>Exodus 20; Deuteronomy 4:39, 6:4</note>

3. And it is not as Reason supposes, that God dwells alone above the stars, outside the location of this world. No place is prepared for Him where He dwells separately, but rather His revelation is merely diverse; He is in, with, and through us; and wherever He becomes active with His Love in a life, there God is manifest in His working; that is, His Love, as the Unity, is there flowing out, willing, and perceptible. There God has made a place for Himself, as in the ground of the soul, in the eternal Idea or "counter-throw" <note>original: "Gegenwurf"; an object or reflection cast back from the divine will</note> of the eternal willing in Love, wherein Love wills and perceives itself, as is to be understood in angels and blessed souls.

---

->The 2nd Question.<-

### What is the Abyss of all things, where no creature exists, but only the unfathomable Nothing?

->**Summaries.**<-

The Abyss is a dwelling of the Unity of God, § 1. where Father, Son, and Spirit bring forth the Eternal Unity; 2. 3. and that which has gone forth is the Wisdom of God. 4. In the perceptibility of the Love of life, five audible senses <note>original: "Sensus"; referring here to the vowels as spiritual sounds</note> are understood: A. E. I. O. U. 5. The Trinity reveals itself with a threefold breathing, called JEHOVAH. 6. Theosophical explanation of the Divine Name JEHOVAH. 7. 8. The sense of the perceptible opening is called ADONAI. 9. Theosophical explanation of the Divine Name ADONAI. 10. 11. This Abyss, or the Eternal Nothing, sees through everything unhindered, as an eye of eternal seeing, 12. and God Himself is the seeing of the Nothing. 13. Why it is called a "Nothing"? ibid.

1. Answer. It is a dwelling of the Unity of God, for the "opening up," or the "Something" <note>original: "Ichts"; a term coined by Boehme to represent the opposite of "Nichts" (Nothing), signifying the first emergence of substance</note> of the Nothing, is God Himself. The opening is the Unity, as an Eternal Life and Willing, a pure Will, which yet has nothing that it can will, except only itself.

<summary>This page explores the omnipresence of God, arguing that He dwells within the soul rather than a distant heaven, and introduces the concept of the "Abyss" (Ungrund) as the source of the Divine Names and the transition from Nothing to Something.</summary>
<keywords>Abyss, Unity of God, Wisdom of God, Jehovah, Adonai, Nothingness, Something, Revelation, Divine Will, Theosophy</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of theosophical questions, specifically focusing on the internal dynamics of the Divine Unity and the mystical anatomy of the name of God.</meta>

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 2.

2. Therefore, the Will is a pure, desiring Love-Delight, acting as an outflowing of itself toward its own sensitivity. The Will is (1) the Eternal Father of the Ground; and the sensitivity of Love is (2) the Eternal Son, whom the Will begets within itself into a sensitive Love-Power; and the outflowing of the willing, sensitive Love is (3) the Spirit of the Divine Life.

3. And thus the Eternal Unity is a threefold, immeasurable, and beginningless Life, which consists of pure Willing, in the grasping and perceiving of itself, and in an eternal outflowing of itself.

4. And that which has flowed out from the Will, Loving, and Life, is the Wisdom of God, acting as the Divine Contemplation and Joy of the Unity of God, through which Love eternally introduces itself into powers, colors, wonders, and virtues.

5. In this opening life of Divine Unity, five audible <term>Sensus</term> <note>Latin for "senses" or "meanings"; here referring to the vowels as the sensory perceptions of the spirit</note> are understood within the sensitivity of the Love of Life, namely A. E. I. O. U., wherein the Divine Willing and Working reside; these lead themselves into a breathing-out toward distinction and toward the understanding of the Single Trinity, through which the eternal life perceives and understands itself.

6. The Trinity reveals itself out of the Unity with a threefold breathing, so that this threefold breathing enters into itself in a threefold way toward its own selfhood; and this same threefold sense is called by its sensory name JEHOVAH. <note>The author is using a mystical linguistic analysis of the Tetragrammaton to explain the Trinity.</note>

7. For the Unity, as the **I**, enters into itself into a threefold being, which is called **JE**, and the **JE** is the Father; He leads Himself with His breathing Will into the **HO**, as into a grasping of Love, and in the **HO** the Word of all powers is understood, for it creates a <term>Circumference</term> <note>original: "Circumferenz"</note> or enclosure of itself, as the eternal Something, or "Ight" <note>A term used by Boehme to describe the first manifestation of substance out of the "Naught" or Nothing</note>; from this the Love-Delight flows out, which outflowing is the Spirit, who grasps and forms Himself into the **VA**. For the **V** is the Spirit, as the outflowing, and **A** is the Wisdom, wherein the Spirit grasps Himself into a working life.

8. Thus, this threefold breathing life is then called within itself **O. JAH**. For the enclosed Delight is the **O**,

<meta>catchword: as</meta>

<summary>This page provides a mystical linguistic breakdown of the Divine Name (Jehovah) and the vowels A-E-I-O-U to explain how the Trinity emerges from the Eternal Unity. It defines the Father as Will, the Son as Sensitivity, and the Spirit as the Outflowing Life.</summary>

<keywords>Trinity, Divine Will, Wisdom of God, Jehovah, Mystical Linguistics, Vowels, Eternal Unity, Theosophy</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical exploration of the divine nature and the origins of existence, following the previous discussion on the "Eye of Eternity."</meta>

->Fr. 2. Contemplation of God. Revelation.<-

as an eye of single seeing, a pure seeing; and the **JAH** <note>original: "JAH"; a shortened form of the Hebrew name for God, Yahweh, representing the divine breath</note> is the threefold entrance of itself, as if toward the sensitivity of willing, which opens itself through the eternal breathing.

9. This opening, as the quality in the *sense* <note>original: "Sensu"</note> of sensitive opening, is called **ADONAI** <note>original: "ADONAI"; a Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord"</note>, and consists of six powers, from which the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note>, as the high name **TETRAGRAMMATON** <note>original: "TETRAGRAMMATON"; the four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH</note> originates, from which all beings of the visible and invisible have sprouted and have come into form and formation.

10. In the word **ADONAI**, as in these six powers, lie the six properties of eternal nature, as of natural life, from which the angels and souls have flowed according to the inwardness of their **IDEA** <note>original: "IDEA"; the divine archetype or blueprint of a being</note>; and also the six days of the creation of this world, which are enclosed for rest with the seeing life (which forms itself as the **O** into the essence), wherein the six powers stand and rest in silent love, as in the eternal unity, and yet with their self-working, they will and go forth without ceasing.

11. And that is the **O**, the seventh day, wherein God has rested from all his works, and eternally rests; that is, the six powers [namely (1) desire, (2) mobility, (3) sensitivity, (4) fire or life, (5) light or love, (6) sound, distinction, or understanding] rest in that from which they originated, namely in the **O**, as in the dwelling place of God, wherein the eternal love is signified, as the unity, or the *Something* <note>original: "Ichts"; a term coined by Böhme to describe the transition from Nothing to Something</note> of unity, which is the eternal Sabbath of all things of good essence.

12. Thus we understand, (1) how the Eternal Nothing outside of all beginnings is a pure radiance, as the eye of eternal seeing. For all things see therein as a nothing, because the *Something* has originated from this seeing, so the Nothing, as the Eternal Unity, sees through everything unhindered.

13. And we understand (2) further, that God himself is the seeing and sensing of the Nothing, and is therefore called a Nothing (even though it is God himself) because it is incomprehensible and inexpressible.

<summary>The author describes the transition of the divine from a "Nothing" into a "Something" through six powers of nature, culminating in the eternal Sabbath or the "O" of unity. He explains how the divine names JAH and ADONAI represent the unfolding of God's will and the creation of all visible and invisible beings.</summary>

<keywords>Divine Names, Tetragrammaton, Adonai, Six Powers of Nature, Eternal Unity, Creation, Sabbath, Mysterium Magnum, Jacob Böhme, Theosophy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of theosophical questions, specifically focusing on the dual nature of the Divine as both Love and Wrath, following the previous discussion on the nature of God's essence.</meta>

### The 3rd Question.

# What is God’s Love and Wrath? How is He an angry, jealous God, since He Himself is unchanging Love? How can Love and Wrath be one thing?

->Summaries.<-

From the "Yes" <note>original: "Jah," a shortened form of "Ja" or "Yes," representing the affirmative, divine outflowing</note> comes Truth itself. §. 1. 2. Yes and No are one thing, but they separate into two centers. 3. 4. From the self-will arises the No; and the Unity is the outflowing Yes. 5. 6. The self-will desires itself, 7. and it desires the Unity for the sake of movement, knowledge, and understanding; 8. It creates desire and is magnetic, just as the Unity is giving. 9. 10. The desiring will is the drawing-in, from which the four first forms <note>referring to the "Seven Properties of Nature" in Boehme’s cosmology</note> are born, 11. 12. according to which God is a consuming fire; 13. and in the darkness, it becomes Hell. ibid. The 1st property of the drawing-in is the No, 14. as the foundation of Hell. 15. But the Love-fire in the 5th property is the foundation of the Light. 16. This Love and Light penetrates the fire; 17. just as the burning of the fire makes the Love actual. 18. Thus, the Wrath-fire and Love-fire make two centers. 19. The Wrath-fire is the foundation of eternal nature, 20. and the center of Love is the Yes, in which the Angelic and Soul-spirit is understood; 21. just as the foundation of souls and angels is understood in the eternal fire of nature. 22. The damnation of souls lies in selfhood <note>original: "Eigenheit," the state of being turned inward toward the self rather than God</note>: 23. just as their gentle well-being lies in Love. 24. In this fire, God dwells in His Trinity; 25. and out of this Holy Fire, the Yes—Jesus—has flowed forth. 26. In God [Himself] there is no wrath. 27. These two, Love and Wrath, make two principles, 28. 29. where the Yes is God’s foundation, and the No is the foundation of Wrath. 30. The 6th form, 31. in which the Holy Names of God stand, 32. and the wonder-working Word according to both fires, 33. which is the basis of Kabbalah and Magic. 34. The 7th form is substantiality <note>original: "Wesenheit," the spiritual corporeality or "body" of God</note>, from which the visible world originated; 35. in all its beings are both central fires, 36. and within lies the Holy Fire hidden, and locked away by the Wrath. 37. This Holy Fire was Paradise, and must first be found inwardly. 38. 39.

1. Answer. Although we might be difficult for the reader to understand here, he may nevertheless understand everything through Divine power and the invocation of God, if he is truly in earnest.

2. The reader should know that in "Yes" and "No" all things consist, whether they be Divine, Devilish, Earthly, or whatever may be named. The One, as the Yes, is pure power and life, and is the Truth of God or God Himself. This would

<meta>catchword: would</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the dialectical relationship between God's Love (the "Yes") and His Wrath (the "No"), explaining how they form the dual foundations of existence and spiritual nature. It outlines the cosmological process where self-will creates a "No" that acts as the necessary friction for the manifestation of Divine Love.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Love, Divine Wrath, Yes and No, self-will, seven properties of nature, Kabbalah, Magic, Paradise, Jacob Boehme, theosophy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the philosophical exploration of the "Yes" and "No" as the fundamental duality within the Divine unity, following the previous discussion on the nature of God's self-revelation.</meta>

# Fragment 3. Contemplation of Divine Revelation
<note>original: "Betrachtung Göttl. Offenb."</note>

->A 4<-

would be in itself unrecognizable, and therein would be no joy or upliftment, nor sensitivity, without the "No." The "No" is a counter-throw <note>or "opposition"; original: "Gegenwurf"</note> to the "Yes," or to the Truth, so that the Truth may be manifest and be something in which there is a contrary <note>original: "Contrarium"</note>, wherein the eternal Love may be active, sensitive, willing, and have something to love.

3. And yet we cannot say that the "Yes" is severed from the "No," and that they are two things side by side; rather, they are only One Thing, but they divide themselves into two beginnings <note>original: "Principia"</note> and create two centers <note>original: "Centra"</note>, where each acts and wills within itself. Just as the day in the night, and the night in the day, are two centers and yet undivided, except that they are divided by will and desire. For they have two kinds of fires within them: (1) the day, the hot and unlocking, and (2) the night, the cold and enclosing; and yet together it is only One fire, and neither would be manifest or active without the other. For the cold is the root of the heat, and the heat is the cause that makes the cold felt. Without these two, which nevertheless stand in constant conflict, all things would be a Nothing, and would stand still without motion.

4. In the same way, it is to be understood regarding the eternal unity of Divine power: if the eternal will did not flow out from itself and lead itself into receptivity <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit"</note>, there would be no formation nor distinction, but all powers would be only One power; thus there could also be no understanding. For understanding originates <note>original: "urständet"</note> in the distinction of multiplicity, where one quality sees, tests, and wills the other.

5. Likewise, joy also resides therein: but if a receptivity is to originate, there must be a specific desire for its own self-sensitivity, as a specific will toward receptivity, which is not equal to and does not will the same as the single will. For the single will wills only the single Good, which it is itself; it wills only itself in equality. But the out-flowing will wills inequality, so that it may be distinguished from equality and be its own "Something," so that there may be something for the Eternal Sight to see and feel. And from this specific will arises the "No"; for it leads itself into selfhood <note>original: "Eigenheit"</note>, as into a receptivity of itself; it wants to be Something, and does not liken itself to the Unity, for the Unity is

<summary>This page explains the necessity of duality—the "Yes" and the "No"—within the Divine Unity, arguing that without opposition and multiplicity, there could be no motion, feeling, or understanding. The author uses the analogy of day and night to show how conflicting forces originate from a single source to make existence manifest.</summary>

<keywords>Divine Revelation, duality, Yes and No, unity, multiplicity, selfhood, eternal will, opposition, spiritual physics</keywords>

--- Page 22 ---
<meta>Continuity note: This page continues the discussion of the "Yes" and "No" as the foundational duality of existence, following the previous section's introduction of the divine unity.</meta>

# XII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 3.

an outflowing "Yes," which eternally stands thus in the breathing of itself, and is an insensibility <note>original: "Unempfindlichkeit"; meaning a state of pure being without sensory perception or self-awareness</note>, for it has nothing within it that it might perceive, except only in the receptivity of the deviated will—that is, in the "No," which is an opposition to the "Yes." Within this "No," the "Yes" becomes manifest, and therein it has something that it can desire.

6. For the One has nothing in itself that it can desire, unless it duplicates itself so that it becomes Two; likewise, it cannot perceive itself in the Unity, but in the Duality it perceives itself.

7. Therefore, now understand the ground correctly: The separated will has proceeded from the equality of the eternal willing, and it also has nothing that it can desire except itself. But because it is a "Something" over against the Unity (which is as a "Nothing," and yet is All), it leads itself into a desire for itself; it desires itself, and also the Unity from which it flowed.

8. It desires the Unity for the sake of sensitive love-delight, so that the Unity may be perceptible within it; and it desires itself for the sake of motion, knowledge, and understanding, so that there may be a distinctness within the Unity, that powers may originate. And although the power has no ground nor beginning, yet in the receptivity <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit"; the capacity to receive or take in</note> distinctions arise, from which distinctions Nature originates.

9. This outflowing will leads itself into desire, and the desire is magnetic—that is, drawing inward—whereas the Unity is outflowing. Now there is a contrary <note>original: "Contrarium"</note>, namely "Yes" and "No." For the outflowing has no ground <note>term: "ground" (Grund); in Böhme's thought, this refers to a foundation or basis of existence</note>, but the drawing-inward creates a ground. The "Nothing" wants to go out of itself so that it may be manifest, and the "Something" wants to go into itself so that it may be perceptible in the "Nothing," in order that the Unity may become perceptible within it. Thus, the "Out" and the "In" are an inequality.

10. And the "No" is called a "No" because it is an introverted desire, closing itself in "No-ward." And the "Yes" is called "Yes" because it is an eternal proceeding, and the ground of all beings, as pure Truth. For it has no "No" before it; rather, the "No" first originates in the outflowing will of receptivity.

11. This outflowing, desiring will is drawing-inward, and grasps itself within itself; from this come forms and properties. (1) The first property is sharpness <note>original: "Schärfe"; a harsh, contracting force</note>: from which

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains the dialectical origin of existence, where the divine "Yes" (Unity) creates a "No" (Duality/Desire) to achieve self-awareness and manifest the natural world. This process transforms the "Nothing" of pure spirit into the "Something" of perceptible reality through magnetic attraction and contraction.</summary>
<keywords>Yes and No, Unity and Duality, Divine Will, Origin of Nature, Jacob Böhme, Dialectics, Theosophy, Desire, Manifestation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the philosophical exploration of the "Seven Properties of Nature" and the origin of darkness and light within the divine essence, following the previous discussion on the contraction of the will.</meta>

# Fr. 3. Contemplation of Divine Revelation
->A 5<-

comes hardness, cold, dryness, and darkness. For the attraction <note>original: "Angezogenheit"; referring to the first property of nature where the will pulls inward</note> overshadows itself; and this is the true ground of the eternal as well as temporal darkness; and the hardness and sharpness is the ground of sensitivity. (2) The second property is the motion within the attraction, which is a cause of separation. (3) The third property is the true sensation between the hardness and the motion, wherein the will feels itself, for it finds itself in great sharpness, like a great anguish, when compared to the Unity <note>The "Unity" refers to the undifferentiated stillness of God before creation</note>. (4) The fourth property is the fire, as the flash of radiance; this originates in the joining together of the great anxious sharpness and the Unity: for the Unity is gentle and still; and the moving, hard sharpness is terrible, as a ground of painfulness.

12. Thus there is a "shrack" <note>original: "Schrack"; a sudden terror or lightning-like shock</note> in the joining together; and in this shock the Unity is seized, so that it becomes a glimpse or luster, as an uplifting joy. Then the light originates in the midst of the darkness: Then the Unity becomes a light, and the pleasantness of the desiring will within the properties becomes a spirit-fire, which takes its source and origin from the harsh, cold sharpness, in the motion and sensitivity in the darkness; and it is the very essence of that, as a terrible consumability.

13. And according to this, God is called an angry, jealous God, and a consuming fire; not according to what He is in Himself outside of all perception, but according to the eternal fire-ground; and in the darkness, the foundation of Hell is understood as a forgetfulness of the Good, which darkness is entirely hidden in the light, like the night in the day, as can be read in John 1:5 <note>original: "Johannis 1: 5"; "The light shines in the darkness, and the darkness has not overcome it."</note>.

14. Thus we see in the above-mentioned properties God's wrath, as the first property of drawing into the "No": for it does not liken itself to the "Yes" <note>original: "Jah und Nein"; the dialectic of Yes and No, or affirmation and negation, central to this philosophy</note>, that is, with the Unity, for it creates a darkness within itself, which is a loss of the Good.

15. Secondly, it creates a sharpness within itself, which is the ground of the eternal dying of gentleness, from the gentle Unity. Thirdly, it creates a hardness within itself, which is the eternal death, as a powerlessness. Fourthly, it creates within itself

<summary>This page describes the dialectical emergence of light and fire from the "dark" properties of contraction and anguish, explaining how God's wrath and the foundation of hell are rooted in the "No" or the negation of the divine Unity.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Revelation, darkness, sharpness, motion, fire, Unity, painfulness, spirit-fire, wrath, John 1:5, Yes and No, theosophy</keywords>

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 1.

in such hardness of death, an everlasting painful sensation. Fifthly, it creates within itself an anxious fire-source <note>original: "Feuer-Quall," referring to a boiling or welling up of fire</note>. And in these properties, God’s wrath and the hellish fire are understood: and it is called hell or a hollow <note>original: "Hölle oder Höhle"</note> because it is a hiddenness or an enclosure; it is also called an enmity of God because it is painful, whereas the unity of God is pure gentleness, and they are against one another like fire and water: from which also the substances of fire and water in the creation of this world took their origin.

16. The fifth property in such an ignition of the outflowing will is now the sensitivity of the unity of God, as the love, which becomes mobile and desirous in the fire, and creates in the fire (as in the painfulness) another principle, as a great love-fire. For she <note>the unity/love</note> is the cause and the ground of the light, so that in the fire-essence the light springs forth; she is the power of love in the light, for thus the unity introduces itself into motion and sensitivity, so that the eternal power may be sensitive, and that a willing, desire, and distinctness <note>term: "Schiedlichkeit" (distinctness/differentiation) — the process by which the unified divine essence becomes perceivable through contrast</note> may be therein; otherwise the unity would be an eternal stillness and insensitive.

17. This love and light dwell in the fire and penetrate the fire, so that the fire’s essence is transformed into the highest kingdom of joy, and no more wrath is recognized, but a pure love-taste of divine sensitivity.

18. For thus the eternal unity over-inflames itself, so that it may be a love, and that there may be something that is to be loved. For if the love of the unity did not stand in a fire-burning manner, it would not be actual, and there would be no joy or motion in the unity.

19. Thus one understands now in the fire-essence God’s wrath: and in the sensitivity of love, as in the sensitive unity, the divine love-fire; these make two centers in one ground, as two kinds of fire.

20. (1) The wrath-fire in the outflowing will of the receptivity <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit," here meaning the capacity to receive or take in</note> is a ground of the eternal nature, from which the angels and the soul of man have received their ground, and is called the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note>; out of which eternal nature this visible world also sprouted and was created, as a reflection <note>original: "Gegenwurf," literally a counter-projection</note> of the inwardness.

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains the dual nature of the divine fire, where the "wrath-fire" of nature provides the necessary friction and motion for the "love-fire" of God's unity to become perceptible and joyous. He identifies this internal spiritual tension as the origin of both the soul and the physical universe.</summary>

# Contemplation of Divine Revelation
### Fragment 3.

21. (2) And the center <term>Center: In Böhme’s thought, the "Centrum" is the point of origin where spiritual forces manifest.</term> of Love is the *Jah* <note>The first half of the sacred name Yahweh, representing the divine breath.</note>, as the fire-flaming breath, which is called God’s Word; it is the breath of the unity of God, the foundation of power. Within this, the true Holy Spirit is understood in the outflowing of the breath of Love, as the movement or the life of Love. Also, the Angelic as well as the soulful spirit is understood to be within it, in which God is manifest and dwells.

22. But the ground of souls and angels, according to their nature, is understood to be in the eternal fire of nature. For the clear Godhead does not become a creature (for it is an eternal unity), but rather it dwells through nature, just as a fire glows through iron.

23. And we understand in this place the possibility of the damnation of angels and souls: if they lose the fire of Love, so that they separate themselves from the Divine Unity and enter into their own desire, then the fire of wrath burns in them and is their very life.

24. But if the Divine fire of Love burns in their central fire, then their fire-life is a pure joy and gentle well-being, and the fire of God and the fire of nature stand within them in a single ground.

25. In this fifth property, the glory and majesty of God become manifest as a light of Love. Of this the Scripture says (1 Timothy 6:16): *God dwells in a light that no one can approach*; <note>original Latin/Greek context: "Habitans lucem inaccessibilem"</note> to indicate that no creature was ever born out of the central fire of Love, for it is the most holy fire, and God in His Trinity <gloss>Threefoldness</gloss> Himself.

26. And out of this holy fire has flowed the *Jah*, as a ray of the perceptible Unity; that is the precious name JESUS, who redeemed the poor soul again from the fire of wrath. By assuming humanity, He gave Himself into the deviated central fire of God’s wrath within the soul, and ignited it again with the fire of Love, and united it with God.

27. O you humans, take note of this! Understand now the true foundation: In God there is no wrath, He is pure, sheer Love; only in the foundation through which Love becomes movable is there a fire of wrath—but in God it is only a cause of the

...rich in joy and powers; and in the center of the wrath-fire is the greatest, most terrifying darkness, pain, and torment.

28. And these two are within one another like day and night, where neither may grasp the other, but one dwells in the other, and they make two <term>Principles (Principia)</term> <note>The fundamental origins or starting points of reality in Böhme's thought</note>, as two eternal beginnings.

29. The first beginning is called the Kingdom of God in Love: and the other beginning is called the Kingdom of God’s Wrath, as the foundation of Hell, wherein the cast-away spirits dwell.

30. The foundation of the Kingdom of God is pure <term>Yes (Jah)</term> <note>The affirmative, creative power of the divine</note>, as the powers of the distinct Word; and the foundation of the Wrath of God is pure No, from which lies originate; therefore Christ said: The Devil was a father of lies, for his foundation is pure No, and a contradiction of the Truth, which is the Yes.

31. The sixth property in the flowed-out will is the resonance, sound, understanding, speech, or distinction, as the true intellect; and it stands in both central fires at once. In the center of the self-receptivity of the natural fire, without the cooperation of the Holy Fire (insofar as these two fires are separated, as is to be understood with the devils and damned souls), it is not understanding, but only cunning and shrewdness, as a testing of the foundation of nature; a vain misuse of the powers of nature, which originates from deceit, suspicion, folly, foolishness, and levity.

32. In this sixth property stand the Holy Names, as the Divine powers in the opening of the Unity, in working and willing, and they stand in both fires at once; namely, in the fire of natural mobility, and in the fire of the love-flame.

33. Here stands the miracle-working Word in its operation, for the great name of God <term>TETRAGRAMMATON (JeHoVaH)</term> <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, considered by mystics to contain the secret structure of the universe</note> is the center of the wonders of God here, which works in both central fires, which the evil spirits—in their transformation according to the center of nature's fire—misuse.

34. And the ground of the entire <term>Cabala</term> <note>Jewish mystical tradition, here used to mean the hidden spiritual meaning of the scriptures</note> and <term>Magic (Magia)</term> <note>The spiritual power of the will to act upon nature</note> is contained in this ground; for these are the actual powers where the insensible works together with the sensible. And in this place lies the Law of Moses before it, so as not to misuse it.

35. The seventh property of the flowed-forth, longing will is the **Substantiality** <note>original: "Wesenheit"</note>, wherein all powers lie and work within the essence, as a **Subject** <note>original: "Subjectum"; here meaning the underlying foundation or vessel</note> of all powers, from which the visible world has sprung, and through the movement of the **Wonder-working Name** <note>The Tetragrammaton or the creative Word of God</note> flowed forth, and passed into differentiation and formality.

36. Therefore, in all beings of this world, both **Central Fires** are present, according to God’s Love and Wrath, as can be seen in the creatures.

37. But the Holy Fire lies hidden within, which the curse (as the movement of God’s wrath) keeps locked away along with sin, as is to be understood by the **Tincture** <note>A term from alchemy used here to describe the spiritual essence or "soul-medicine" that can transmute base nature into divine light</note>, and yet a possible entrance exists through God’s permission.

38. That same flowed-forth Holy Fire, as it still worked through the earth, was **Paradise**, and it truly still is; but man has been cast out, and many a person seeks themselves to death for this fire and yet finds it not, unless he has first found it within himself.

39. Thus understand us in this question of God’s Love and Wrath: that two kinds of fire are to be understood, namely (1.) a **Love-fire**, where there is pure light, which is called God’s Love, as the **perceptible Unity**. (2.) And a **Wrath-fire** from the receptivity of the flowed-forth self-will, through which the Love-fire becomes manifest; which Wrath-fire is a ground of the **eternal Nature**, and in the center of its inwardness is called an eternal darkness and pain: And yet both fires are but a single ground, and have been from eternity to eternity, and remain so, but they separate into two eternal beginnings, as can be reflected upon in the relationship between [physical] fire and light.

### The 4th Question.
## What existed before the angels and creation were?

->Summary.<-

God existed with the two central fires as an eternal birthing of wonders, where all creatures lay within as an **IDEA** <note>The divine blueprints or archetypes of all things before they were physically created</note>, §. 1. and both central fires were a Single Being, however

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 4.

in two principles. 2. Now that the central fire of the self-will has moved itself: thus the creation has come forth, so that the Idea became pictorial; 3. In which movement the hellish foundation also broke forth, 4. after Lucifer turned away from God's love into the central fire of wrath, 5. which is now a center of the visible world, and is called Satan. 6.

1. Answer. God existed with the two central fires <note>The "two fires" refer to the fire of God's wrath/strength and the fire of God's love/light</note>, with the great powers, as an eternal infinite birthing of wonders, colors, and virtues, wherein the angels and the soul of man, together with all creatures of this world and the internal angelic spiritual world, lay within an IDEA or spiritual modeling <note>original: "Inmodelung," referring to an internal shaping or blueprint</note>; therein God has seen all His works from eternity, not in a creaturely, formed manner and shape, as in a separation, but in the formality of the powers, where God's Spirit played with itself.

2. In these central fires was the Element, and both central fires were a single being, distinguished only in two principles, like fire and light.

3. But when the central fire of the self-will moved itself, and led into a greater desire for its own contemplation and formation, then the creation came forth, which the eternal will of God moved according to both fires, so that the Idea became pictorial <note>meaning the abstract divine thought took on a visible or "imaged" form</note> to the praise of God's wonders.

4. And in such movement the hellish foundation of God's wrath broke forth, which God cast out from His operation and enclosed in the darkness; there it stands even today, like a hungry palate, full of desire for the creation, and wishes also to be creaturely and pictorial.

5. And that is the ground and the cause that the Throne-Prince Lucifer turned himself away from God's love into the central fire of wrath, wherein he intended to rule over God's gentleness and love, but for that reason was cast out from the central love-fire, and lost his throne in the light, and now possesses the hell; thus it also happens to the damned souls.

6. This hellish foundation in the curse of God's wrath is a center of the visible world, and is called Satan.

### The 5th Question.
## What was the ground and the essence from which the angels were created; what was that same power in the Word of God which flowed out and became a creaturely being?

->Summaries.<-

Scripture calls the angels flames of fire. §. 1. 2. The IDEA <note>The "Idea" here refers to the eternal, divine archetype or blueprint of a being before it is manifested in the physical world.</note> was a form of the Divine Name, 3. and became manifest through the breathing in the fire. 4. With the "Yes" <note>original: "Jah"</note>, the "No" was also formed as the self-will, 5. which in Lucifer broke away from the "Yes," 6. therefore the devil is a vain liar. 7. The Holy Angels' IDEA is a figure of the Holy Name of God; 8. As many names as there are, so many are the ideas, like the stars. 9. 10. From the Thrones <note>A high order of angels in traditional celestial hierarchy.</note> the Idea sprouted forth. 11. The angels are distinguished into thrones and offices, 12. and dwell in the inner element of fire and light. 13. Their foundation is from both central fires, 14. from the Divine Senses <note>original: "Sensibus"</note>. 15. The power of the Prince-Angels stands in the strength of the fire-power. 16. There are also princes in the elements; but they are not equal to the inner ones. 17. The spirits of the letters and their composition can open the understanding, 18. as in the angels, so also in the human kingdom. 19. The Word of the Lord <note>original: "Verbum Domini"</note> is the understanding: the angels are its letters; 20. the Prince-Angels signify the letters, and the ministering ones the composition: 21. just as the power for the sense lies in the letters, so it is also in the Prince-Angels. 22. Man does not know the power in his mouth, 23. in that he carries the power of all things in his mouth. 24. 25.

1. Answer. Scripture says of God: Psalm 104:4. You make your servants winds, and your angels flames of fire. In these words lies the whole understanding; for with the word "servants," the figurative Idea is understood as the spirit of the angels, from the breath or breathing of Divine strength and power, from the Holy Name of God.

2. And the phrase "flames of fire" indicates the central fire of eternal nature, wherein the creature [finds] its essence

# XLIX. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 5.

of being stands, as the self-will of one's own being. This is understood as follows:

3. The IDEA <note>Jacob Böhme uses the Greek term 'Idea' to refer to the eternal, uncreated pattern or blueprint of a thing within the divine mind.</note> or the Image of God was a form of the Divine Name within the expressive Name of God, in which God has known all things from eternity, as an imagination of the Divine Will; there, the will of the *Ungrund* <note>The 'Ungrund' is Böhme’s term for the 'Abyss' or 'Groundless'—the absolute, undifferentiated divine nothingness that precedes all existence.</note> formed itself into a shape, and yet it was not a creature, but only an IDEA, just as an image forms in a mirror; thus the imagination of Divine Power formed itself in the outflowing Name of God.

4. But when God desired to have such an IDEA in a living creature, as a self-willing entity, He moved and divided the Central Fire of eternal nature; thus the IDEA became manifest in the fire, which occurred through the breathing-out or the *Jah* <note>A reference to the first syllable of the Hebrew name for God, Yahweh, representing the outgoing breath of creation.</note>.

5. Thus the "No"—as the outflowing will of self-gratification—formed itself along with the breathed-out "Yes," so that the creature might stand in its own will: which self-will is understood to be in the Central Fire, as in the properties pertaining to fire, wherein the creaturely life stands.

6. For if that had not been the case, Lucifer could not have broken away from the Good and fallen through his own will. Had he not possessed a self-will, then God’s power would have had to fall; but as it was, the creature broke away from the Good and desired to rule in the power and properties of the Central Nature-Fire, as in transformation and *Phantasey* <note>In Böhme's vocabulary, 'Phantasey' refers to a false, self-centered reality or a state of delusion separated from the divine light.</note>, into which he indeed entered.

7. Therefore Christ called him a murderer and a liar from the beginning, and this because the "No" gained the mastery within him; thus he is a vain liar.

8. The essence and being of the good angels is a power of the Central Fire and the Central Light; therein stands their likeness. But the IDEA within them is a figure of the Holy Name of God, as the Wonder-working Word.

9. And just as the Divine powers and Names are many and without number, so also is there a distinction among the IDEAS within them; just as one power has a different effect than another—though they are indeed equal in God—they are nevertheless distinct in the outflow, as in the Wisdom, for the sake of the manifestation.

revelation and miracles. Thus, there is also a distinction of the Angelic IDEA <note>The "IDEA" here refers to the eternal, spiritual archetype or blueprint of a being as it exists in the mind of God before creation.</note> within the powers; and each one has a greater power and might than the other, as well as different virtues.

10. Just as the stars in the firmament are distinct from one another, so also are the angels, so that there may be a Harmony, serving as a joy and a recognition of the Divine powers.

11. Their true foundation stands in the thrones of the powers of GOD, and they are everywhere (altogether) servants in such thrones of powers. For out of the thrones—as out of the holy, outflowing Name of GOD, out of the eternal Unity—the IDEA has sprouted; therefore, there are distinctions and dominions among them.

12. Although they are all God’s servants, yet every throne has its offices and legions with special names, according to those same thrones and powers. Therefore, there are Prince-Angels <note>Archangels or ruling spirits</note> among them, all according to the property of each throne; whatever power the throne possesses, such is also the Prince-Angel. The others are ministers—not slaves, but voices in the Harmony of the throne, all for the praise of the great GOD.

13. The entire depth within the world and outside of the world is full of such thrones and dominions, but not within the four elements <note>Earth, air, fire, and water</note>; rather, they exist in the pure element of the inner fire and light. They possess another Principle <term>Principium: a fundamental realm or level of reality</term>, namely another world, which is indeed also within this world, but in a different quality, in another Chaos <note>Here, "Chaos" refers to the primordial "hidden space" or spiritual atmosphere from which forms emerge, rather than mere disorder.</note>.

14. Thus, understand the foundation of the angels: they are from the Essence of both inner, eternal, Central Fires. Their powers are the great outflowing Names of GOD, all sprouted from the **Yes** and led into the **No**, so that the powers might become manifest; and thus there had to be an opposition <note>A "counter-throw" or resistance</note> in which a distinction could exist.

This is only to indicate to the reader that these all flow from the Divine Senses.

16. But the names of the Prince-Angels which carry the R, T, or S in their meaning <note>original: "Sensu"</note>, their power stands in the strength of the Fire-might, from the high name Tetragrammaton <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH</note>,

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 5.

->(JEHOVAH)<- <note>The Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God, representing the eternal being.</note> and they are princes over the stars and elements, for they have their dwelling in the inner element, from which the four elements <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</note> have flowed out and still continue to flow.

17. Although there are also other external princes who dwell in the four elements and are called star-spirits, such as the Ascendants <note>term: Ascendants; in astrology, the sign rising on the eastern horizon, here referring to spirits governing those influences</note>, who also have their qualities in the world, but are not equal to the inner ones. For they have a different <term>Chaos</term> <note>original: "Chaos"; here meaning a primordial matter or atmospheric substance</note> of an external kind, of which no more shall be mentioned here because of misuse and superstition.

18. If we wish to consider the powers and virtues of the angels and understand them quite rightly—and not cling to mere images, as foolish reason always does—then we should only consider the spirits of the letters, in what sense and power each one stands; and afterwards consider the joining together of those same literal spirits, from which the word or understanding arises; thus we have the whole ground with "Yes" and "No," their entire foundation.

19. Just as the words are distinguished by the literal spirits (the vowels), so also is their distinction in a creaturely, figurative way: just as the human kingdom is also thus, and everything that is called a creature. They are altogether only divine senses <note>original: "Sensus"; meaning perceptions or meanings</note> out of the two central fires, like composite words of divine powers.

20. For just as the entire alphabet is the whole understanding of all beings, so God's Word is the single understanding of all things, and the angels are His letters.

21. The princely angels signify the letters, as the thrones, and the ministering angels signify the composition of the senses; these allow themselves to be used for the harmony of divine understanding to the praise of God.

22. Just as the power and strength to distinguish the senses lies in the letters, so also the greatest power lies in the Throne-angels; the others are like the joining together of the letters for the sake of distinction, and are co-powers. Like a tree in its branches, so also is their government and order to be understood. For in such a form stand all generations in the four elements, including mankind; almost every generation has its superiors, all of which is a figure <note>A symbolic representation or type</note> of the inner spiritual world.

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains that angels are the "letters" of God's Word, forming a spiritual alphabet that governs the elements and stars. He warns against literal imagery, urging a deeper understanding of how these divine "senses" compose the harmony of the universe.</summary>

# Question 6. Contemplation of Divine Revelation
<note>Original header: Fr. 6. Betrachtung Göttl. Offenb.</note>

23. If man were not so enclosed within the "No" <note>The "No" refers to the restrictive, material, or fallen state that limits human perception</note> that he might understand what kind of powers he carries in his mouth, and what sort of authority resides therein, he would greatly rejoice; but he is not permitted to know it because of the curse that lies before it, for he would surely abuse it.

24. He carries the power of all things hovering in his mouth: if he had the faith to give form to this power, he would possess the foundation of all secrets and could perform wonders like the angels; as the Scripture also testifies: *If you have faith as a grain of mustard seed, you may say to the mountain: Cast yourself into the sea.* (Matthew 21:21). Likewise: *The word is near you, namely in your mouth and in your heart.* (Romans 10:8).

25. And this is understood by our own <note>Likely referring to fellow initiates or "Theosophers"</note>, as mentioned above, and no further, because of the potential for abuse; a firm seal lies before it so that no unworthy person shall understand it, and it is also forbidden to write of this in great detail; nevertheless, the time is born that it shall stand open. But only to the worthy. Enough on this here.

### The 6th Question.

## What is the office and activity of the angels, and why does God’s power introduce itself into formation?

->Summaries.<-

God governs through the angels, § 1. who are powers of the Word of God, 2. just as the senses come from the mind. 3. God is the eternal mind as the understanding. 4. The "Yes" <note>Original: "Jah"</note> is Divine: the "No" is self-hood <term>Eigenheit: the state of being centered on oneself rather than God</term>. 5. In God there are no thoughts <note>Meaning no discursive or fragmented thoughts like humans have</note>. 6. He works everything through the business of the angels, 7. who are workers of the wonders of God, 8. 9. and whatever they desire becomes theirs. 10. The joy of the angels in their knowledge. 11. Their nourishment is a creation. 12. The creation of the inner and outer world is a formation of the powers of God. 13. The visible world is the out-flowed Word. 14. The more inward one is in the power, the nearer to God. 15. 16. The angels are playmates of the Kingdom of Joy of God. 17. The heathens did not possess the inner foundation. 18.

1. Answer. Just as we humans on earth [govern] all things, as the entire understanding with the distinction of words,

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 6.

1. ...govern; so also God works and governs, as the eternal Mind <note>original: "Gemüthe" — refers to the deep seat of the soul, intellect, and emotion combined</note> of the single Power, with such figurative words in Wisdom.

2. The Angels are purely formed powers of the Word of God: for the mind of man is a counterpart <note>or "reflexive image"</note> of the eternal Power of God.

3. For all senses come from the Mind, and from the senses come right thoughts, as a conclusion or Imagination <note>term: "Imagination" — in Böhme's thought, this is the power of the mind to "image" or give form to spirit</note>, from which Desire arises; this Desire passes into a Being <note>or "substance"</note>, from which the sensible longing arises, and from that, the Work.

4. In the same way, God is also the eternal Mind, as the Understanding; yet there would be no distinction within Him if He did not flow out from Himself. His out-flowings are the Powers, just as the senses are in man, and the Powers lead themselves into an Imagination, wherein stands the Angelic Idea <note>original: "Englische Idea" — 'Englische' here means 'Angelic'</note>, and the Imagination leads itself into Delight toward sensibility, which is *JAH* <note>The first half of the divine name Yahweh, representing the outgoing breath of God</note>, and the Delight leads itself into Longing, which is the ground of self-receptivity, as the "No"; and the Longing leads itself into qualities, even unto the Fire, from which the Light springs forth. Thus, these same qualities of Longing are Divine Thoughts, to be understood in two centers: as in the "Yes" <note>original: "Jah"</note> and the "No."

5. The "Yes" is Divine, and the "No" is the selfhood of Nature, as the sensibility of Longing. This Longing of sensibility has become a work, namely the Angels, who are nothing other than the thoughts of God, according to Love and Wrath, as a revelation of His Mind or Will.

6. Not that there are thoughts *in* God <note>i.e., in the undifferentiated Essence</note>, but rather in His out-flowed, longing Will, which leads itself into Fire and Light for the sake of sensibility.

7. And just as a man governs the world and all beings with his thoughts, so God, as the eternal Unity, governs all things through the business of the Angels: yet the power and the working are God’s alone; they are His instruments with which He delights and moves Himself, through which and with which He reveals the eternal powers and wonders, and leads them into a Play of Love.

8. They are altogether merely strings in the great Harmony of the Divine Kingdom of Joy, in the song and sound of the Powers.

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains the metaphysical origin of Angels as "thoughts of God" that arise from the divine mind's movement from unity into the "Yes" and "No" of manifestation. He compares the human psychological process of thought and desire to the cosmic process by which God reveals His hidden wonders through angelic beings.</summary>

# Friday 6. Contemplation of Divine Revelation
->B 3<-

...and they are altogether workers of the miracles of God, acting as the formers of the powers, and of the Holy Names of God. <note>The author refers to angels as active participants who shape divine energy into specific manifestations.</note>

9. Just as we humans form the powers of the senses in our mouths and make them formal into audible words: so also is their work a pure formation of Divine powers and forms.

10. For whatever they will and desire is brought into formation and forms through their imagination <note>original: "Imagination"; in this context, it refers to a creative, spiritual power of the mind to manifest reality</note>, which forms are pure Ideas, in the same manner as the Divine powers formed themselves into such Ideas before the creation of the angels; thus is their imitation.

11. And herein stands the Holy Cabala <term>Cabala: a mystical tradition here referring to the transformative power of divine language and numbers</term> of changes, and the great kingdom of joy, wherein the Divine Wisdom and Science is built and formed through the central spirits of fire and light; and there is such a joy of knowledge therein, that they eternally bow and humble themselves before such highness out of great joy and recognition, so that the "No" <note>The "No" represents the principle of negation, self-will, or darkness</note> does not gain the mastery in them, and they be not robbed of such honor and glory.

12. Their nourishment is a drawing-in, or an indrawing desire for the Unity of God; from this, their central fire receives balsam, so that the wrath does not awaken; and for that reason, they also live eternally in composed humility, so that the "No" does not rise up, as happened with the devils; and the fall of Lucifer stands before them as a mirror.

13. Therefore, understand us rightly: The entire Creation of the inner and outer world (both in the Holy pure Element, and in the four elements) is a pure building and forming of the Divine powers, but according to both central fires, namely in the "Yes" <note>original: "Jah"; the affirmative, light principle</note> and the "No." One outflow has simply proceeded from the other, down to the very coarsest matter or compaction of the earth and stones.

14. For the visible world is only the breathed-out Word, with the two Central fires, which fires have again made for themselves a subject <note>a physical vessel or manifestation</note> with the outer elemental fires, wherein the outer creatures live.

15. The more inward one can come into the power of a thing, the nearer one comes to the Deity, as is to be understood in metals and growing things, as well as in all living beings. For the outermost are the four elements; the second...

It warns against the "No" (negation/wrath) by citing the fall of Lucifer as a cautionary example for spiritual beings.</summary>

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 7.

...afterward is the Astral Body; the third is the *Quinta Essentia* <note>Latin: "Fifth Essence," referring to the purest, most subtle element that permeates all nature</note>, as the foundation of the out-flowing Holy Element; the fourth is the Tincture <note>A term used in alchemy and mysticism to describe the highest spiritual power or "dye" that transforms and enlivens matter</note>, as the highest power of the out-flowing Word, wherein both inner Central Fires lie within one Subject: and according to this, the pure, clear God is understood.

16. 17. Thus, this is the conclusion regarding the activity of the Angels: that they are playmates of the Divine Kingdom of Joy, and are members and branches of the great trees of Divine Names upon which the Heavenly Fruits grow. They have their nourishment from the sap of their trees, each Angel from his own throne; and as the throne is—namely, the Name of God—so also is the office of those same Angels; but the entire tree is God.

18. The wise heathens <note>Refers to pre-Christian philosophers, likely the Greeks and Neoplatonists</note> understood the *Subjectum* <note>Latin: "Subject" or "underlying matter"</note> as the counterpart of such thrones, and they honored these as gods, but they still lacked the true ground of the Inwardness. Among the Christians, however, it has become entirely silent, except for a few to whom God has revealed it, who have kept it secret in a parabolic manner.

### The 7th Question.

## What moved Lucifer, so that he lusted against God and turned away from the Good?

->**Summaries.**<-

The "No," as his own self-gratification, moved the Devil, §. 1. For he did not want to be surrendered <note>original: "gelassen," a key mystical term meaning "yielded" or "released" from self-will</note>; 2. his throne moved him; 3. and in the creation of the Angels, the qualities were strongly active. 4. Since the self-will broke itself off from the Unity, 5. and wished to rule with the "No" over the "Yes"; 6. and immediately the qualities became manifest within him. 7.

# V. 7. Contemplation on the Revelation of God

1. The Answer. His own self-interest, as the **Mine** <note>original: "Mein"; refers to the egoistic desire for possession and self-will</note>, moved him; the outflowing will in the central fire of eternal nature is the cause of it; this will swung itself upward within him and desired to form the Divine power into the power of fire.

2. He desired to test the property of eternal nature and did not wish to remain in resignation <term>Gelassenheit: a core mystical concept meaning yieldedness or letting go of the self-will to allow God to act through the soul</term>, but wanted to rule in and with the holy name of the Throne.

3. The cause, however, which moved him to such desire, is the Throne in which he was a prince, and indeed remains eternally according to his property of the power of fire; yet he did not remain therein according to the holy name of the power of light, but rather according to the darkness.

4. When the movement toward the creation of the angels occurred, the outflowing will of self-interest rose up, and the properties stood in great activity and desired to be creaturely <note>to exist as independent, individual beings rather than remaining unified with the Divine source</note>.

5. Within these properties, the creaturely will of Lucifer drew its source; when he recognized the omnipotence therein and found within himself the wonder-working power, his creaturely will exalted itself according to the power of fire and misused the holy name within him. He did not wish to remain in resignation, but wanted to rule over the thrones, and thus broke himself away from the Unity.

6. He wanted to rule with the **Mine** over the **Jah** <note>original: "Jah"; likely a reference to the first syllable of the Tetragrammaton (Yahweh), representing the Divine Being or the "Yes" of God</note>, for the **Mine** had exalted itself in him and despised the **Jah**. Because the power for distinction and formality <note>the ability to take on specific shapes or forms</note> resided in the **Mine**, the creaturely will wanted to rule in the **Mine** as if in a state of transformation; he broke away from God’s Unity and entered into the self-interest of the properties.

7. Immediately the properties became manifest in him, such as the cold fire, likewise sharpness, bitterness, hardness, stinging, enmity, anxiety, and the torment of fire. Thus he became an enemy of all love, humility, and gentleness, for the foundation of God’s wrath captured the false will.

<summary>This page describes the fall of Lucifer as a psychological shift from Divine resignation to egoistic self-will (the "Mine"), resulting in his separation from God's Unity and the manifestation of wrathful, "fiery" spiritual properties.</summary>
<keywords>Lucifer, self-will, Gelassenheit, Divine Unity, ego, properties of nature, spiritual fall, wrath of God</keywords>

### The 8th Question.

# How could an angel become a devil, or what is a devil? In what essence and being does he exist after the Fall?

->**Summaries.**<-

God’s Holy Name, or Light-Power, is not formed <note>meaning it is not a physical shape but a spiritual force</note>; §. 1. rather, the devil broke himself away from God’s will; 2. so that the "No" remains in him as a creaturely thing, 3. and a painful fire-essence. 4. In such a state, the devil cannot become an angel. 5. He flees from love and lives only in wrath; 6. he indeed has remorse, but no faith, 7. and curses God’s power. 8. For his own self-will has swung itself upward and has forfeited the Divine Idea <note>original: "Göttliche Ideam"; referring to the perfect spiritual blueprint or image of God within a being</note>. 9. 10. However, the devils are of various qualities; 11. for every Divine power has a contrary, 12. 13. which becomes manifest through the separation. 14.

1. Answer. It is not to be understood that the Holy Name, in which Lucifer was a Throne-Angel <note>a high-ranking celestial being</note>, became a devil within him: much less the Central Love-Fire, as the Light-Power; no, that cannot be.

2. For when Lucifer directed his desire into his own power, he broke himself off from the Will of God; thus the Holy Name separated from him, and the Light was extinguished in his fire-life. For he broke himself away from the Unity, which is a balsam of the fire, in which the fire receives its radiance of light.

3. Thus there remained in him only the **No** as a formed creature, and the **Yes** <note>original: "Jah"; the affirmative, divine principle of "Yes"</note> departed from him; for the **No** separated itself from the **Yes** into its own will, and did not want to be under the **Yes** (as under the Divine breathing of the Unity), but wanted to be its own breathing.

4. Thus he remained a pure cold, sharp, hard, pointed, bitter, prickly, poisonous, anxious, painful fire-essence, in which the Central Fire exists in nothing but strife, hunger, and thirst, and can reach no refreshment.

5. For if Lucifer were to become an angel again, he would have to draw again from God’s Unity and Love, and would have to have such a fire-life killed by Love and transformed into humility; this, the hellish foundation [refuses]...

<summary>This page explores the metaphysical transformation of Lucifer from a Throne-Angel to a devil, explaining that the fall resulted from a rejection of Divine Unity in favor of self-will. It describes the demonic state as a "fire-essence" characterized by the absence of the divine "Yes" and the presence of a painful, conflicting "No."</summary>

6. Their entire life is nothing other than the hellish foundation, a torment of the wrath of God, a vain poison and stench, and a dying agony. When they hear mention of love and humility, they flee from it, for love is the death of their false life.

7. They certainly have an eternal regret for their lost inheritance, that is, for the Good; but they can have no faith that they might obtain grace; rather, eternal doubt is their faith.

8. They have been separated from God: therefore they curse or flee from God’s power and will. He is like a withered tree without Divine power, a figure without movement. He is ashamed that an angelic <note>original: "Englischer." In 17th-century German, this frequently means "angelic" (from Engel) rather than "English."</note> character still stands within him, and thus he is no longer in the angelic manner and form. He has entirely lost the image of God and has become like the poisonous worms and beasts whose life exists in venom.

11. The devils have received such a deformity, each according to their specific property, for their characteristics are entirely different, all according to the foundation of the hellish essence. They also have their princely governments among themselves, all according to their properties, such as: devils of pride, devils of greed, devils of envy, devils of wrath, devils of lies, devils of sorcery, and many other such kinds, as a contrary <note>original: "Contrarium" (Latin). A fundamental concept in this philosophy: that every "Yes" must have a "No" to be manifest.</note> against God’s wisdom and truth.

12. Every Divine good power has a contrary in the hellish foundation, as in the "No," so that the "Yes,"
