13. But as to the question: How is it possible that from a beautiful angel, an ugly, fierce devil should emerge?

14. This happens through the two eternal Central Fires, in which the Will stands within the [divine] properties; if one separates itself from the other, then it has already occurred: it happens even with man, that a good man is corrupted.

### The 9th Question.

## Since God is Almighty, why did He not resist Lucifer, and instead let such a thing happen?

->Summaries.<-

God does not desire the devil, just as the devil does not desire God. §. 1. The devil did not will it, even though God willed [otherwise]. 2. Now Love and Wrath have an eternal Will; 3. (where indeed Love transforms Wrath into Love, 4. but does not resist it with force: ) 5. and the "No" <note>the principle of negation or opposition</note> in the devil has entered into its own kingdom, 6. which occurred within the creature's own will; 7. so that consequently not God, but Selfhood <note>original: "Selbheit"; the egoistic turning away from the universal divine will</note> is to blame for the Fall; 8. which its own fierce wrath has hardened. 9. Reason is blind in this matter. 10.

1. Answer. When Fire and Light once separate, there is a great enmity against one another; just as water and fire are enemies, and neither desires the other anymore, for one is a death to the other: but because they stand together in a growing life, in a Single Ground <note>original: "Einem Grunde"; the underlying essence of all being</note>, they love one another and stand together in great joy. Thus it is also to be thought regarding the devil and God: God no longer desires the devil, and the devil no longer desires God.

2. But as to the question, why God did not prevent this during the [initial] movement? It is said: God had given him His Love, and restrained him, just as He restrained Adam; but the Central eternal Fire-Will, as the Wrath of God, did not want [the Love], and it separated itself within them according to its own nature.

3. Here one must distinguish between God’s Love and Wrath:

<summary>This page addresses the theodicy of Lucifer's fall, explaining that evil arises from the self-willed separation of the "Fire" and "Light" principles within a creature, rather than by God's decree. It emphasizes that God's love does not use force to override the free, central will of an eternal being.</summary>

<keywords>Lucifer, theodicy, free will, divine wrath, divine love, the Fall, selfhood, central fire, Jakob Böhme, dualism</keywords>

--- Page 41 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the dual nature of God's will, specifically the relationship between Divine Love and Divine Wrath, following the previous section's exploration of the "Yes" and "No" within the Godhead.</meta>

Fr. 9. Contemplation of Divine Revelation

They are indeed both called God, but God, insofar as He is the eternal Good, is not the Wrath: the Wrath has a different <term>Principle<note>original: "Principium." In Boehme's thought, a Principle is a fundamental source or realm of existence.</note></term>. They are indeed One in the fire of love, but in the distinction they are two; and because they are both eternal without beginning, they also have an eternal will, where neither may kill the other, but each remains in itself eternally: it is only a twofold power, and there are two centers, yet they come originally from the Unity, from One Ground.

4. Therefore, when I say of God’s love: it is Almighty, over all and in all, this happens according to the will of the <term>Jah<note>The "Yes" or the affirmative, light-bringing aspect of God, derived from the first half of the name Jehovah.</note></term>, as the Light; and if the "No" gives its will to it, then the "Jah" transforms the "No" into its power and love. Yet two central wills remain within one another, but in One Ground, in One love and desire; otherwise the angry God would not be almighty if Love alone held Him in her omnipotence—and though there is indeed only One God, Love would not be manifest, nor would Love be recognized without the Wrath.

5. Therefore, Love yields itself to the Wrath-fire so that it may be a Love-fire; but if the Wrath separates itself from Love into its own self-acceptance, Love does not prevent this with force, otherwise it would follow that God would become at odds with Himself.

6. Thus the "No," as God’s Wrath, has gone into a separation from Love in the fallen angels, as into a kingdom of its own; and that is also a wonder, and good for this reason: so that the other angels may have a mirror and that their own will does not exalt itself; also that there may be an eternal joy and praise of God, that they do not live in such a fire-essence, and may turn their desire all the more into God’s Unity, and remain standing in <term>Equanimity<note>original: "Gelassenheit." A central mystical term meaning yieldedness, surrender, or letting go of the self-will.</note></term> and humility, as in Divine Harmony.

7. Now, when one speaks here of the will of God’s Wrath—that it has broken itself off from Love and wished to be figurative <insert>or take form</insert>—one must not understand this as being outside of the creature. It was not a foreign will that was not already present in a formative way within the fallen angels; rather, it was the angry God within the formed creature, which was previously figurative, otherwise their entire <term>Place<note>original: "Locus." Referring to their station or region in the spiritual hierarchy.</note></term> would have been a falling away: but that it is not so is proven by the fact that they were cast out from their throne.

<summary>This page explains that God's Love and Wrath are two eternal principles arising from a single source, arguing that Love can only be known in contrast to Wrath. It further describes how the fallen angels' separation into the "Wrath-fire" serves as a cautionary mirror for the remaining heavenly host.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Wrath, Divine Love, Jah and Nein, fallen angels, Gelassenheit, dualism, Jacob Boehme, theodicy, spiritual harmony</keywords>

--- Page 42 ---
<meta>The previous page concluded with a discussion on the nature of the divine will and the origin of the "No" or the contrary will in creation. This page continues the exploration of why the creature, rather than God, is responsible for its own fall.</meta>

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 10.

8. One must not blame God for the Fall, but only the formed power within the creature according to the "No" <note>The "No" refers to the "Nichts" or the negative principle of self-will that opposes God's "Yes."</note>. This power has forfeited its state and has become a lie—not God, but the creature. It was not the unformed power of wrath, in which love burns, but the throne <note>The "throne" refers to the established seat of authority or the individual being of the angel.</note>, according to its own pleasure and selfhood. And as their king and prince did, so also did all his legions.

9. For when God’s wrath hardens a creature, it is not a foreign fury that enters into it to harden it, but its own fury does this. For this reason, a judgment may be passed upon it; otherwise, if God forced it toward evil, no judgment could be passed, for it would only be doing what God wanted. But as it is, the creature does what God does not want from it, and therefore it falls into judgment.

10. Reason <note>term: Vernunft (Reason). In Boehme’s thought, this is human logic divorced from divine revelation, which he often calls "Mistress Reason."</note> speaks much of God and His omnipotence, but it understands little of God and His essence—what and how He is. It separates the soul entirely from God, as if it were merely a separate being, and does not know what an angel or a soul is. This is the great damage of blindness, which causes people to quarrel and dispute, never arriving at the true foundation.

### The 10th Question.
## What did the devil desire, for which he departed from God’s love?

->Summaries<-

The devil understood the ground of creation and wanted to rule in all things as God, § 1. But he did not know that his light was to be extinguished. 2. He wanted to be lord in the "No," 3. and to work with the power of fire, 4. thinking that the fire-nature was stronger than gentleness. 5. But the Holy Name JESUS <note>original: "H. Name JESUS." Boehme views the name of Jesus as the manifestation of divine Love and Light that separates from the dark fire of the ego.</note> departed from him, and the darkness became manifest within him. 6. 7.

1. **Answer.** He desired to be an artist <note>In this context, "artist" refers to a "maker" or "shaper" who manipulates the forces of nature for his own ends.</note>; he saw the creation and understood its foundation. Therein he wanted to be his own god, and to rule in all things with the central power of fire, and to form himself with all things—even to want to form himself into all shapes, so that he would be whatever he wished, and not what the Creator wished. Even today, this is still their greatest joy: that they can transform themselves,

<summary>This page argues that the Fall of creatures like Lucifer results from their own self-will and desire for autonomy rather than God's intent. It explains that the devil sought to master the "fire-power" of creation to become his own creator, leading to his spiritual darkness.</summary>

to wander, and to bring himself into various images, and thus to engage in <term>Phantasy</term><note>In this context, "Phantasy" refers to the power of creating false or illusory mental images, rather than simple imagination.</note>.

2. He wished to be a fool, and that is what he became; but he did not know how that would be once the light was extinguished for him. Likewise, Adam did not know how it would be when he would feel heat and cold, and when the <term>Central Fire</term><note>The "Central Fire" is a recurring concept in Boehme's theosophy, representing the raw, energetic core of life which must be tempered by divine light.</note> with its properties would awaken within him, and that he would fall out of equality and lose the Central Light-Fire; Adam did not know this either.

Submissiveness did not please him, but rather highness; he despised humility and sweetness (gentleness) such as love and truth, and did not wish to be in the Unity, but in multiplicity.

4. He wanted to work with his own name, as if with the power of the Central Fire, and not with the Name of God belonging to his throne; he set the "No" above the "Yes" <note>original: "Jah," a reference to the Hebrew 'Yah' or the affirmative 'Yes' of God's creative will</note>. Thus he became an enemy of God, and a liar and murderer of the good formation of good powers; he desired to destroy what God’s working formed, so that he might display his own working and formation.

5. The <term>Magical Ground</term><note>The underlying spiritual foundation from which all reality is "magically" or supernaturally called into being.</note> of omnipotence pleased him; because of it he exalted himself and contradicted the "Yes," because in his center it is not a fire-working. He saw that the "Yes," as the outflow of the Unity in the Fire-Center, introduced itself into a light and active love: so he thought the fire-nature was stronger and more than the gentleness of the Unity—more than the formed Names in the opening Unity, which he intended to lead in his fire-power as he pleased; and God had not created him for such a will.

6. Therefore, because he left God’s order, the holy opening Name of God separated from him and remained in the Unity. Lucifer remained dwelling within himself in the properties of the Central Fire, and yet he stood in his princely throne, wherein the holy Name of God had formed itself into a throne; and he was no longer an angel with his legions, but a masked image <note>original: "Larven-Bild," implying a hollow or deceptive facade</note> and a monster.

7. Then, when the Light was extinguished within him, the darkness and the cold sharpness of wrath became manifest in him; he immediately became an enemy of God and of all the angelic hosts.

### The 11th Question.
### What was the battle between Michael and the Dragon? Who is Michael and who is the Dragon, and how did the victory and the expulsion happen?

->Summaries.<-

What Michael is? §. 1. This name is transformed into Jesus. 2. The Dragon is the hellish foundation, 3. acting as the self-will, the power of wrath; 4. 5. and Satan is this will of selfhood, the first Principle <note>original: "1. Principium"; in Böhme's thought, the first principle is the dark, fiery source of existence without the light of love</note>, 6. 7. Hell, 8. Belial, 9. Beelzebub, 10. Asmodeus, 11. Lucifer; 12. which qualities are also within man. 13. For within man is the struggle between the figurative "Yes" and "No," 14. 15. in which struggle the Idea <note>The divine image or spiritual pattern</note> must triumph, just as Christ did in the wilderness. 16. This struggle was also on Sinai, 17. but Israel fell away; 18. therefore Christ had to die. 19. 20. From the "Acceptance" <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit"; refers to the self-centered grasping or "taking in" of the soul</note> the Dragon arises. 21. 22. Thus Love and Wrath have struggled with one another. 23.

1. Answer. MICHAEL is the high name of the Divine Figure of this throne, in which Lucifer was a prince; it is he who separated himself from the "No," as if from lies, in which name and power Lucifer was meant to work with God. It was the might and power of the strong Love of God in this throne, and remains so eternally. Likewise, it is the throne itself according to the Divine Figure in the Unity—not as a creature, but as a figure of the power of God in the opening or movement of the Unity.

2. Which name afterwards—when the Unity moved once more—was assigned to mankind through the outflowing of JESUS; for this reason, the Devil became an enemy of mankind, because they are meant to possess his throne; therefore he desires to draw men into his kingdom.

3. The Dragon, however, is the hellish foundation in its manifestation, in which Lucifer had formed himself with his legions; it had risen up with the throne-prince Lucifer as the central fire-kingdom according to the "Acceptance" <note>The selfish grasping of the will</note>,

<meta>catchword: -ceptance,</meta>

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains the metaphysical identity of Michael and the Dragon, framing their conflict as the struggle between divine Love and the fiery self-will of the "No." He links this cosmic battle to the internal spiritual life of man and the eventual replacement of Lucifer's throne by humanity through Jesus.</summary>

Likewise: It is the fury, a torment and essence of heat, cold, hardness, sharpness, sting, bitterness, anxiety, and woe; it is the sensitivity, the first principle <note>original: "Principium"</note>, a vain hunger and thirst, a desire for vanity and lies, a stench of poison and the torment of death.

14. This is now the Dragon, the old serpent, with whom Michael (as the figure of Divine Power) has fought, and cast him along with his legions out of the holy Name; and within man, the name JESUS fights against this dragon.

15. This struggle is not a creaturely, figurative being <note>The author means the battle is a fundamental spiritual reality rather than just a physical event between two individuals</note>. 17. This struggle was with Moses on Mount Sinai, in the Father’s property in the fire, as he waited there for 40 days, when Israel was tested to see whether they would or could persist in the Father’s property.

18. But as it could not be, Israel fell away and made themselves an idol, the golden calf; therefore the Tablet of the Law was broken, to signify that the human will of selfhood <note>term: Eigenheit — the state of being self-centered or "own-ness" as opposed to being resigned to God</note> could not conquer against the foundation of the Wrath; it had to be broken and killed, and led back into the holy Name through death.

19. 22. Thus Love and Wrath may stand undivided in a single ground and be entirely one thing; this is how one contemplates God, and also the holy angels.

23. ### The 12th Question.

## How does one contemplate God’s eternal counsel in Divine Vision, since the Spirit searches all things, even the depths of the Deity—as Saint Paul says—and since it does not lie within human capacity (as a creature), and yet is possible? How may a human being truly understand such a ground of the deep Unity?

# XLIX. Theological Questions. Fragment 12.

### Summaries.

Love and wrath stand in constant wrestling. §. 1. 2. The powers of nature also form themselves in a dark, hard manner. 3. Which formation of Ideas has existed from eternity, yet not out of God’s <term>Counsel</term><note>German: Rathschlag; referring to a formal deliberation or decree</note>, but only as a play of love. 4. The purpose stands in its own will, 5. and is also the very ground of the Devil; 6. against which formed false wills Michael has fought. 7. Examples from Holy Scripture: Saul and Zedekiah. 8. In the light is nothing but love: 9. but in the power of fire arise the rays of wrath, as opinions, 10. which are nothing but evil spirits. 11. Into this conflict JESUS has lowered himself into humanity. 12-14. Man is God’s image according to love and wrath, as 1) the soul, 2) the spirit of the soul, as the temple of the Holy Spirit, 15. 16. just as the body is from the elements and stars. 17. To this spirit, together with the <term>Idea</term><note>The eternal prototype or spiritual form of a being</note>, Christ has come to help. 18. In which entry of Christ into the soul the Idea becomes movable, and unites the "No" with the "Yes"; 19. thus in this light the soul also becomes seeing, and hears what God speaks within it. 20. Thus man comes to <term>contemplation</term><note>German: Schaulichkeit; the state of direct spiritual vision or intuitive seeing</note>. 21.

1. Answer. Favorable reader, this question is placed here because unenlightened reason considers it impossible to know such mysteries, since it cannot grasp them, and therefore blasphemes and ascribes such things to the Devil: so we wish to explain it a little for the sake of love for our neighbor, if perhaps some might become seeing and understand the deep meaning.

2. Reason runs in vain imagery according to a creaturely manner, and thinks: God deliberated within his Trinity what He wanted to make, and how it must and should go, which is why it also wants to enclose the fall of Lucifer and Adam within a certain destination that God had ordained in his purpose; but a thought of reason is not the <term>Ground</term><note>German: Grund; the underlying foundation or origin of being</note>. God does not need to deliberate about anything, for He is the Counsel Himself; the high, outflowing names of the powers are the Counsel of God, as the <term>Wonder-working Word</term><note>German: Wunderthuende Wort; the creative Logos</note>. There is also no pre-determined purpose for any thing, for the origin of all things—how they can happen—lies in the Idea, in eternal formation; not as a finished thing, but in everlasting formation where God’s love and wrath, as the two central fires of the powers, stand in everlasting love-wrestling: there, in such wrestling of the powers, the Wonder-working Word forms itself into figures, which are pure counter-images of the holy names and powers, and are called Ideas.

3. Conversely, the powers of eternal nature also form themselves, according to

<meta>catchword: according to</meta>

<summary>This page outlines the mystical struggle between divine love and wrath, arguing that creation and the "Ideas" of things arise from a dynamic wrestling of powers rather than a static divine decree. It emphasizes that Christ enters the soul to harmonize these opposing forces, allowing man to achieve spiritual vision.</summary>

Fr. 12. Contemplation of Divine Revelation.

...according to the manner of the formation toward fire, as in a dark, sharp, hard, fierce, prickly, bitter, anxious, fiery, and cold manner; these are not creatures, but rather a formation of the powers in the "No," <note>The "No" (Nein) refers to the contractive, self-centered principle of nature that stands in opposition to God's expansive "Yes."</note> as in God's wrath. However, they are continually permeated by the "Yes" <note>original: "Jah"</note> and the Central Light, and transformed into a play of love—in the same way that a good thing comes from an evil one, or a good, holy angel is made from a damned soul or angel.

4. Such a formation of the Idea <note>original: "Ideæ"</note> has indeed existed from eternity, as the Spirit of God has foreseen all things—what would and could be, if such a formation were brought into a creaturely state. But this does not arise from the Divine Counsel of the Holy Name; rather, it originates in the flowed-out free will, according to both central fires of the powers where the powers draw from and form themselves. Thus, the self-will forms itself into the "No," as in the fierce fire-nature; and the "Yes," or the out-breathing of the Unity, forms itself into the Light. These two figures stand within one another in a single ground. In the "Yes" stands the Holy Name (of Love) of God, and in the "No" stands the name of the Wrath of God. And there, the "Yes" in the holy name of Divine Love wrestles with the "No"—as with the flowed-out self-will of its own self-gratification—for the sake of victory; and yet, it is only a play of love. For the Light permeates the formation of the darkness, and so the formation is set into a triumph of the kingdom of joy. Thus, there is an eternal overcoming of the dragon in God's wrath, and yet it is only a play of the two central fire-wills.

5. And here is the "purpose of God" of which the Scripture speaks, yet it stands in the self-will: God, insofar as He is and is called God, compels no figure; rather, the powers compel themselves for the sake of victory.

6. And here also is the ground of the Devil: how the self-will, as the "No," fought against the "Yes" in a formed manner, and departed from the "Yes," and separated itself in its own movement, and became the Devil—as an enemy against the "Yes"—and entered into its own figurative dominion.

7. We also see rightly in this ground how Michael fought against this dragon and the formed false wills...

There, Michael stands in battle against the dragon, as the name of the throne in Christ Jesus.

20. In this light, the soul looks back into its first-held fatherland, as into its origin from which it sprouted; for the name of God is in it and works in the spirit. There it stands in the beholding of God and may search all things, but not in its own movement and will; rather, as the name of God moves within it, so it gazes upon the formation of the Wonder-working Name. It sees and hears what God speaks within it—not figurative speech, but actual speech in the understanding, just as the prophets spoke. Yet this is incomprehensible to outer reason, of which St. Paul says: *Our conversation is in heaven* (Philippians 3:20); and yet he also said that a thorn was given to him in the flesh, so that Satan’s angel—as a torment of the wandering spirit of lust—might buffet him with fists. (2 Corinthians 12:7).

21. Therefore, understand it correctly: A person sees such a mystery in the spirit of Christ, in whom all the treasures of wisdom lie, as the Scripture testifies throughout. Thus, understand now correctly what God’s eternal counsel is, and also how a person may attain such contemplation; we have here only indicated the possibility, but the process of how one may come to it we have described extensively enough in other books.

### The 13th Question.

## How did the casting out of the dragon and the legions of Lucifer happen; where was he cast, so that he can be outside of God, since God fills all things? Or what is the foundation of the hells in which he dwells?

->Summaries.<-

One's own false will must be cast out. §. 1. The dragon is the hellish foundation, 2. whose casting out happens through the power-names of the Throne, 3. as he dwells beneath the firmament, 4. and his dominion is in the elements, 5. in the flowed-out wrath <note>original: "Grimm" — refers to the harsh, fiery quality of nature separated from God's light</note>. 6. For in the elements two kingdoms are understood, 7. where the good holds the evil captive; although the human will often changes it. 8. The devil is a liar; 9. the dragon a being of wrath, 10. and separated from God: 11. his natural fire is burning, 12. and compared to the heavenly, it is like night against the day. 13. This abyss also pours itself out in many places, 14. although it is not yet entirely manifest. 15. God is "Nothing" in the hells <note>meaning God's presence is not felt as love or light there</note>; 16. so also is hell "Nothing" in God. 17. In the light, God is "Something" <note>original: "Ichts" — a term coined by Boehme to describe the manifestation of the divine "Something" out of the "Nothing"</note>: in the hells, Nothing. 18. Therefore the devils stand in great mockery. 19.

1. Answer. The casting out of the dragon was the false, turned-away, figurative will of self-gratification, into which turned-away will Lucifer and his angels formed themselves.

2. The dragon is the hellish foundation, and the cast-out spirits are the natural, formed qualities of the hellish foundation. For when they broke themselves off from the holy name of God, from the essence of the unity of God, and formed themselves entirely into the "No," as into lies, they became such.

<summary>This page addresses the metaphysical origin of evil, explaining that Lucifer's fall was a result of his will turning away from divine unity toward self-centeredness. It defines hell not as a physical location outside of God's omnipresence, but as a state of "wrath" where God's light is perceived as "Nothing."</summary>

<keywords>Spirit of Christ, eternal counsel, Lucifer, fall of the dragon, foundation of hell, wrath, Ichts and Nichts, human will, elements</keywords>

--- Page 54 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the fall of Lucifer and the nature of the demonic realm within the elemental world, following the previous section on the origin of evil.</meta>

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 13.

3. The expulsion occurred through the Holy Power-Name of his former throne; he was cast out from his throne with his legions, as if from his own dwelling, and was immediately enclosed by the darkness and seized by the wrath <note>German: *Grimm*; in Böhme’s thought, this refers to the harsh, consuming quality of God's fire when separated from His love</note> of the hellish foundation.

4. Thus he is separated from God’s holiness, and dwells beneath the firmament and upon the earth between time and eternity, as a Prince of Darkness in the wrath of God.

5. His princely government is in the heights, but in diverse places, as well as in the elements in all locations, according to the nature and property of the four elements <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</note>, according to their characteristics: for their characteristics are manifold, and they have dwellings in all four elements, each species among them within its own likeness.

6. However, this must be understood correctly: they do not possess the elements according to their good qualities, no: but only the outflowing wrath, derived from the eternal wrath of the hellish foundation.

7. For two kingdoms are always understood to be within the elements: one according to God’s poured-out love, and the other according to His anger; thus they dwell only in the portion of anger and are enclosed in the eternal night, and they may not touch the good powers of the elements: but whatever flows out of the wrath and forms itself into a substance, therein they can indeed dwell; it is with this substance that sorcery <note>German: *Zauberey*</note> is practiced.

8. There are indeed some powers in which the Holy Power of the central light-fire has flowed out, which are entirely contrary to the hellish foundation: But in most (people) evil and good are together, and the good holds the evil captive and penetrates it so that they [the demons] can achieve nothing therein; unless the will of man transforms it by his own power, or it is transformed by the *Great Turba* <note>Latin: *Turba Magna*; refers to a state of extreme agitation, chaos, or the "awakener" of God's wrath in nature</note>, so that a movement of wrath enters into a thing, such that the movement of evil overcomes the good.

9. Their dwelling is a pit of lies, a maw of eternal perdition, a formation of the fantasy with a false light, where this pit imagines a light for itself out of the central fire through its own imagination <note>German: *Imagination*; here meaning the creative or formative power of the mind/spirit</note>, which—

<summary>Jacob Böhme describes the fall of Lucifer into the "wrath" of the elements, explaining how demonic forces inhabit the dark aspects of the natural world without being able to touch its inherent goodness. He introduces the concept of the Turba Magna as the force that disrupts the balance between good and evil in humanity and nature.</summary>
<keywords>Lucifer, four elements, wrath of God, hellish foundation, sorcery, Turba Magna, imagination, spiritual warfare, theosophy</keywords>

# Fragment 13. Contemplation of Divine Revelation.
## C 5

stands in no foundation and does not touch God’s Majesty; which, if the Name of God is broken therein, is extinguished, as is to be understood in the case of sorcery <note>The author suggests that magic or sorcery relies on a distorted use of divine names, which loses its power when the spiritual connection is severed.</note>.

10. This expelled dragon, or the maw of the hellish foundation, is not itself the central fire of eternal nature, but rather a self-indulgence <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit seiner selber"</note>, like an outflow from the fire; just as smoke and fire are two distinct beings and yet come from one single ground; thus he is only a being of wrath, like the smoke from the fire.

11. Therefore he is entirely separated from God, like smoke from fire, but receives power and strength from the central fire of eternal nature, for his life stands therein; but his will and desire resemble the smoke from the fire.

12. For his natural fire in his creaturely <insert>or: central</insert> essence is burning: But in God’s Being it is a temperament <note>term: "Temperamentum" — a balanced state or harmonious mixture of qualities, where the harshness of fire is mitigated by light.</note> with the central love-fire of the light. The cause of the fire is transformed into the light, but in the abyss of the dragon or the maw of the hellish foundation, the properties are manifest in their working.

13. The hellish foundation and the heavenly one are toward each other like day and night, and are near to one another, but neither grasps the other, nor does one see the other in its essence. For what in God is a love-burning, that in hell is a wrath-burning.

14. This hellish abyss also pours itself out to some extent in many places through the elements, especially in the earth, as well as in the upper realm, where pools of fire are perceived, sometimes of great cold and sometimes of great heat, especially in great wildernesses and where there are clefts in the earth, wherein the highly damned spirits dwell, as well as the highly damned souls of men, at which the evil spirits themselves are terrified, for it is the burning hell.

15. Otherwise, their true foundation is not yet entirely manifest, and they must still await a greater judgment; the sun and the water still keep their kingdom hidden, so that it may not become entirely manifest until the Day of Judgment; it is only manifest within the properties in themselves—

<summary>This page describes the metaphysical nature of hell and the 'dragon' as a byproduct of eternal fire, comparing the relationship between heaven and hell to the difference between light and smoke. It notes that while hellish forces are currently restrained by the natural elements, they manifest in desolate places on earth.</summary>

# XIV. Theosophical Questions. Fr. 14. 15.

manifest; therefore the devils also tremble before the Last Judgment.

16. Therefore understand it rightly: God is *nothing* in the foundation of hell, for He is not flowing out therein according to His love; He is indeed there, but only in Himself; but to the hellish foundation, He is nothing according to the love. <note>Böhme distinguishes between God's omnipresence and His "revelation." While God is everywhere, the damned cannot perceive His love, making Him "nothing" to their experience.</note>

17. Likewise, hell is also nothing in God; it exists indeed, but the light does not grasp it; they are toward one another as death and life. Each lives and wills in itself, and both are together like *Something* and *Nothing*. <note>original: "Ichts und Nichts." Böhme uses "Ichts" as a technical term for "Somethingness" or "Aught," the opposite of "Nichts" (Nothing).</note>

18. God is a *Something* in the light, and a *Nothing* in hell; for the eternal Unity is a being and power only in the light, and the Lie is a being and power only in its own unacceptability.

19. For this reason, the devils stand in great mockery, that they are so near to God and yet may not reach Him, and that is also their anguish and torment.

### The 14th and 15th Questions.

## The 14th Question: What is the devils' office in hell? The 15th Question: Did the foundation of hell take a temporal beginning, or has it been from eternity; or how may it exist eternally or not?

->Summaries.<-

The wrath of God has been from eternity, §. 1. and was first ignited in the fall of Lucifer, 2. which awakened ground cannot be extinguished, 3. because two eternal beginnings must be within one another. 4.

1. Answer. The foundation has ever been from eternity, but not in such revelation; for God's wrath has indeed been from eternity, but not *as* a wrath, not figuratively, or flowed out, but just as fire lies hidden in wood, or in a stone, until it is awakened. <note>Böhme uses the metaphor of potential energy: just as a spark is latent in flint, the "wrath" was a latent quality of the divine fire before the rebellion of the angels.</note>

2. The awakening or ignition, or the "Abyss of the Dragon," took its beginning in the fall of Lucifer as a creature, when the self-will, as the *No*, turned away from the *Yes*. <note>original: "vom Jah abgewandt." "Jah" refers to the divine "Yes" or the name of God (Yahweh), representing the positive affirmation of being.</note>

<meta>catchword: 3. Because</meta>

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains that hell is not a physical place but a state of being where God's love is unperceived, and argues that while the potential for "wrath" is eternal, it was only activated by Lucifer's pride.</summary>
<keywords>theosophy, foundation of hell, something and nothing, Lucifer, wrath of God, revelation, dragon, free will, dualism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the dual nature of existence and concludes the author's direct responses to a series of theological questions.</meta>

# Fr. 13-18. Contemplation of Divine Revelation

3. But because such an awakened ground has originated from the eternal foundation, and possesses an eternal will, such a foundation cannot perish; otherwise, the whole of creation would be abolished again, and eternal nature would be extinguished in its own self-satisfaction. And if that were to happen, then knowledge and sensitivity would also be extinguished, as would the kingdom of joy.

4. Which cannot be; for there must be two eternal beginnings within one another, so that one may be recognized and felt in the other, and so that the Holy Angels and souls, together with all heavenly creatures, may praise God and rejoice in the good—that they do not dwell in evil, nor are forced to be evil. It would have to be, etc.

Note. Thus far has the dear <term>Theosophist</term><note>A reference to Jakob Böhme (1575–1624), the German mystic and shoemaker whose "theosophy" sought to understand the nature of God through inner illumination.</note> answered these deep questions from his Divine gift, after which he was called away by God into the rest of the Saints. In the meantime, the eager seeker will find almost all the remaining questions sufficiently resolved in the other high writings found herein, which may be searched for in the third index at the appropriate places. Otherwise, the famous English mystic Edward Taylor, as a great lover of these Theosophical writings, undertook to answer all the remaining questions from the same ground according to his gift, and had them printed in England under the title: *Jac. Boehmii Theosophia*, as can be seen in the Biography, Number VI.

<meta>This page continues the series of "Theosophical Questions" (Quæstiones Theosophicæ) posed by Jacob Böhme, following the previous discussion of the nature of the soul and the divine. The catchword "The" links to the start of Question 19.</meta>

# XLIX. Theosophical Questions. Questions 19-31.
<note>original: "Quæstiones Theosophicæ" — Theosophy here refers to "divine wisdom," a mystical understanding of God and nature.</note>

The 19th Question. What are the Dominions or Thrones and Princes of the Angels, both evil and good, in the invisible world; and how is the spiritual world of eternity understood to be within the visible world? Are they separated by place and location? Or what is the inner foundation?

The 20th Question. Out of what was the visible world created, since the Scripture says: God has made all things through His Word; how is this to be understood?

The 21st Question. Since God and His Word alone are good, from what then has the evil in this world’s essence flowed? Such as poisonous worms, beasts, herbs, and trees, as well in the earth as in other things?

The 22nd Question. Why must there be strife and opposition in Nature?

The 23rd Question. What is the ground of the four elements: how does the separation occur, so that out of One, four elements are formed?

The 24th Question. Out of what were the stars created? And for what use?

The 25th Question. What is the ground of the temporal Light of Nature, and of the Darkness? From what does that originate?

The 26th Question. What is the created Heaven from the midst of the waters? And what is the separation of the water above the firmament from the water under the firmament?
<note>This refers to the creation account in Genesis 1:6-7.</note>

The 27th Question. What is the ground of Male and Female kind in this world’s essence? From whence did the conjunction <term>conjunction: the joining together of opposites</term> and desire arise? Might it not have happened in one single ground, without separation?

The 28th Question. What are the Principles <note>original: "Principia"</note> in the Spirit of the World, of the upper and lower essence?

The 29th Question. What is the sperm <note>In 17th-century usage, "sperma" refers to the vital seed or life-force of any living thing.</note> of the generation of all things?

The 30th Question. What is the difference of the seed between metals and stones, and the vegetables, such as herbs, trees, and earthly things?

The 31st Question. How does their conjunction occur, of Female and Male kind, from which their seed and growth arise?

<summary>This page lists a series of mystical inquiries (Questions 19-31) regarding the origins of the physical world, the nature of evil, the four elements, and the biological/alchemical principles of reproduction and gender.</summary>
<keywords>Theosophy, Angelology, Creation, Problem of Evil, Four Elements, Light of Nature, Firmament, Gender, Alchemy, Generation</keywords>

# Contemplation of Divine Revelation

## Questions 32–46

The 32nd Question. What is the <term>Tincture</term> <note>In alchemy and Boehme’s thought, the 'Tincture' is the hidden spiritual power or "dye" that gives life, color, and growth to physical matter.</note> in the spermatic kind, from which growth and luster arise?

The 33rd Question. From what have all creatures of mortal life sprouted and been created?

The 34rd Question. What was the <term>Archaeus</term> <note>A term used by Paracelsus and Boehme to describe the "master workman" or internal spiritual force that directs the formation of physical bodies.</note> and the <term>Separator</term> <note>The divine or natural agent that divides the unified "Chaos" into specific forms and species.</note> of their kind and property, which formed them and still forms them today?

The 35th Question. What are the six days' work of Creation, and the Sabbath?

The 36th Question. What is the distinction between mortal creatures? And what is their <term>Chaos</term> <note>Here meaning the specific environment or "elemental atmosphere" in which a particular species lives and breathes.</note> in which each species lives, and by which they are distinguished from one another?

The 37th Question. To what end, or why, were mortal creatures created?

The 38th Question. From what was man created according to the body?

The 39th Question. What was the "breathing in" <note>Referring to Genesis 2:7, where God breathes the breath of life into man.</note> by which man became a living soul?

The 40th Question. What is the immortal life in him, namely the soul, and what is the spirit of man; and what is the outer life of this world within him?

The 41st Question. What is the <term>Idea</term> or the Image of God in man, in which God works and dwells?

The 42nd Question. What was the Paradise into which God created him? Is it changeable and a created thing, or does it stand in the eternal ground?

The 43rd Question. Why did God initially create only one human being, and not immediately a man and woman together, like other species?

The 44th Question. Was the first man created in such a habit <note>The physical state or outward appearance.</note> for eternal life, or for change?

The 45th Question. What kind of image was Adam before his Eve? In what form and shape was he, when he was neither man nor woman, but both?

The 46th Question. Did Adam also have male members before his Eve, and such bones, stomach, intestines, teeth, and everything that we have now?

### XIX. Theosophical Questions. Fr. 47-57.

The 47th Question: If Adam had been as we are now, how could he have remained in a state of impassibility <note>original: "Unleidenheit"; the state of being unable to suffer or feel pain</note> and indestructibility?

The 48th Question: What would Adam's eating and drinking have been like in the Paradisiacal manner, without worry or need, if he had withstood the trial?

The 49th Question: Would Adam have eaten such fruits in Paradise as the heavenly food will be after this time; or where would that food have gone, and where would it have remained, since all beings of this world are earthly and transitory, and he alone was an eternal, heavenly image and had no need of vanity <note>original: "Eitelkeit"; here referring to the empty, perishable nature of material things</note>?

The 50th Question: Did the four elements <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</note> rule within Adam in his innocence, or was there only one element in the balance of the four; and did he also feel heat and cold before he fell?

The 51st Question: Could anything have killed or broken him?

The 52nd Question: What would his condition on Earth have been; what would he have done if he had remained in Paradise?

The 53rd Question: What was the Earth and its plants like before the curse, when it was called a Paradise?

The 54th Question: Could procreation have occurred without man and woman, since in the resurrection of the dead they shall be neither man nor woman, but like the angels of God in Heaven?

The 55th Question: How could it have been that a man and woman were intended to remain eternally, and would God have wanted to change this creature, man, because in eternal life they are to be like the angels? Was Adam created in the beginning in that same angelic form, or in another form, as he is to be resurrected and live eternally?

The 56th Question: What were the trees in Paradise, which were lovely to look upon and good to eat?

The 57th Question: What was the Tree of Life, and then the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil, each in its own power, nourishment, and property?

# I. Contemplation of Divine Revelation

**The 58th Question.** Why God created [the tree] <note>Referring to the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil</note>, since He well knew that man would lay hands upon it?

**The 59th Question.** Why God forbade man from it, and what the cause of this was?

**The 60th Question.** Why man was intended to rule over all animals on Earth; how that might have been possible, and to what end?

**The 61st Question.** Why God said: *It is not good that man should be alone* <note>original: "Es ist nicht gut, daß der Mensch alleine sey"</note>, even though in the beginning He saw all His works and said: *They are very good*; yet of man alone He says: *It is not good that this man should be alone*; why was it not good?

**The 62nd Question.** Why God caused a deep sleep to fall upon Adam when He built a woman for him out of his rib, and what that signifies?

**The 63rd Question.** How the woman was made out of Adam, and what the rib from his side signifies, from which He made the woman, as Moses writes?

**The 64th Question.** Whether Eve also received a soul and spirit from Adam’s soul and spirit, or if a new, foreign one was specially given to her by God?

**The 65th Question.** How the separation of Adam into the woman took place?

**The 66th Question.** Why Adam immediately took his Eve to himself and said she was his flesh; how he was able to recognize her?

**The 67th Question.** What the serpent was at the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil, which seduced Eve?

**The 68th Question.** Why it persuaded Eve, and not Adam, toward the desire and the fruit? What the fruit was, by which they ate death?

**The 69th Question.** What the sin was, and how it became a sin, such that it is an enmity against God?

**The 70th Question.** Why God did not prevent such a thing—since He forbade it to them—so that it would not have happened?

**The 71st Question.** How Adam and Eve’s eyes were opened, so that they saw they were naked, which they did not recognize before?

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fr. 72-85.

The 72nd Question. What was their shame, that they hid themselves behind the trees of Paradise; and from whence did their fear and terror come?

The 73rd Question. How Adam and Eve, in the Fall, truly died to the Kingdom of Heaven and to Paradise, and yet naturally lived to this world?

The 74th Question. What was the Voice of God in the Word <note>original: "Wort," likely referring to the Logos or the creative Word</note> when the day grew cool? How did God call Adam back, and how is that to be understood?

The 75th Question. What was the Seed of the Woman and the Crusher of the Serpent? What did God speak back into them—was it merely an external promise, or an incorporation of actual Grace? <note>The "Crusher of the Serpent" refers to the messianic prophecy in Genesis 3:15</note>

The 76th Question. What was the curse of the Earth, and what happened because of it?

The 77th Question. How were Adam and Eve cast out of Paradise into the world? And what was the Cherub with the naked sword? <note>The "naked sword" refers to the flaming sword guarding Eden</note>

The 78th Question. Why did the first human (born of woman) become a murderer?

The 79th Question. What were the offerings of Cain and Abel? Why did they offer them? What did they accomplish by doing so?

The 80th Question. Why did Cain’s murder happen because of the offering? What was the underlying reason? What kind of archetype <note>original: "Vorbild"</note> did these two brothers represent?

The 81st Question. By what Grace was the first world saved without the Law, and what was their justification?

The 82nd Question. Was Cain damned because of his sin? What was his doubt regarding Grace?

The 83rd Question. Why did God place a mark upon Cain and say: whoever slays Cain, his blood shall be avenged sevenfold?

The 84th Question. Why did Lamech, his successor, say to his wives Zillah and Adah: Lamech shall be avenged seventy-seven times? What does this signify?

The 85th Question. What was the greatest sin of the first world?

### XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Questions 102–113.

Prophecy? Through what knowledge and spirit did they prophesy?

**The 102nd Question.** What was Christ, of whom the prophets prophesied in the Old Testament?

**The 103rd Question.** What was John the Baptist, his forerunner?

**The 104th Question.** What kind of virgin was Mary (in whom God became man) before she became pregnant?

**The 105th Question.** Why did she first have to be betrothed to the elderly Joseph before she became pregnant by the Holy Spirit; what does that signify?

**The 106th Question.** How did God (as the Word) become flesh; what did He take on from humanity? <note>This refers to the doctrine of the 'Assumptio Humanitatis,' exploring which parts of human nature—body, soul, or spirit—the Divine joined with.</note>

**The 107th Question.** Why did God want to become man; could He not have forgiven man’s sins without the Incarnation? <term>Incarnation: the act of God taking on a human body and nature.</term>

**The 108th Question.** How did the union of the Deity and humanity occur in this Incarnation?

**The 109th Question.** How was Christ born into this world by Mary without violating her virginity; how could she remain a virgin after the birth?

**The 110th Question.** Why did Christ walk upon the earth for thirty years before He took up His office? Why did He increase in age and grace with God and men, since He was Himself God and required no such increase?

**The 111th Question.** Why did He allow Himself to be baptized with water by John, since He was Himself the Baptism, who was to baptize with the Holy Spirit?

**The 112th Question.** Why did Christ have to be tempted in the wilderness for forty days after His baptism? What was it that a God-Man should be tempted; and why should the Devil tempt Him before He began His miraculous works?

**The 113th Question.** How was Christ simultaneously in Heaven and also on Earth?

<summary>This page lists a series of theological inquiries regarding the nature of Christ, the role of Mary and Joseph, and the mystical mechanics of the Incarnation. It seeks to explain the necessity of Christ's earthly experiences, such as his baptism and temptation, through a theosophical lens.</summary>

<keywords>Christ, John the Baptist, Mary, Joseph, Incarnation, Virginity, Baptism, Wilderness, Devil, Deity, Humanity</keywords>

# Fr. 114-124. Contemplation of Divine Revelation

**The 114th Question.** Why did Christ, while on earth, teach the people in parables concerning the Kingdom of Heaven?

**The 115th Question.** Why did Christ not write down his Gospel in letters, but only taught it, and afterwards let his Apostles write it down?

**The 116th Question.** Why was it that the High Priests and Scribes <note>The religious authorities and legal scholars of the day.</note>, who taught the people, had to contradict Christ, and continually blaspheme Him, and want to kill Him? Why should the secular authorities not do it, or the common crowd; what does that signify?

**The 117th Question.** Why was such a trial held with Christ, involving mocking, scorn, and scourging before his suffering? Why did God allow that to happen?

**The 118th Question.** Why did the teachers of the Law <note>original: "Gesetz-Lehrer"</note> have to lead Christ to judgment, and yet he had to be put to death by a Heathen <note>Non-Jewish/Roman</note> authority?

**The 119th Question.** Why did Christ have to suffer and die? Was God so intent on such a revenge in order to be reconciled? Could He not otherwise forgive sin?

**The 120th Question.** What is the figure <note>The symbolic or spiritual representation.</note> of the two murderers who were hanged on the cross beside Christ; and why did Christ have to die on a wooden cross, and not otherwise?

**The 121st Question.** How did Christ, by his dying, strangle death upon the cross; how did that come to pass?

**The 122nd Question.** Why did He have to be nailed to the cross, and why was his side opened with a spear, from which blood and water ran; what does this signify in the figure?

**The 123rd Question.** Why did he have to be mocked upon the cross?

**The 124th Question.** Was the Divine power also present in the blood that He shed upon the earth?

<summary>This page lists a series of twelve theological questions (114-124) probing the spiritual necessity and symbolic meaning behind the specific details of Christ's ministry, trial, and crucifixion. It seeks to understand the "figure" or hidden divine logic behind historical events like the Roman execution and the piercing of Christ's side.</summary>

<keywords>Christology, Passion of Christ, parables, atonement, crucifixion, High Priests, symbolism, divine justice, reconciliation</keywords>

# XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fr. 125-136.

The 125th Question. Why did the earth tremble when Christ hung upon the Cross?

The 126th Question. What is signified by the darkness which occurred supernaturally at that time?

The 127th Question. Why did Christ commend his soul into his Father's hands at his death? What was that same "Hand of God"? <note>Böhme often interprets the "Hand of God" as a specific divine power or "working" within the mystical anatomy of the Godhead.</note>

The 128th Question. Why did some people convert and turn back when they saw what happened during Christ's death, while the High Priests did not? Why did they have to be blind and hardened toward such a work?

The 129th Question. What was Christ’s descent into hell <note>original: "Höllenfahrt"</note>, through which He overcame the Devil and Death?

The 130th Question. How did He preach to the spirits who did not believe in the time of Noah, as it is written? <note>A reference to 1 Peter 3:19-20 regarding the "harrowing of hell."</note>

The 131st Question. What is signified by His rest in the grave, and that He was meant to lie in the grave for 40 hours?

The 132nd Question. Why did His grave have to be secured with guards? What does it signify that the High Priests wanted to resist God’s power and keep Christ in the grave?

The 133rd Question. Why does the Evangelist say that the angels rolled the great stone away from the door of the grave? Could Christ not have risen from the grave otherwise?

The 134th Question. What was the power of His resurrection through death? How did He display victory over death in His body? What did He accomplish thereby?

The 135th Question. What kind of gate did He open through death in our humanity—into God’s wrath and righteousness—through which we may enter to God? How does that happen? <note>In Böhme's thought, Christ "breaks through" the fire of divine wrath to open a path for human souls.</note>

The 136th Question. Why did Christ appear for the first time after His resurrection to a woman, and not to the disciples?

The author seeks to move beyond literal history to explain the spiritual "mechanics" of salvation and the transformation of human nature.</summary>

The 137th Question. What is signified by the pilgrimage of the two disciples from Jerusalem toward Emmaus, when they complained in their distress concerning their Master, and Christ walked among them, and rebuked and taught them, and yet they did not know Him?

The 138th Question. Why did Christ eat of the roasted fish with His disciples after His resurrection, and enter in to them through closed doors, and teach them?

The 139th Question. Why did Christ not show Himself to everyone after His resurrection, but only to some?

The 140th Question. Why did Christ walk upon the earth for forty days after His resurrection before He ascended to Heaven; what does this signify?

The 141st Question. What is Christ’s Ascension, that He ascended visibly; where has He gone? And where is He now?

The 142nd Question. What is signified by the two men in shining garments <note>referring to the angels at the Ascension described in Acts 1:10</note> who said: "You men, why do you look after Him here? This Jesus will come again, just as you have seen Him ascend"?

The 145th Question. What is the Feast of Pentecost; how did the outpouring of the Holy Spirit occur; and how was the bond of their tongues loosened? <note>The "loosening of the bond" refers to the miraculous ability to speak in new languages.</note>

The 146th Question. How is the distinction of languages among them to be understood, in that they spoke all languages at once in one <term>Sense</term> <note>original: "Sensu"; meaning they spoke with a single spiritual meaning or purpose that was understood by all</note>, so that all nations understood them?

The 147th Question. How does this outpouring of His Spirit—proceeding from Christ’s death, resurrection, and ascension—benefit us; and how may that also occur within us?

The 148th Question. What are the <term>Literal Word</term> <note>original: "Buchstabische Wort"; the written or spoken scripture</note> and the <term>Living Word</term> <note>original: "lebendige Wort"; the active, spiritual presence of Christ</note> of Christ together in such an outpouring; how are they distinguished? Since not all [receive] the Holy Spirit...

Does he also belong to Christ in such a state, or what does he receive in the Supper of Christ?

**The 155th Question.** Can Christ’s flesh and blood also be consumed by believers outside of the testamentary order and custom <note>Referring to whether the spiritual benefits of the Eucharist can be received outside the formal church ritual.</note>? Or how might that happen?

# Fr. 156-167. Contemplation of Divine Revelation

The 156th Question. Why did Christ ordain and institute such a **Testament** <note>The "Testament" here refers to the Last Supper or Holy Communion</note>, and say: That as often as we do it, we should do it in his remembrance? For what benefit does it happen with bread and wine? and not without the same; or may it also be enjoyed without bread and wine?

The 158th Question. Is the Testament also powerful <note>meaning spiritually valid or effective</note> in the altered order, or not?

The 159th Question. What do the **Scholars** <note>referring to the academic theologians and church authorities of the day</note> do, when they blaspheme and abuse one another for the sake of Christ’s Testament and the New Covenant, and give one another over to the Devil for it? Do they also perform Christ’s office? Is it right or wrong, do they do such things as servants of Christ; or whom do they serve thereby?

The 161th Question. What is a Christian properly, from within and without; how is he a **Temple of the Holy Spirit**, in that the Kingdom of God is manifest within; how does he walk in Heaven and on Earth at the same time?

The 162th Question. What is the **Antichrist** on earth among Christendom?

The 165th Question. How does Christ then take possession of the kingdom in man, when this beast is slain?

The 166th Question. What is the true **new Rebirth** in the Spirit of Christ? Does it happen in this time, or after this time?

The 167th Question. What is the death of a true Christian; what dies in him?

The 169th Question. Where does the soul go when it departs from the body, whether it be blessed or not?

# TABLES OF PRINCIPLES,
->or<-
# Tables of the Three Principles of Divine Revelation.*

### Explanation of the Diagram and the Three Tables of Divine Revelation.

In these (following) three tables, it is explained and indicated how the hidden God has revealed Himself out of Himself through the breathing out of His power: What heaven and hell, the world, angels, devils, and all creatures, together with all being and moving, are; from whence good and evil, light and darkness, life and death, friend and foe, hardness and softness originate; and how the transformation of all beings occurs; how good is transformed into evil, and evil into good. It is also presented therein how all things are good and useful in the ground from which they initially sprouted, and how all movements exist in necessity. <note>The author uses "necessity" here to imply a divine inevitability or the internal logic of spiritual laws.</note>

2. And specifically, the three <term>Principles</term> <note>Latin: Principia; the fundamental sources or origins of reality in the author's mystical system.</note> of Divine Revelation are indicated herewith, showing how they originate from a single ground according to eternity and time.

3. The first Principle concerns the eternal darkness, as an attraction of properties from which sensitivity, willing, and life originate, which reaches with its ground into the fire.

4. The second Principle is understood in the light with the Angelic or Power-World, wherein the outflow—

---

> <margin>Note: The author had already drafted these tables elsewhere, namely on the occasion of the 47th Epistle; several months later, at the request of his close friends, Mr. Johann Siegmund von Schweinich and Abraham von Franckenberg, he produced this more detailed draft.</margin>

<summary>This page introduces a series of tables explaining the "Three Principles" of divine manifestation, detailing how God emerges from hiddenness into light, darkness, and the physical world. It establishes the metaphysical origin of opposites like good and evil, life and death.</summary>
<keywords>Three Principles, Divine Revelation, cosmogony, light and darkness, Johann Siegmund von Schweinich, Abraham von Franckenberg, spiritual transformation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the three principles of divine manifestation, likely following a description of the first two principles (the dark fire-world and the light-world).</meta>

# XIX. Table of Principles.

Divine power and will revealed through the magical fire in the light with fire-flaming love, wherein one understands the Kingdom of God.

5. The third <term>Principle (Latin: *Principium*)</term> is the visible elemental world with its hosts, which is an outflow from the first and second Principles, through the movement and out-breathing of Divine power and will; therein the spiritual world is mirrored according to light and darkness, and has entered into a creaturely form.

6. In the <term>Diagram (Greek: *Schema*)</term> of <term>ADONAI (Hebrew: Lord)</term> it is indicated what God is in Himself, outside of nature and creation. In the first table is shown the out-breathing of the Divine Word through <term>Wisdom (Greek: *Sophia*)</term>, how the breathing of Divine power creates a <term>reflection (German: *Gegenwurf*)</term> <note>Literally a "counter-throw" or "opposition," referring to how God's will reflects off itself to create manifestation.</note> for Him, and how the flowed-out will introduces itself into receptivity—and specifically into seven properties—toward the eternal nature of sensitivity and reality. In this reality, the eternal will of God is recognized naturally as a Creator of being, wherein primarily the angelic and soulful foundation is understood with eternal, spiritual fire and light.

7. In the second table, the visible world is understood as an outflow of that same inner spiritual world of power; how the properties of the inner foundation have separated themselves and introduced themselves again into a reflection, from which the stars, <term>elements (Latin: *elementa*)</term>, and creatures have taken and received their origin.

8. In the third table, Man is understood according to all three Principles, as a true <term>counter-image (German: *Gegenbilde*)</term> of God from eternity and time: what he is in soul, spirit, and body. Likewise, what he was in Paradise in the first <term>creation (German: *Schöpfung*)</term>, and what he became in the Fall through the spirit of error; what the poison of the serpent is within him, and how Christ has come to help him again and to <term>reborn (German: *neu zu gebären*)</term> him; what he is in Christ in the new <term>Rebirth (German: *Wieder-Geburt*)</term>.

<meta>This page continues the systematic explanation of the divine principles, specifically focusing on the linguistic and symbolic breakdown of the names of God. Catchword from previous page: 12. The.</meta>

### DIAGRAM.

#### Wherein God is considered according to His Essence in Unity, what He is in Trinity outside of Nature and Creature; whereby He fulfills all things, and yet requires no place.

| | | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AD.......... | Father .......... | Will.......... | JE | |
| O........... | Son........... | Delight........... | HO | |
| N........... | Spirit........... | Science <note>original: "Scienz"; here meaning divine knowledge or the innate "knowing" of the spirit</note>......... | VAH | |
| A........... | Power........... | Word .......... | Life | |
| I....... ..... | Colors ........ | Wisdom..... | Virtue |

### Explanation.

9. The word ADONAI <note>Hebrew: "Lord"</note> signifies the opening or self-movement of the unfathomable eternal Unity, representing what the eternal generation, opening, and proceeding of the Trinity of God is within itself.

The (A) is a threefold I <note>The letter 'I' here likely refers to the 'I' of the Ego or the Unity of the Self</note>, which encompasses itself crosswise, as in a beginning, entrance, and exit.

The (D) is the movement of the threefold I, acting as the opener.

The (O) is the circumference of the threefold I, acting as the birth of the dwelling-place of God within Himself.

The (N) is the threefold Spirit, which proceeds from the circumference out of itself as a threefold I.

The lower (A) is the counter-reflection or the working of the threefold I or Spirit, from which eternal movement, power, colors, and virtues arise or originate.

The (I) is the substantial outflow of the threefold I, where the Trinity flows out within the Unity; and in this entire word Adonai, one understands the eternal life of the Unity of God.

10. The word Father is the eternal beginning of the working and willing in the threefold I of the Unity.

11. The word Son is the effect of the power, as the comprehensibility of the will, wherein the threefold Spirit encloses itself as a place of the Divine Selfhood <note>original: "Ichheit"; the quality of being an 'I' or a distinct personality</note>.

<summary>This page provides a mystical linguistic analysis of the name Adonai and the Trinity, explaining how each letter represents a stage in God's self-manifestation from Unity into Trinity. It defines the Father as the beginning of Will and the Son as the realization of that Will into a "Divine Selfhood."</summary>
<keywords>Adonai, Trinity, Unity, Divine Selfhood, Father, Son, Holy Spirit, Sacred Philology, Mystical Anatomy of Language</keywords>

--- Page 76 ---
<meta>This page continues the systematic definitions of mystical and cosmological principles, following the previous discussion of the divine nature and the initial emanations of the Godhead.</meta>

# XIX. Tables of Principles, Schema

12. The word **Spirit** <note>German: *Geist*</note> is the living, outgoing movement within the captured power, used as a likeness, just as one might understand it by observing a flower. The opening or the active growing is the beginning; the power of the activity is the enclosure and bodily casing of the growth; and the scent, which goes out from the power, is the movement or the growing, outgoing joy-life of the power from which the flower springs. In this, one sees a likeness of how the birth of Divine power portrays itself.

13. The word **Power** <note>German: *Kraft*</note> indicates the breathing, outgoing, intelligible, and sensible life, serving as the ground and source <note>German: *Quall*, an archaic form of *Quelle*</note> of the flowing-forth of the knowledge of diversity.

14. The word **Colors** indicates the object or "counter-throw" <note>German: *Gegenwurf*, meaning a reflection or objective manifestation</note> of the power, wherein the diversity and origin of the sensory life of knowledge is understood, and where an eternal contemplation originates.

15. The word **Will** indicates the volition or movement within the opening Unity, by which the Unity wills itself into Trinity <note>German: *Dreyheit*</note>; it is the Nothing willing itself into its own Something, wherein it possesses its accomplishment and desire.

16. The word **Delight** <note>German: *Lust*</note> indicates the actual sensitivity of the will or volition, as the highest ground of primordial love; it is where the will of the Abyss <note>German: *Ungrund*, the "groundless" or "bottomless" void before creation</note> perceives itself within its "Something," surrendering itself to that "Something" as its own sensation, and working and willing within that sensitivity in its own taste.

17. The word **Science** <note>German: *Scientz*, used here as "active knowledge" or "inner knowing"</note> indicates the actual sensitive knowledge and understanding within the taste of love; it is a root of the five senses and a ground of eternal life, from which understanding wells up and wherein the eternal Unity grounds itself.

18. The word **Word** indicates how the eternal love of the sensitive Unity, together with knowledge, speaks itself out eternally into an object; thus, this same Word is the speaking-out or breathing of the will from the power through the understanding. It is the driving and forming of the eternal (single) power into infinity and multiplicity, acting as the creator of power out of the eternal power into virtues.

19. The word **Wisdom** is the flowed-out Word, as a

<meta>catchword: a</meta>

an object of Divine Science and Divine Will, acting as the essential power of the great Love of God, from which all things have received their movement and possibility: A foundation of all three principles; a revelation of the Unity of God: a passive being of Divine operation and the ground of humility; a mother of all sciences of the creatures, and an eternal casing of the active Love of God, a ray and breath of the Almighty Spirit.

20. The word Jehovah is the most holy name of God, representing the Divine, sensory life, the One Good, in which the Holy Trinity is understood with glory and omnipotence; a life of the <term>Ungrund</term> <note>The "Ungrund" is a central term in Boehme's thought, referring to the "groundless" or "abyssal" void that precedes all existence and even God's self-manifestation.</note> as the Unity, which primarily exists in the eternal (single) Love; and within it, the most holy name Jesus is understood as the out-flowing "I": A ground and source of the breathing of the Unity of God: a formation of understanding, for the outflow of the Unity leads itself with the "I" into the "E" as if into a vision or seeing of a chaos, in which the <term>Mysterium Magnum</term> <note>Latin: "Great Mystery." In this context, it refers to the chaos or "mysterium" from which all created things proceed.</note> is understood according to the Divine manner; and it is a threefold breathing of power.

21. JE is a breathing of the Unity, and the HO is a breathing of the JE, and the VA is a breathing of the HO, and yet it is only One breathing, but it creates a threefold exit of three centers or comprehensions: And we understand therein how the threefold "I" finally closes itself into the "A" as into a beginning toward Nature.

22. Beneath this stands "Life," which indicates that this threefold breathing is a pure life and power. And beneath that stands "Virtue," which indicates the immeasurable virtue of such a breathing Life.

23. In this table or schema, it is now rightly understood what God is outside of Nature and Creature in Trinity, as in a threefold out-breathing of the Unity into Himself, where one cannot speak of a place or site of His dwelling, nor of any measurability or divisibility; for He is neither here nor there, but everywhere at once; just as one contemplates the <term>Ungrund</term> as the eternal Unity outside of Nature and Creature, so He is an actual power and essence of the Unity.

# XIX. Tables of Principles. Table I.

24. But that such power and virtue of the Unity is truly understood therein, one understands by its flowed-out power of the world and creatures, which is born out of its out-breathing; and there is no thing in the essence of this world which does not bear witness to this, if one perceives it correctly.

### Explanation of the First Table:
## TETRAGRAMMATON <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH, representing the eternal essence.</note>

25. In this table it is presented how the Holy Name of eternal power—with knowledge and science from eternity to eternity—introduces itself into nature as eternal light and darkness in various properties; how the Word of the out-breathing <note>original: "Aushauchen"; refers to the divine creative breath or emanation</note> introduces itself into an object or counterpart <note>original: "Gegenwurf"; a central term in Boehme’s thought meaning a reflection or "opposite-throw" that allows the divine to perceive itself</note>, and how in that counterpart a self-will and an "acceptability" <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit"; the capacity of a property to receive or take on a specific quality</note> of the properties arises. Therein one always understands two essences: namely, God’s own outflow, and then the properties' own receptions within the free will. In this reception, a counterpart of an external kind is again understood, so that the Unity in its outflow becomes ever more external; and thereby eternal Love introduces itself into sensitivity and a fire-flaming manner, as a working of Divine powers.

26. Above the table stands God’s Wrath, the Dark World, and beneath it the First Principle <term>Principle: In this context, one of the three fundamental realms of existence—darkness/fire, light, and the physical world</term>; and opposite, from the fourth number to the seventh, stands God’s Love, the Light-World, and beneath it, the Second Principle. This indicates how the flowed-out will encloses and overshadows itself with the reception of its own desire, introduces itself into properties through the desire of "selfhood" <note>original: "Ichheit"; the ego-driven or individualizing tendency</note>, and turns into darkness; wherein the flowed-out One becomes manifest and sensible in the darkness through the fire in the light, and is a cause of the light. In this light, God’s Love takes on a fiery effect from the fire of eternal nature, and shines out through the dark, painful reception within the fire, like a light from a candle, and like the day in the night; from which also day and night received their origin, ground, and names in Time.

<summary>This page explains the first table of the Tetragrammaton, describing how God's eternal unity emanates into light and dark principles through a process of "out-breathing" and reflection. It establishes the metaphysical origins of light, darkness, and the "selfhood" that defines individual existence.</summary>

### The First Table.
# TETRAGRAMMATON.
<note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God (YHWH), here used to represent the structure of all reality.</note>

In this table, the outflow of the eternal Divine Word is contemplated: how the Word, through Wisdom, introduces itself from Unity into multiplicity and distinction, both in Light and Darkness. According to these, God calls Himself an angry, jealous God, and a consuming fire, as well as a dear, merciful God. Within this, one understands the foundation of the Angels and Souls, and how they may receive either salvation or damnation. In the horizontal spaces <note>the rows of the table</note>, the seven properties of Eternal Nature are understood as the *Mysterium Magnum* <note>Great Mystery; the source of all manifestation</note>. Up to the fourth line at the Fire, the First Principle is understood as the Darkness and the causes of motion and of the painful life; and from the Fire to the seventh property, the Second Principle is understood as the Angelic World; and in the downward spaces <note>the columns</note>, the properties of Life are understood.

| | I. T | II. I | III. N | IV. C | V. T | VI. U | VII. R |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **E 1.** | Desire or Enclosing. | Science <note>original: *Scienz*; here meaning a proto-consciousness or "drawing" force</note>, pulling, or sting. | Anguish. | Fire. | Light- or Love-Fire. | Sound. | Being <note>or Substance</note>. |
| **W 2.** | Dark. | Feeling, or Moving. | Boiling <note>or surging</note>. | Painful Life. | Love-Life. | Intelligible Life. | Essential Property: Working, or essential life. |
| **I 3.** | Harshness. Hardness. | Enmity. | Mind <note>or Disposition</note>. | Terror. | Joy. | Five Senses. | Form. |
| **G 4.** | Sharpness. | Ascending. | Wheel of Life. | Killing. | Power. | Love. | Seed <note>original: *Sperma*</note>. |
| **N 5.** | Wrath. | Pride. | Despair. | Hell. | Glory. | Giving. | Taking, or Enclosing. |
| **A 6.** | Great Death. | False Will. | Small Death. | Soul-Ground, Devil. | Soul-Spirit, Angel. | Praising. | Multiplying. |
| **T 7.** | Selfhood Standing-still. | Breaking. | Parting from the Original State. | Folly. | Wisdom. | Highness. | Humility. |
| **V 8.** | Impotence. | Self-Will. | Robbing. | Phantasy. | Knowledge. | Strength. | Throne. |

<note>The table header spans across two conceptual realms: "Dark World, God's Wrath: the First PRINCIPLE" (Columns I-III) and "Light-World, God's Love: the Second PRINCIPLE" (Columns V-VII), with Column IV (Fire) acting as the transition point.</note>

<summary>This page presents a symbolic table of the Tetragrammaton, mapping the seven properties of nature across the "Dark World" of wrath and the "Light World" of love. It serves as a metaphysical chart explaining the origins of the soul, angels, and the dual nature of divine manifestation.</summary>
