27. But in eternity, an eternal light and darkness exist within one another. The darkness is the ground of nature, and the light is the ground of the joyful kingdom of Divine Revelation. Thus, the dark world—as the ground of the properties of self-desire and self-will—is called the first principle <term>Principium: a fundamental source or beginning</term>, because it is a cause of Divine Revelation according to sensibility, and also constitutes a distinct kingdom within itself as painful torment. Accordingly, God calls Himself an angry and jealous God, and a consuming fire. And the light, which becomes manifest in the fire, wherein the unity of the Divine outflow of love is understood, is called the second principle, as the Divine Power-World. Therein, God’s love is a love-fire and actual life, as it is written: God dwells in a light to which no one can come <note>Reference to 1 Timothy 6:16</note>, for the power of the unity of God works in the light, and that same power is God; but the fiery nature within the light is the eternal nature, wherein the eternal love of the unity loves and perceives itself.

28. Beneath the first and second principles, seven numbers (I. II. III. IV. V. VI. VII.) stand in the seven spaces. These indicate the seven properties of eternal nature, and beneath them stands TINCTURE <note>original: "TINCTVR"</note> divided among the seven spaces. This indicates the Divine Word, a temperament or equal property, wherein the Divine powers lie within an equal will, operation, and essence, as the flowed-out Name of God. Within this, the great mystery of Divine power and operation is understood through the characters of the letters distributed among the seven properties.

29. The word TINCTURE is the "distinguishing word" from which the seven properties flow. The letter **T** is the *Tau* or the opening of the Unity, like the † <note>The cross symbol</note> of the triple **I**, a ground for breathing. **I** is the outflow from the **T** or the exit of the Unity, acting as the cross-hinge <term>Creuz-Angel: a pivotal point or axis shaped like a cross</term> of life. **N** is the outflow of the sounding triple spirit. **C** is the dividing of the sounding, where the **I**—as the outflow of the Unity—separates itself again from the darkness and breaks the pleasantness of the eternal will. The second **T** under Number V. is the Holy *Tau* or the opening of Glory in the fiery sensibility with the burning love, by which God’s kingdom opens itself; it indicates the great power of the light-force. **V** is

The Holy Spirit’s character is thus marked with three points; two pointing upward signify the fire and the light, and the third pointing downward signifies the unity in love, as humility. With the letter R, the Holy Fire and Light are grasped into a real, natural being: for it indicates the Kingdom, as the throne, and hereby it is indicated how the Holy Name, with the outflowing will, introduces itself into the *Mysterium Magnum* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; the Great Mystery or the underlying chaos/potential from which all things are created</note>, as into the eternal mystery from which the visible world has sprouted.

### The Great Mystery of the Tincture, or the Highest Ground of the Trinity of GOD.

30. **T** Is the triple I, indicating the Father.
**I** Is the born I, which is Jesus.
**N** Is the triple I in the Spirit.
**C** Is indicating Christ.
**T** In the fifth space is the Father in Christ.
**U** Is the Spirit of Christ in the Word, which makes alive.
**R** Is the Royal Throne, around which light and darkness struggle, where Satan and Christ stand against one another (namely, according to the pleasure of self-will, Satan as the wandering spirit; and according to the unity, Christ), where one understands love and wrath in one single ground, but in two kinds of revelation; understood here by our own who belong to God, but for others, a lock is placed before this place.

### The Great Mystery of the Tincture, or the Greatest and Highest Ground of the Trinity of GOD.

31. This table in seven spaces is the ground of the angels and souls, as the *Mysterium Magnum* of transformation, wherein all possibilities lie.

32. Across, according to the seven numbers, the outflow from One into seven is understood. Up to the fire, from which the light becomes manifest, the first Principle <note>original: "Principium"; referring to the dark, fiery source of existence</note> is to be understood; and from the fire to the being, the second Principle <note>the realm of light and divine love</note>, and downwards under each property it is understood what kind of outflow comes from each property in the cooperation of the other properties: Not to be understood that a single property gives such an outflow alone, but all seven—

# Chapter 1. Explanation of the Tables of the 3 Principles.

...give it; but the first form rules within it and maintains the supreme government.

33. Where Number 1 stands as **Desire** or **Enclosure**, one understands that desire is magnetic, and it encloses and darkens itself, which is the ground of eternal and temporal darkness. From such a drawing-in comes sharpness, bitterness, and hardness, which is the origin of wrath <note>original: "Grimm," referring to the harsh, fiery quality of nature before it is softened by light</note>, from which the great eternal death originates. For this magnet draws the power into itself and encloses it so that the activity stands still and enters into powerlessness, as can be seen under Number 1 downwards.

34. Under Number 2 stands **Science** <note>original: "Scienz," used here in the archaic sense of "knowing" or "active sensing"</note> or **Pulling**. This is the second form of nature, acting as the movement of the magnetic drawing-in, from which the sensitivity of nature originates. It is the ground of all contrariety <note>original: "Contrarietät," the friction between opposing forces</note>, for hardness and movement are enemies; the movement breaks the hardness apart again, and yet it also gives birth to the hardness through the attraction.

35. Thus, two essences originate in the desirous, flowed-out will of God: the pulling of the magnetic power gives movement and sensitivity, and that which is pulled gives essence <note>original: "Wesen," often implying a substantial or bodily reality</note>, in which one understands the causes of spirit and body. In the pulling of sensitivity, one understands the spirit, and in that which is pulled, the body, or the cause of corporeality.

36. Now, if such a drawing-in and essence cannot reach the **Light of the Unity of God**—by which it would be softened—it remains within itself a vain enmity and is a torment of raging and rising up, from which self-centeredness and pride originate. For the will of self-centeredness is false and a constant breaker of itself, that is, of its own essence. In these two forms, Desire and Pulling, one understands God's wrath in its flowing-out properties. And although they are the ground of sensitive life, if the Light shines into them, they become the ground of the **Kingdom of Joy**, acting as an inner movement of the Unity of God and a ground of the five senses. From this, creaturely life also took its beginning, and within this also lies its corruption should it lose the Light; for it is then the torment of hellish anguish, acting as the cause...

### XIX. Tables of Principles. Table I.

of painfulness, and is yet also the root of natural life.

37. Under No. 3 stands the third form of nature, and is called **Anguish**, as a spiritual sulfur-torment <note>original: "Schwefel-Qual"; Böhme uses sulfur to represent the combustible, agonizing quality of the soul's inner fire</note> according to its property. It takes its ground from the first and second forms: namely (1) from the magnetic desire, and (2) from the motion of drawing-in, wherein the flowed-out eternal Will stands in unrest and anguish. Anguish is the cause of the natural willing, the mind, and the senses; it is the Wheel of Life <note>original: "Lebens Rad"; a recurring Böhme concept describing the circular, restless motion of nature</note>, acting as a cause of the fiery life.

38. For when the flowed-out Will of the Unity of God stands in anguish, it longs again for the Unity, as for rest; and the Unity or Rest longs for motion and revelation. Yet there could be no revelation in the Unity without motion; therefore the Divine Will flows out from itself and introduces the Divine Delight into the flowed-out Will, into desire and motion, toward a sensitivity <note>original: "Empfindlichkeit"; the capacity to feel or perceive</note>, so that it may feel itself. Thus two remain in One Being: the sensitive Divine Delight, and the cause of sensitivity. In this, God calls Himself a "loving God" according to the sensitive Divine Love-Delight, and an "angry God" according to the cause of sensitivity, which is the eternal Nature.

39. And we understand in this Anguish—so far as the Divine Light is not manifest within it—the hellish fire and an eternal despair and terror, where the self-will of nature stands forever in dying torment and forever desires to separate itself from such a ground. This I call the "Little Death," because it is the eternal dying death; whereas in the hardness <note>referring to the first form of nature</note> is the "Great Still-standing Death."

40. This form, if it does not have the light, is the wellspring of the false mind; but if it perceives the light within itself, it is the wellspring and ground of the sensory mind, and the true root of the fire, as is to be seen downward under No. 3.

41. The fourth form, No. 4, is the **Fire** of eternal nature. Understand this as a spiritual life-fire, which originates from the everlasting conjunction <note>original: "Conjunction"; the alchemical or astrological joining of forces</note> or joining together of hardness and motion. Understand that the painfulness originates from this; but the Fire-Glance <note>the brilliance or luster of the fire</note> originates from the free delight of the Will,

For it is in the essence of the conjunction as if one rubbed steel and stone together, from which the flash arises.

42. Whichever of these two holds or receives the upper-regency, therein stands the life.

44. And two kingdoms divide themselves within the fire: the kingdom of Glory from the outflow of the Unity of God, and the kingdom of the qualities of eternal nature, each within itself, and yet they dwell in one another as One.

45. The kingdom of nature is in itself the great eternal darkness, and the kingdom of God or Glory is the light. John 1:5 says: *The light shines in the darkness, and the darkness has not understood it.* <note>original: "Das Licht scheinet in der Finsterniß, und die Finsterniß hats nicht begriffen."</note> Just as day and night dwell in one another, and yet neither is the other; so from the fire’s self-property comes the painful life. If that life breaks itself off from the eternal light and enters into the opposition—that is, into the selfhood <note>original: "Selbheit"; the state of being centered only on oneself</note> of the qualities—then the life of the fire is only a fantasy and folly, just as the devils have become, and also the damned souls, as can be seen from the number four downwards.

46. In the fifth quality of nature, No. 5, the second principle <term>Principle: one of the three fundamental realms of existence in Böhme's cosmology</term> is now understood with its foundation, as the being of the Unity in the power of light. Therein, the flowed-out Unity is a fire-flaming love, from which the true, intelligent spirit originates with the five senses. The first three

# XIX. Tables of Principles. Table I.

Forms are merely properties for life. The fourth form is life itself, but the fifth is the true spirit; when the fifth property is revealed out of the fire, it dwells in all the others and transforms them all into its sweet love, so that no pain or hostility is recognized in any of them anymore, just as the day transforms the night.

47. In these first four properties, life is like the devils, but when the power of the light—as the second principle—is revealed in the properties, it is an angel and lives in Divine power and holiness, as can be seen from the fifth number downwards.

48. The sixth property, No. 6, is understanding, acting as the resonance or sound; since the properties in the light all stand in equality, they rejoice. Thus the power of the five senses becomes audible, and all properties rejoice in one another, each in the other. And so the love of the Unity leads itself into working and willing, into sensation, finding, and highness. And thus there is a <note>original: "Contrarium"</note> contrary in eternal nature, so that properties may arise in which love may be known, and so that there may be something to be loved, in which the eternal love of the Unity of God has to work, and in which the praise of God may occur. For when the properties of life are permeated with the Divine flame of love, they praise the great love of God and surrender themselves all back into the Unity of God. Such joy and knowledge could not be revealed in the Unity if the eternal will did not introduce itself into painful, mobile properties.

49. The seventh property, No. 7, is the Being <note>original: "Wesen," often translated as "Substance" or "Essence" in Böhme's work</note>, in which all the others are substantial, and in which they all work, like the soul in the body. In this, one understands Nature and also the eternal Substantial Wisdom of God, as the <term>Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum," the chaos or "mother" out of which all things are manifested</note></term>, from which ground the visible world with its essence and creatures has sprouted.

50. Thus, with this table, one should understand the hidden spiritual world as God's eternal revelation, from which the angels and the souls of men have received their original state <note>original: "Urstand"</note>; therefore they can transform themselves into evil or into good, for both lie in their center <note>original: "Centro"</note>. The spiritual world is nothing other than God's revealed Word, and has been from eternity, and remains in eternity, and within it Heaven and Hell are understood.

<summary>Jacob Böhme explains the final four properties of eternal nature, describing how the "painful" qualities of the first principle are transformed by Divine Light into a harmonious, spiritual substance. He identifies the seventh property as the "Mysterium Magnum," the foundational essence from which the visible world and all souls originate.</summary>

<meta>Continuity from page 68: The text continues the systematic explanation of the mystical diagrams, moving from the internal spiritual world to the external physical universe. ### Explanation of the Second Table.
## THE MACROCOSM <note>original: "Macrocosmus"; the "Great World" or the universe as a whole, reflecting the divine.</note>

51. In this table, one understands how the hidden spiritual world has made itself visible, and has created a reflection of itself through its out-breathing, wherein the eternal principles <note>original: "Principia"</note> have flowed out, and the powers therein have become material; for external nature is nothing else than an outflow or reflection of eternal nature.

52. The four elements <note>original: "Elementa"</note> originate from the first four properties of eternal nature: namely, the earth and the coarseness of all beings come from the dark desire, wherein the other six properties have always become material along with it, as one may understand by the good and evil found in metals and herbs. But the dark desire has coagulated <note>original: "coaguliret"; thickened or solidified</note> them all, as still happens today.

53. The air originates from the movement of the magnetic impression through the fire in the dispersed Mercury <note>original: "Mercurio"; here referring to the alchemical principle of volatility and movement</note>, acting as the dispersed motion from which the water comes.

54. The water is the dispersed Mercury wherein the fiery nature is killed: Water is the female of the fiery Mercury, in which he works; from this, heat and cold, as well as thickness and thinness, are in conflict.

55. The fire originates from the spiritual fire of the inner ground. The cold is understood to be in the magnetic sharpness, acting as the true root of the fire.

56. Above the seven properties over the table stands the phrase, "Ground of Nature," divided into the first three forms: and into the fourth and fifth form or property, the word "Pure Element" <note>original: "Rein Element"</note> is divided; and into the sixth and seventh form, the word "Paradise" <note>original: "Paradeis"</note> is divided.

57. By the phrase "Ground of Nature," one understands the root of the four elements, acting as the four causes of motion and sensitivity.

58. By the phrase "Pure Element," one understands the temperament <note>original: "Temperamentum"</note> or equality of nature and the four elements, where the light transforms all properties into a single Will; therein the light is also present in the sensitive, mov- <note>The text cuts off mid-word "beweglichen" (movable), matching the catchword for the next page.</note>

<summary>This page explains the origins of the physical universe (Macrocosm) as a material reflection of spiritual principles, detailing how the four elements and alchemical properties like Mercury emerge from divine "out-breathing." It introduces the "Pure Element" as a state of perfect balance where light harmonizes all conflicting forces.</summary>

# XIX. Tables of Principles. Table 2.

acts through the mobile elemental property; thus one understands how the eternal element—as the movement of Divine power, sharpened through the ground of nature—has revealed itself in the light. This Pure Element is the movement of the inner spiritual world and flowed out into being during the creation of the world; it is understood as the <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>The "Fifth Essence" or Quintessence; the pure, celestial substance that permeates all things beyond the four physical elements.</note>.

59. The word <term>Paradeis</term> <note>Paradise</note>, in the sixth and seventh properties, indicates the spiritual activity within the essence of light, like a greening or spiritual growth. In the beginning of the world, this spiritual essence flourished through all four elements and formed itself out of the earth into all fruits, transforming all properties of wrath into the <term>Temperamentum</term> <note>The Temperament; a state of perfect balance or harmony where no single quality dominates another.</note>. But when the wrathful properties were awakened along with the four elements through Adam’s turned-away desire and false will, and they gained dominion, this "greening" fled back. That is, it remained standing in the <term>Tinctur</term> <note>Tincture; in Behmenist thought, the hidden spiritual life-force or "dye" that gives quality and color to matter.</note> of the inner ground. It is indeed still within the four elements, but only in the inner pure element, and cannot be reached except through the new <term>Wiedergeburt</term> <note>Rebirth or regeneration</note> of the inner man. In the material tincture, this paradisiacal activity is also quite manifest. Understood by our own <note>A common phrase in mystical texts implying "those initiated into these mysteries will understand."</note>.

60. This table indicates from what all beings of this world have originated, and what the Creator is. Namely, that the Creator was the spiritual world of power, which God moved as the Divine Will. But the <term>Separator</term> <note>The Divider; the archetypal force that differentiates unity into multiplicity.</note> was the will that flowed out from the spiritual world; in such movement, it flowed out from itself and created an "opposite-throw" <note>German: Gegenwurf; an object or reflection created by the projection of the will.</note> of its activity. In this movement, one reflection continually flowed from the other, down to the very outermost <term>Materia</term> <note>Matter</note> of the earth.

61. This matter was drawn into a mass through Divine movement, and that same drawing or movement still stands as it is. Therefore, all materials in the depths fall toward the earth, and this is the reason that the power of movement remains so today and until the end of this time.

62. The seven days and seven planets indicate the seven properties of the Spiritual World. ### Explanation of the Third Plate.
# THE MICROCOSM <note>original: MICROCOSMUS</note>.

->Summaries.<-

Here man is presented as an image of all 3 worlds. §. 63. The soul is the eternal nature, 64. 65. and the spirit is God's Idea. 66. Through self-will, however, 67. and the devil's deceit, 68:70. man has fallen and become a monster, 71. in that the hellish foundation, as the spirit of error, rules in body and soul. 72. etc.

<margin>63.</margin>In this plate, man is presented as an image of all three worlds, according to soul, spirit, and body: what he was in the beginning after his creation; what he became in the Fall through the spirit of error; and what he becomes through the Spirit of Christ in the New Rebirth, which is a true, substantial image out of the three principles of Divine Revelation, as from the out-flowed Word of the Divine Will.

64. According to the soul, he is the eternal nature of the fiery kind, like a spark from the Center from which the fire originates; if this ground cannot reach the Divine Light, then it is a darkness, from the magnetic, attractive, desiring power; but if it reaches the light out of the fire, so that this magnetic desire eats from the out-flowed unity of the Love of God, then the true good spirit springs forth from the fire, just as the light shines from the candle.

65. These are two principles: namely, in the fire of eternal nature is the soul, the first principle; and in the light of Divine power is the spirit, the second principle. The body, however, is the third principle, being a substance of the visible world, made into an image from the stars and elements, out of the seven properties of nature.

<summary>This page defines the "Microcosm," explaining how the human being is composed of three principles—soul (fire), spirit (light), and body (matter)—mirroring the entire structure of the universe and divine revelation.</summary>
<keywords>Microcosm, soul, spirit, body, creation, rebirth, three principles, fire and light, seven properties of nature, the Fall</keywords>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical and mystical exploration of the human soul's structure and its relationship to the divine, following the previous discussions on the "Seven Properties of Nature."</meta>

### XIX. Tables of Principles. Table 3.

66. The soul possesses the seven properties of the inner spiritual world according to nature, but the spirit is without properties, for it stands outside of nature in the Unity of God. However, it becomes manifest within the soul through the soul’s fiery nature, for it is the true image of God, as an Idea <note>The author uses "Idea" in the Platonic sense: a perfect, eternal divine pattern.</note> in which God Himself works and dwells, provided the soul directs its desire into God and surrenders itself to the will of God. But where this does not happen, this Idea—the spirit—is mute and inactive, and stands only like a faded image in a mirror, remaining without essence, as happened to Adam in the Fall. However, if the soul surrenders itself to God and directs its magnetic hunger into God’s love, the soul draws divine essence—the substantial Wisdom of God—into itself. Thus its Idea or spirit becomes substantial in the power of the Light and receives Divine Life: then it is the true temple of God, within which God’s Unity is active and manifest.

67. But if the soul, through desire, leads itself into its own self—as into self-love—and turns its desire toward the seven properties to test them and eat of the pleasure of those properties, it elevates itself and creates for itself an *Evestrum* <note>A term likely borrowed from Paracelsus, referring to a subtle or astral "double" or "shadow" of the being.</note>, as an astral counter-projection. This *Evestrum* immediately hungers after the vanity of false pleasure, as happened to Lucifer and Adam; for Lucifer’s *Evestrum* formed itself into Phantasy, and the *Evestrum* of Adam’s soul formed itself into the animal <insert>or: fiery</insert> properties of the external world. By this the soul was poisoned, and it immediately infected the body made from the *Limo* <note>Latin: *limus*, meaning slime, mud, or clay.</note> of the earth, so that the animal properties awoke within it. It then lusted after earthly, beastly food—after heat and cold, after bitterness, sweetness, and sourness—and with such properties led itself into a fountain of such pleasure. By eating of Good and Evil with its desire, the image of God, as the Idea, became darkened and inactive. Thus the true spirit, as the active Idea, was mute and dead, just as an image in a mirror is dead. In this way the soul was separated from God and stood in its own natural willing, for God’s will no longer worked in the spirit, and the willing of the *Evestrum*—the counter-formation of the dark and external world—began to take over; for the Holy Genius <note>The "Genius" here refers to the guiding divine spirit or guardian angel-nature of the human being.</note> was transformed.

68. In this table, at the top, stands TINCTURE, in the seven prop—

<summary>This page describes the metaphysical distinction between the soul's natural properties and the divine spirit (Idea), explaining how the Fall occurred through the creation of an "Evestrum" or astral shadow. It emphasizes that the soul becomes a temple of God only when it directs its "magnetic hunger" toward divine love rather than self-love.</summary>

### The III. Table.

### MICROCOSM. that is: The Little World, or Man; who is an image of all 3 Principles: according to the Soul, the Fire-World; according to the Spirit, the Light-World; according to the Body, the Air-World.

In this table, Man is presented as he was in Paradise, when all properties stood within him in equality, without the adoption of self-desire, while he stood in the Image of God: and what he has become through the deception and infection of Satan; what the *menstruum* <note>original: "Menstruum (al. Monstrum)"; here referring to a corruptive "solvent" or "monstrous" influence</note> of the serpent within him is, by which he became earthly and mortal: and then how the Word and Love of God came again to his aid, and births him anew in Christ, daily killing the serpent-image; and in what danger and misery he stands in such a formation, as he stands upon the foundation of both Hell and Heaven. Thus it is designed to show how he is an image of the seven properties, set into one form out of all three Principles according to Eternity and Time, as an image of Divine Revelation and Science <note>meaning "knowledge" or "gnosis"</note>: For a greater understanding of how he should govern his life, and to which impulse he should yield himself.

# Plate 3. Explanation of the Plates of the 3 Principles

properties <note>original: "Eigenschaften." In Boehme’s theosophy, these are the seven foundational qualities of nature and spirit.</note> divided; this signifies the equality of the seven properties, according to soul and body. In the first human before the Fall, the properties stood in a balanced will toward distinction and their own delight, and they directed all their desires into the Unity of God. Thus they were a true Paradise, for the essential Spirit with the Unity of God was manifest in them, and they were meant to work in God’s love through all things.

69. But the Devil begrudged them this, and deceived the seven properties of life with false pleasure. He persuaded them it would be good for them, and they would become wise, if each property introduced itself into its own delight according to its own kind; then the spirit would taste and recognize what is Evil and Good. But he did not tell them that such a thing could not subsist within the Unity of God.

70. However, when they introduced themselves into their own pleasure, such strife and adversity awoke within them, and the properties all became pictorial <note>or "form-giving"</note> in their selfhood. Thus the Unity, as the Element <note>The "One Element" or pure spiritual quintessence</note>, was torn asunder, and the four elements gained the dominion in the strife. Immediately, inequality fell upon them from without—such as heat and cold—as did the stars with their divisiveness, affecting the body; and the Wrath of God <note>original: "Grimm GOttes"</note>, according to the property of the dark world, fell into the soul. From this came the soul's terror, anguish, distress, and eternal despair: and in the body awoke heat and cold, pain, sickness, and the mortal life.

71. Thus the Image of God, the whole human being, fell from his order and became a monster <note>original: "Monstrum"</note> and a mask <note>original: "Larva," implying a hollow or deceptive appearance of the true human</note>. Immediately the seven properties began their dominion in their ignited state, with envying, murdering, stinging, and breaking. Out of Love came Pride and self-pleasing love; out of Desire came Greed; out of Sensitivity came Envy; and out of the Fire-life came a vain, poisonous Wrath. Thus the foundation of Hell was made manifest in the whole human being, and ruled in soul and body.

72. This hellish foundation is now the spirit of error, in which man would have had to be eternally damned, exist, and remain, if Divine Grace had not—immediately after such a fall—[promised] the Crusher of the Serpent <note>original: "Schlangen-Treter," a reference to the "seed of the woman" in Genesis 3:15</note> as the outflowing of Divine Love, in the most holy name of JESUS, in-spo- <margin>E 5</margin>

# XIX. Tables of Principles. Table 3.

spoken, toward a new rebirth; which Holy Name, in pure mercy and with the highest humility, has surrendered itself into the human soul and body, and assumed humanity, and has broken the power of this devilish spirit of error, and killed the selfhood of the life-will, and brought the qualities back into equality, and united them with His love, and led them back into Divine Unity.

73. There the true spirit, as the human Idea and the image of God, was renewed once more and filled with the Divine Essence of Love; and through Christ’s soul and spirit, the human soul has received an open gate to God again within such love and Divine Essence.

Beneath this stand the seven days of the week with their characters <note>astrological or planetary symbols</note>, to indicate that man is exactly the same thing.

75. 76. With the word <term>Satan</term>, by which the spirit of error is indicated, a creaturely devil is not meant, but rather the torment of such a spirit of error. And with the word <term>Christ</term>, the new man in the spirit of Christ according to the inwardness is understood. The other spaces are understood as in the other tables, in which one understands the cause of transformation; for the reader to further contemplate.

# CLAVIS
### or
# KEY.
### That is:
## An Explanation of the most prominent Points and Words in these Writings.

---

## Preface of the Author
### to the Reader of these Writings.

->**Summaries.**<-

Without God’s Spirit nothing is to be found. §. 1. Therefore our undertaking must stretch toward God’s Honor and the Neighbor’s benefit, 2. and the soul must be entirely devoted to God; 3. also with a calm, surrendered will, ask God for understanding. 4. following.

IT is written: The natural man understands nothing of what is of the Spirit, nor of the mystery of the Kingdom of God; it is a foolishness to him, and he cannot grasp it. <note>original: 1. Cor. 2: 14. "The natural person does not accept the things of the Spirit of God, for they are folly to him."</note> Therefore I wish to have the Christian lover of mysteries warned, if he wishes to apply himself to these high writings, and to read, search, and understand them, that he should not begin to read from the outside with sharp speculation and reflection; he will otherwise only remain in the external figurative ground alone, and attain nothing more than a painted color from it; for one's own reason without God’s light cannot come into the ground <note>The "ground" or *Grund* refers to the fundamental essence or depth of reality.</note>, it is not at all possible for it, let the talent <note>original: "Ingenium" — refers to natural wit or intellectual ability.</note> be as high and subtle as it ever will, it yet only seizes a looking-glass of it: For Christ speaks: Without me you can do nothing. <note>original: Joh. 15: 5.</note> Likewise: He is the light of the world, and the life of men. <note>original: Joh. 8: 12.</note>

2. If one wishes to search the Divine ground, as the Divine revelation, he should beforehand bethink himself to what end he desires to know such, whether he also that which

<summary>This page serves as the introduction to the "Clavis" or Key, where the author warns that spiritual mysteries cannot be understood through intellectual speculation alone, but require divine illumination and a selfless motive.</summary>
<keywords>Clavis, Key, Divine Revelation, Holy Spirit, Reason vs. Light, Spiritual Understanding, Preface</keywords>

# XX. Key, or Explanation

he might wish to obtain, desires to practice, and intends to apply to God’s glory and his neighbor’s welfare: whether he also desires through this to die to worldliness and his own self-will, and to live in that which he seeks and desires, and to become One Spirit with Him.

3. If he does not have such a purpose—namely, that if God were to reveal His mysteries to him, he would want to be One Spirit and Will with Him, and surrender himself entirely to God so that God’s Spirit may do through and with him whatever He wills, so that God is his knowing, willing, and working—then he is not yet fit for such knowledge and understanding. For many seek only mysteries so that they may be highly and respectably esteemed before the world, only for their own benefit; but they do not reach that Ground where the Spirit searches all things, even the depths of the Godhead, as it is written: *1 Corinthians 2:10*. <note>original: "da der Geist alle Dinge, ja auch die Tieffe der Gottheit forschet"</note>

4. There must only be a completely <term>resigned and surrendered will</term> <note>German: "gelassener und übergebener Wille." This refers to 'Gelassenheit,' a central concept in German mysticism meaning a state of letting go or yielding to God's will.</note> in which God Himself searches and works; a will which constantly presses into God in resigned humility and self-surrender, and seeks nothing else but its eternal fatherland and to serve its neighbor therewith. Then it may well be attained. He must begin it with the <term>work of repentance</term> <note>German: "Buß-Wirckung"</note> and earnest prayer, so that the understanding may be opened to him from within; then the internal will also lead itself into the external. <note>The word "einführen" is repeated across the line break in the original text.</note>

5. When he reads such writings and yet cannot understand them, he should not quickly cast them away and deem them impossible to understand; he should turn his mind to God and ask Him for grace and understanding, and read again. Then he will see more therein, until he is finally drawn into the depths through God’s power and comes into the <term>supernatural, supersensual Ground</term> <note>German: "übernatürlichen, übersinnlichen Grund." In Boehme's thought, the 'Ungrund' or 'Ground' is the absolute abyss of the divine before manifestation.</note>, namely into the eternal Unity of God. There he will hear unspeakable, actual words of God, which will lead him back out through the <term>Divine Outflow</term> <note>German: "Göttlichen Ausfluß," referring to the emanation of the created world from the Divine source.</note> even unto the coarsest matter of the earth, and back in again to God. Then the Spirit of God searches all things through him and with him: and thus he is truly taught and driven by God. *1 Corinthians 2:7, 10*.

# Of the Principal Points and Words

6. Because a <term>Clavis<note>Latin: "key"</note></term> or key to my writings is desired by the seekers, I will gladly grant their wish and present a brief summary of the foundation regarding the foreign words. These are taken partly from nature—that is, from the <term>Sensu<note>Latin: "sense" or "sensory perception"</note></term>—and are partly the words of foreign masters, which I have tested according to the sense and found to be good.

7. Human reason will take offense when it sees <term>heathen terms<note>The author refers to Greco-Roman philosophical or alchemical vocabulary</note></term> and words used to explain natural things; it thinks one should only use Biblical words. However, Biblical words are not always suited for a thorough explanation of the properties of nature, because the underlying ground cannot be fully expressed through them. Furthermore, the wise <term>Heathens and Jews<note>Likely referring to Hermetic philosophers and Kabbalists</note></term> kept the deep foundation of nature hidden under such words, for they well understood that the knowledge of nature is not for everyone, but belongs only to those whom God has chosen for it through nature.

8. No one should take offense at this; for when God reveals His mysteries to a person, He also leads them into a sense of how they should express them, as God recognizes what is necessary and needed in every <term>Seculo<note>Latin: "century" or "age"</note></term> to restore confused tongues and opinions back to the foundation. No one should think that this happens by chance through human reason. The revelation of Divine things is opened through the inner ground of the spiritual world and brought into visible forms, just as the Creator wishes to reveal it.

9. I will, however, write a brief explanation of Divine revelation, as much as I can grasp in this narrow space, and explain the foreign words for a greater understanding of the other books. I will present a <term>Summam<note>Latin: "summary" or "totality"</note></term> of these writings as a brief <term>Formulam<note>Latin: "formula" or "set pattern"</note></term> for beginners to contemplate; further explanation will be found in the other books.

<summary>The author introduces a glossary or "key" to his works, justifying the use of non-Biblical, philosophical, and alchemical terminology as necessary for explaining the mysteries of nature. He asserts that such specialized language is divinely inspired to clarify spiritual truths for each specific age.</summary>

# EXPLANATION OF TERMS.

## I. How one should contemplate God outside of Nature and Creation.

->Summaries.<-

God is One, §. 1. 2. in Trinity <note>original: "Dreyfaltigkeit"</note>. 3. 4. The Will is the Father; 5. the Delight is the Son, as the receptivity of the Will; 6. 7. and the outgoing Will is the Holy Spirit. 8. Thus there are three kinds of workings in the Unity. 9. The essential Desire is the Magnet; 10. otherwise the Unity would be still. 11. What God’s Word may be, 12. 13. which opens the Senses? 14. The Holy Name **Jehovah** is the essential sensitivity. 15. Each letter indicates a power. 16. The Divine Wisdom 17. 18. is the Divine Understanding; 19. **Great Mystery** <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum," a term used by Boehme to describe the chaotic state from which all things are manifested</note> 20. has two essences and wills within itself, 21. and is the Chaos, 22. from which Light and Darkness originate. 23. Concerning the Center of eternal Nature. 24. Eternal Nature 25. forms the Word into properties, 26. and is the Master-Craftsman; 27. it stands in 7 properties, 28. which are: Desire, 29. the Drawing-in of Desire, 30. and the Ground of Anguish: in which first three the foundation of Hell stands. 31. Furthermore, Fire, wherein the Unity appears in Love, 32. Light, 33. Sound, 34. and finally the Corporeality of the six properties. 35. In these 7 properties, two essences are to be understood: the Divine of the Unity, 36. and then Nature’s own essence. 37. The 1st property is Desirousness; 38. the 2nd is the Drawing, a perpetual strife within itself. 39. Thus the Delight comes from the Fire; 40. and this Desire was the beginning of this world, 41. whereby the 2nd property was the Separator. 42. The Unity is also called Freedom. 49. How the Fire is extinguished and the Darkness swallowed up? 50. 51. from which 2 beginnings arise, or 2 Principles, 52. 53. as Fire and Light, so in Love, the Reconciliation. 54. The 5th property is the Love-Fire, 58. as the true Soul of man and God’s Temple: 59. 60. is the 2nd Principle, as the Angelic World. 61. Parable of a candle. 62. The 7th property is the Housing <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword "häuse" indicates the continuation of "Gehäuse," meaning the container, shell, or body of the other properties.</note>

house of the other six properties. 73. The power of fire and light is the tincture <note>In alchemy and Boehme’s thought, the "tincture" is the spiritual essence or life-force that transforms and ennobles matter</note>. 74. There are only three properties of nature: 75. Father, 76. Son, 77. and Holy Spirit. 78. The Divine Revelation is twofold, 79. and all properties are equally eternal. 80.

Moses says: "The Lord our God is one God" <note>original: "Der HErr unser GOtt ist ein einiger GOtt"</note>; Deuteronomy 6:4. And in another place it stands: "From Him, through Him, and in Him are all things"; Romans 11:36. Likewise: "Am I not HE, who fills all things?" Jeremiah 23:24. Likewise: "Through His word all things are made, that are made." John 1:3.

2. Therefore one must say that He is the origin of all things: He is the eternal and immeasurable (unchangeable) Unity. For example: if I think, what would remain in the place of this world if the four elements with the stars together with nature went away and ceased, so that no nature or creature existed anymore? Answer: There would remain that same eternal Unity, from which nature and the creature received their origin. So also if I think: What is many hundred thousand miles above the stars, or what is in the place where there is no creature? Answer: It is the eternal, unchangeable Unity, which is the single Good, which has nothing behind it or before it that could give or take anything from it, or from which this Unity could originate; there is no ground, goal, (time) nor place there, and it is the one God, or the eternal Good, which cannot be expressed in words.

## II. Further consideration of how this one God is Threefold.

3. The Holy Scripture shows us that this one God is Threefold, namely a single Triple Being, which has three kinds of activity and yet is only a single Being, as one can see this in the outflowing power in all things, for whoever perceives it.

4. Specifically, there is an image in fire, light, and air, which are three kinds of activities and yet are only a single ground and being; and as one sees that fire, light, and air arise from the candle, though the candle is none of those things and yet is a cause for such; so also is the eternal Unity.

# XX. Key, or Explanation

the cause and the ground of the eternal Trinity, which reveals itself out of the eternal Unity itself, and leads into a surging or a willing, a delight, and an outgoing.

5. (1) The surging or the willing is the Father, as the revelation or movement of the Unity, by which the Unity wills itself.

6. (2) The delight is the Son, as the "One" thing that the will desires, as his love and well-pleasing, as is to be seen at the baptism of Christ, where the Father testified: Matthew 3:17. *This is my beloved Son, in whom I am well pleased; hear ye him.* <note>original: "Dieser ist mein lieber Sohn, an dem ich Wolgefallen habe, den solt ihr hören."</note>

7. The delight is the "containability" <note>The capacity for the formless will to take on a defined shape or vessel.</note> of the will, wherein the will introduces itself within the Unity into an activity and a place of its own, through which the will works and desires, acting as a sensitivity and power of the will.

8. The will is the Father, as the act of willing; and the delight is the Son, as the power and activity within the willing, by which the will works; and the Holy Spirit is the outgoing will through the delight of the power, acting as a life of the will, of the power, and of the delight.

9. Thus there are three kinds of activities in the eternal Unity: the Unity is the willing of itself, and the delight is an actual being <note>term: "Wesen" - often translated as essence or substance, referring to the reality of a thing.</note> of the willing, and an eternal joy of sensitivity within the willing, and the Holy Spirit is the outgoing of the power, just as one sees a likeness in a herb.

10. The magnet or the essential desire of nature (acting as the will of nature's desire) gathers itself into an *Ens* <note>term: "Ens" - a Latin philosophical term for a "being" or "entity" in its most basic form.</note> or essence to become a herb, and in the "containability" of the desire, the desire becomes sensitive and active; and in that same activity the power arises, into which the magnetic desire of nature—as the flowed-out will of God—works in a natural way. In such actual sensitivity, the magnetic, desiring will becomes significant and joyful, and proceeds from the working power; from this arises the growth and the scent of the herb; and thus we see the image of the Trinity of God in all growing and living things.

11. If such a desiring sensitivity and outgoing activity of the Trinity were not present in the eternal Unity,

### III. Concerning the Eternal Word of GOD.

<margin>12.</margin> The Holy Scripture says: God made all things through His Word. Likewise, that the Word was God, John 1: 1-3. This is understood as follows:

13. The Word is nothing other than the out-breathing will from the power, a differentiation of the power into a multiplicity of powers, a division and outflow of the Unity, from which <term>Science</term> <note>original: "Wissenschaft"; here meaning conscious knowledge or systematic understanding</note> originates. For in a single Being, wherein there is no differentiation and which is only One, there is no knowledge; for even if it existed, it would know only one thing: itself. But when it divides and separates from itself, the separated will enters into multiplicity, and every separation works within itself.

14. However, because the Unity cannot be torn apart or depart from itself, the division remains within the out-breathing will inside the Unity. This division of the "breathing" provides only distinctions, through which the eternal will—along with its delight and its outgoing—enters into the knowledge of infinite forms or understanding. This is an eternal, perceptible, active, and <term>sensual</term> <note>original: "sensualische"; referring to the divine senses or the capacity to perceive and feel within the spiritual realm</note> knowledge of the powers. In this division of the will, one <term>sense</term> <note>original: "Senfus"; Latin for sense or perception</note> or form of the will beholds, feels, tastes, smells, and hears the other. And yet, it is only a single, sensory effect: the great bond of joy in Love, and the benevolent, single (eternal) Being.

### IV. Concerning the Holy Name JEHOVAH.

<margin>15.</margin> The ancient Rabbis among the Jews understood this to some extent, for they said this Name was the highest and holiest Name of GOD, by which one understands the active Godhead in the <term>sense</term> <note>original: "Sensu"; the perceptive or felt aspect of the divine</note>. And it is true; for in this active perception lies the true life of all things, in time and eternity, in the <term>Ground and the Unground</term> <note>original: "Grund und Ungrund"; a central concept in Böhme's theosophy referring to the manifest foundation of being and the "Abyss" or "Bottomless" nothingness from which God emerges</note>. It is God Himself, as the Divine, active perceptibility, findability, knowledge, and love, as the—

true understanding in the actual Unity, from which the five senses of true life originate.

16. Each letter in this name indicates a power and a particular effect, as a form within the active power. For **I** is the outflow of the eternal, inseparable Unity, as the sweet holiness <note>original: "Heiligkeit"; variant: "Huldigkeit" or graciousness</note>, the ground of Divine Selfhood. **E** is a threefold **I**, where the Trinity closes itself into a Unity, for the **I** goes into the **E** and is called <note>variant: "closes as"</note> **IE**, as a breathing out of the Unity into itself. **H** is the Word or breathing of the Trinity of God. **O** is the circumference, as the Son of God, through which the **IE** speaks out with the **H** or breathing; understand this as coming from the conceived delight of the power. **V** is the joy-filled outflow from the breathing, as the outgoing Spirit of God. **A** is that which has gone out from the power, as Wisdom, a subject <note>term: Subjectum — here meaning a receptive vessel or underlying substance</note> of the Trinity, in which the Trinity works, and in which the Trinity is manifest. This name is nothing other than an utterance of the threefold activity in the Holy Trinity within the Unity of God: read further concerning this in the explanation of the tables of the three principles of Divine Revelation. ->No. XIX. page 61.<-

### V. Of Divine Wisdom.

17. The Holy Scripture says: Wisdom is the breathing of Divine power, a ray and breath of the Almighty. <note>Wisdom of Solomon 7:25</note>. Likewise, God has made all things through His Wisdom. This is understood as follows:

18. 19. She is the great mystery <note>term: Mysterium — the hidden spiritual core of reality</note> of the Divine nature, for in her the powers, colors, and virtues become manifest: In her is the distinctness of power, as the understanding: she is herself the Divine understanding, as the Divine contemplation <note>original: "Beschaulichkeit"</note>, in which the Unity is manifest: She is the true Divine

### VI. Concerning the Great Mystery.
<note>original: "Mysterio Magno." A central term in Boehme's theosophy referring to the "Great Mystery" or the hidden potentiality of all things within God.</note>

20. The Great Mystery is a subject of Wisdom, where the breathing Word or the active, surging power of Divine Understanding flows out through Wisdom. Within this, the Unity of God also flows out for His revelation: for in the Great Mystery, eternal Nature originates; and within the Great Mystery, two beings and wills are always understood.

21. The first being is the Unity of God, as Divine Power, or the outflowing Wisdom. The second being is the separable will, which arises through the breathing, out-speaking Word. This will does not have its foundation in the Unity, but in the mobility of the outflow or out-breathing, which leads itself into its own willing and into a desire for Nature—that is, into the properties up to the fire and light. In the fire, the natural life is understood, and in the light, the holy life, as a revelation of the Unity, whereby the Unity is a Love-Fire or Light. And at this place or operation, God calls Himself a dear, merciful God, according to the sharpened, fire-burning love of the Unity; and an angry, jealous God, according to the fiery foundation of eternal Nature.

22. The Great Mystery is the Chaos from which Light and Darkness, as the foundation of Heaven and Hell, have flowed and been revealed from eternity. For the foundation that we now call Hell, as its own principle, is the ground and cause of the fire of eternal Nature, which fire in God is only a burning of Love;

# XX. Key, or Explanation

and where God, according to the Unity, is not manifest in a thing, there is a painful burning of fire. This burning of fire is a revelation of Life and of Divine Love, through which Divine Love, as the Unity, becomes over-inflamed and sharpened into a fiery effect of the power of God.

23. This ground is therefore called the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note>, or a Chaos, because out of it Good and Evil originate, such as Light and Darkness, Life and Death, Joy and Sorrow, Salvation and Damnation; for it is the ground of souls and angels, and of all eternal creatures, both the evil and the good. It is a ground of the Heavens and the Hells, and of the visible world, together with everything that exists there, since everything lies within a single ground—just as the image exists within the tree before the artist carves and forms it. Yet one cannot say of the spiritual world that it had a beginning; rather, it has been manifested from eternity out of the Chaos, for the Light has shone in the Darkness from eternity, and the Darkness has not comprehended it <note>A reference to John 1:5</note>, just as day and night are within one another and yet are two in one. I must only write in such a divided way—as if it had taken a beginning—in order to contemplate the Divine ground of Divine revelation, and how one should distinguish between Nature and Deity for a better understanding of where Good and Evil have come from, and what the Essence of all Essences <note>original: "Wesen aller Wesen," a term for the ultimate reality of God</note> might be.

### VII. Concerning the Center of Eternal Nature.

24. By the word *Center* <note>original: "Centrum"</note> one understands the first beginning of Nature, as the innermost ground where the self-originated will introduces itself into an acceptance of "I-ness" <note>original: "Ichheit," referring to the emergence of individual self-consciousness or ego</note> as a natural operation. For Nature is only an instrument of God, with which God's power works, and yet it also has its own mobility from the flowed-out will of God. Thus, the Center is the point or ground of one's own acceptance of selfhood, from which something comes to be, and from which the seven properties <note>The "seven properties of nature" are a core concept in Böhme's theosophy, describing the process of manifestation from dark desire to spiritual light</note> originate.

<summary>Böhme explains the "Mysterium Magnum" as the eternal, chaotic ground from which all dualities and creatures emerge, and defines the "Center" as the beginning of self-aware nature.</summary>

### VIII. Concerning Eternal Nature and its Seven Properties.

25. Nature is nothing other than the properties of the receptivity of one's own arisen desire; this desire arises within the differentiation of the breathing Word <note>original: "hauchenden Worts," referring to the creative breath of God</note> as the breathing power, where the properties introduce themselves into a being. Then that same being is called a natural being, and is not God Himself; for God indeed dwells through nature, but nature only grasps Him as far as the Unity of God gives itself into the natural being and also makes it substantial—as in the Light-Being, which works within nature in itself, and permeates and penetrates nature. Otherwise, the Unity of God is incomprehensible to nature, as nature is merely the receptive desire.

26. Nature arises in the flowed-out Word of Divine sensitivity and knowledge, and is a constant formation and shaping of that knowledge and sensation. Whatever the Word works through Wisdom, nature builds and forms into properties.

27. She is like the carpenter who builds the house which the mind <note>original: "Gemüthe," the inner disposition or soul-mind</note> has previously modeled within itself. It is to be understood the same way here: whatever the eternal mind models in the Wisdom of God within the Divine power and leads into an Idea <note>original: "Ideam," the spiritual archetype</note>, nature forms that into a property.

28. Nature stands in its first foundation in seven properties, and these seven divide themselves into infinity.

29. The first property of nature is Desire, which creates Harshness, Sharpness, Hardness, Coldness, and Substance.

30. The second property is Motion or the drawing-in of the desire; this creates stinging, breaking, and the division of the hardness. It cuts through the attracted desire and brings it into multiplicity; it is the foundation of bitter woe, and also the true root of life, and is the Vulcan <note>original: "Vulcanus," the Roman god of fire, used here as a technical term for the transformative heat or "fire-striking"</note> for the striking of fire.

31. The third property is Sensitivity in the breaking of the harsh hardness, and is the foundation of Anguish <note>original: "Angst," a technical term in this philosophy for the tension or constriction before spiritual breakthrough</note> and of the natural willing, wherein the eternal will

It explains that while God permeates nature, He remains distinct from it, acting as the architect whose ideas are given form by nature's "carpentry."</summary>

# XX. Key, or Explanation

will become manifest; that is, He desires to be a fire and light, like a lightning bolt or a radiance, wherein the powers, colors, and virtues of Wisdom appear. In these first three properties lies the foundation of wrath and of the hells, and of everything that is fierce.

32. The fourth property is the fire, wherein the Unity appears in the light as a burning of love, and the fierceness is the very essence of the fire.

33. The fifth property is the light with its power of love, wherein the Unity works together within a natural being.

34. The sixth property is the sound or resonance, or the natural understanding, wherein the five <term>senses</term> <note>original: "Sensus"; referring to the sensory faculties of the soul</note> work spiritually, as in an intelligent life of nature.

35. The seventh property is the <term>subject</term> <note>original: "Subjectum"; the underlying substance or container</note> or the enclosure of the other six properties, wherein they work like life in the flesh; and the seventh is rightly called the ground or the place of nature, wherein the properties stand together in one foundation.

36. In these seven properties, one must always understand two essences: First, according to the abyss of such properties, one understands the Divine Being as the Divine Will, with the outflowing Unity of God, which flows out through nature and introduces itself into the sharpness <note>The "sharpness" refers to the friction or tension required for manifestation</note> for the sake of receptivity, so that the eternal love may be perceptible and active, and that it may have something that is passive <note>original: "leidende"; meaning capable of feeling or suffering</note> wherein it may manifest itself, and wherein it may be known, by which it may be loved and desired in return—namely, the pained and passive nature, which is transformed in love into an eternal kingdom of joy. For when love reveals itself in the fire within the light, it over-flames nature and penetrates it just as the sun penetrates a plant, or as fire penetrates iron.

37. The second essence is nature’s own essence, which is pained and passive, and is the instrument of the working; for where there is no passivity, there is also no desire for redemption or for something better. And where there is no desire for something better, there a thing rests within itself; and for this reason, the eternal Unity leads itself through its outflow and differentiation into nature,

so that it may have an object in which to manifest itself, so that it may love something, and in turn be loved by that "something," so that there may be a perceptible working and willing.

### IX. Explanation of the Seven Properties of Eternal Nature. <margin>*</margin> <note>The asterisk refers to the marginal note regarding a missing table in earlier editions.</note>

38. The first property is Desire, like a magnet, acting as the "compressibility" or "containment" of the will. Since the will desires to be something, yet has nothing from which to make itself something, it leads itself into an acceptance of itself; it impresses and grasps itself to become a "Something." And yet, this "Something" is nothing but a sharp, magnetic hunger, a harshness like a hardness, from which hardness, coldness, and substance arise. This impressing or attracting shadows itself and turns into darkness, which is the foundation of both eternal and temporal darkness. Through this sharpness, at the beginning of this world, salt, stones, bones, and everything similar to them originated.

39. The second property of eternal nature arises from the first, and is the "Pulling" or "Motion" within the sharpness. For while the magnet creates hardness, the motion breaks that hardness apart again; it is a perpetual strife within itself. Whatever the Desire grasps and makes into a "Something," the Motion cuts asunder so that it may come into form and structure. Between these two properties arises the bitter woe, acting as a sting of perceptibility. For when there is motion within sharpness, the property is painful; this is the cause of all sensitivity and the feeling of pain. If there were no sharpness and motion, there would be no perceptibility.

40. This motion is also the foundation of the Air in the visible world, which becomes manifest through Fire, as shall be mentioned hereafter.

M." referring to Böhme's work *Mysterium Magnum*</note> chapter 6, paragraph 21. In that form, it appears twice inappropriately; therefore, it is rightly omitted in this place, where otherwise no table is to be seen in any edition or manuscript.

# XX. Key, or Explanation

41. Thus we understand that Desire is the ground of Selfhood <note>original: "Ichheit," a term Böhme uses to describe the emergence of individual identity or "I-ness" from the divine unity</note>, so that Something might come from Nothing. We must consider that this was the beginning of this world, through which God brought all things into being; for it is the very same through which God spoke: "Let there be!" Genesis 1:3. It is the "Let there be" <note>original: "Werde"</note> that created where there was nothing but a Spirit: It has made the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum," the chaotic or unformed potential of all things in God</note>, which is spiritual, visible and substantial, as can be seen in the elements, stars, and creatures.

42. And the second property, namely Motion, was in the beginning of this world the *Separator* <note>original: "Separator," the dividing or differentiating force</note> or divider in the powers, with which the Creator, as the Will of God, brought all things out of the *Great Mystery* into a form; for it is the flowed-out, movable Word, through which the supernatural God made all things and brought them into formation.

43. The third property of eternal nature is Anguish <note>original: "Angst," here meaning a state of contraction or tension rather than just fear</note>, as the Willing that has introduced itself into the nature and Selfhood through un-pleasantness. When the self-will stands in sharp motion, it enters into anguish, which is Sensitivity; for outside of nature, the will cannot be sensitive, but in the sharp motion it becomes sensitive. And this sensitivity is the cause of Fire, as well as of the Mind and the Senses; for the natural self-will becomes feeling through this and seeks rest. Thus the distinctiveness of the will goes out from itself and penetrates the properties; from this arises the Taste in the sharpness, so that one property tastes and feels the other. This is also the ground and cause of the Senses—that one property penetrates the other and ignites the other, so that the will recognizes from whence the suffering <note>original: "Leidenheit," the state of being acted upon or experiencing passion</note> comes. For if there were no sensitivity, the will would know nothing of properties, for it would be only one; and thus the will takes nature upon itself, in that it feels the sharp motion within itself.

44. This motion is in itself like a turning wheel; yet not that there is such a physical turning, but rather it is thus in the properties; for the Desire pulls into itself, and the Motion presses out of itself. Thus the will, in such anguish, can go neither into itself nor out of itself, and yet is pulled out of itself and into itself; thus its form remains, which in itself

# Of the Principal Points and Words

wills itself and out of itself, that is above itself and below itself, and yet can go nowhere; rather, it is an **Anguish** <note>original: "Angst"; in Boehme's thought, this refers to a state of constriction or "narrowness" where forces are locked in conflict before breaking through into light.</note> and the true foundation of the Hells and God’s Wrath, for this anguish stands in the dark, sharp movement.

45. From this ground, in the creation of the world, the **Sulfuric Spirit** arose together with the matter of the sulfuric kind; this Sulfuric Spirit is the natural life of the earthly and elemental creatures.

46. The wise Pagans <note>Referring here to the ancient Alchemists and Hermetic philosophers.</note> understood this ground to some extent, for they said: in **Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury** all things in this world consist. By this, they did not only look upon the *Materia* <note>Matter</note>, but upon the Spirit from which the matter arises. For their ground does not consist in the coarse Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—they do not mean those—but rather the Spirit of such properties. In these consists everything that lives, grows, and exists in this world, whether it be spiritual or material.

For with **Salt** <note>Latin: "Sale"</note> they signify the sharp, magnetic desire of nature; and with **Mercury** <note>Latin: "Mercurio"</note> they signify the movement and separation of nature, through which every thing is distinguished and formed; and with **Sulfur** <note>Latin: "Sulphure"</note> they have signified the sensitive, bubbling, growing life. For in the Sulfuric Spirit lies the **Oil**, in which the Fire-life burns; and in the Oil lies the **Fifth Essence** <note>Latin: "Quinta Essentia"; the purest, most concentrated medicinal or spiritual force within matter.</note>, as the sulfuric Mercury, the true life of nature, which is an outflow from the Word of Divine Power and movement, wherein the ground of Heaven is understood. And in the Fifth Essence lies the **Tincture** <note>The highest spiritualizing principle that can transform or "color" lower substances into higher ones.</note>, as the paradisiacal ground, the flowed-out Word of Divine Power, wherein the properties lie in equality.

47. Thus, with the third property of nature—namely, with **Anguish**—we understand the sharpness and painfulness of the fire, such as burning and consuming. For when the Will is placed in such sharpness, it always desires to consume the cause of such sharpness, because it always presses back toward the **Unity of God**, as toward rest. And the Unity, with its outflow, presses toward this movement and sharpness; and thus it is an everlasting conjunction for the revelation of the Divine Will. Just as one finds in these three—in Salt, Sulfur, and Oil—always a Heavenly thing within the Earthly, for whoever understands this rightly and meditates <note>original: "nachsinnet"</note> according to the spirit:

Thus, a heavenly thing always lies hidden within the earthly, for the invisible spiritual world flowed out along with the creation.

48. The fourth property or form of eternal nature is the spiritual fire, in which the light—as the Unity—becomes manifest; for the radiance of the fire originates from the out-flowed Unity, which has entered into the natural desire; and the fire's torment and burning, as heat, originates from the sharp consumability of the first three properties. This happens as follows:

49. The eternal Unity, which I otherwise call the "eternal Freedom" in several of my writings, is gentle, still, and lovely, like a gentle well-being that cannot be expressed—what a gentleness exists in the Unity of God outside of nature. And the three properties belonging to nature are sharp, painful, and terrifying; in these three painful properties stands the out-flowed will, which arose through the Word or Divine Breathing, and the Unity also stands therein. Thus the will longs for the gentle Unity, and the Unity longs for the sensitivity, as if for the fiery ground; so one enters into the other; and when that happens (understand: the longing), it is like a shock or a flash of lightning <note>original: "Schrack oder Bliz"</note>, just as if one rubbed steel and stone together, or poured water into fire, speaking metaphorically.

50. In this flash, the Unity perceives the sensitivity, and the will receives the gentle Unity; thus the Unity becomes a luster of the fire, and the fire becomes a love-burning, for it receives *Ens* <note>original: "Ens (Essenz)"; the essential spiritual substance</note> or power from the gentle Unity.

51. In such an ignition, the darkness of the magnetic impression or "containability" is permeated with the light, so that it is no longer recognized, even though it remains eternally within itself in the impression.

52. And here two eternal beginnings arise: namely, the dark, harsh sharpness and pain dwelling within itself, and the sensitive power of the Unity in the light, of which the Scripture says: <note>The text breaks off here, likely leading into a biblical quotation on the following page.</note>

# Of the Most Principal Points and Words

God, as the eternal Unity, dwells in a light that no one can approach <note>original: "Licht darzu niemand kommen kann," a reference to 1 Timothy 6:16</note>, for thus the eternal Unity of God reveals itself through the spiritual fire in the light; and that same light is called Majesty, and God, as the supernatural Unity, is the power within it. For from the Unity, this Spirit-fire receives its <term>Ens<note>Ens: A philosophical term for "being" or "essence"—the fundamental substance that gives something its reality.</note></term> so that it shines; otherwise, the fire-ground would be only a painful, terrible hunger and a prickly desire. Indeed, it is exactly so where the will breaks away from the Unity and wishes to live in its own desire, as the devils have done, and as false souls also do.

53. Thus, understand here two <term>Principles<note>Principia: The fundamental sources or origins of reality in Boehme's cosmology.</note></term>: the first in the ground of the fire-burning, as in the sharp, mobile, sensitive, painful darkness within itself; and the second in the light of the fire, wherein the eternal Unity enters into movement and joy.

54. For the fire is a <term>counter-throw<note>Gegenwurf: An object or reflection that stands opposite to something else; a manifestation that allows the original to be perceived.</note></term> of the great love of the Unity of God. For in this way, the eternal delight becomes perceptible, and this perceptibility of the Unity is called Love—as a burning or life within the Unity of God. And according to such love-burning, God calls Himself a merciful, dear God; for the Unity of God loves or penetrates the painful will of the fire (which originally arose in the breathing of the Word or the outgoing of Divine delight) and transforms it into the greatest joy. And in this fiery will of eternal nature stands the soul of man and also the angels; this is their ground and center.

55. Therefore, if a soul breaks away from God’s light and love and enters into its own natural desire, the ground of such darkness and painful torment becomes manifest within it. This is the hellish fire and God’s wrath when it becomes manifest, as can be seen in Lucifer. And whatever can be thought of as existing within the creature, that same thing is also everywhere outside the creature; for the creature is nothing other than an image or figure of the distinct power of the whole Being.

56. Thus, now rightly understand what the fire-ground is: namely, cold from the <term>impression<note>Impression: The drawing-in or contraction of spiritual forces, creating density and darkness.</note></term>, and heat from the anguish, and the movement is the <term>Vulcan<note>Vulcanus: Named after the Roman god of fire and smithing; here representing the active, transformative power of fire.</note></term>. In these three stands the fire, but the radiance of the light originates from the—

# XX. Key, or Explanation

The joining together of the Unity in the fire-ground <note>original: "Feuer-Grunde"; refers to the foundational source of spiritual energy</note>; and yet the entire ground is only the outflowing will.

57. Therefore, the life of all things stands in the fire and light, as if in that same will, whether it be in the mute things, such as growing plants, or in rational beings; everything according to the ground the fire has, either from the Eternal, such as the soul, or from the Temporal, such as the astral and elemental things: For the Eternal fire is one thing, and the Temporal is another, as shall be indicated hereafter.

58. The fifth property is now the Love-fire, or the power and world of the Light, which dwells within itself in the darkness, and the darkness comprehends it not, as it stands in John 1:5: *The light shines in the darkness, and the darkness has not comprehended it.* Likewise: *The Word is in the light, and in the Word is the true, understanding life of man, as the true spirit.*

59. But this fire is the true soul of man (as the true spirit), which God breathed into man for a creaturely life: Thus, understand the true, desiring soul from the eternal ground to be in the spiritual will-fire; and in the power of the light, understand the true, understanding spirit, in which the Unity of God dwells and is manifest, as the Lord Christ says: *The kingdom of God is within you*, Luke 17:21. Likewise Paul: *You are temples of the Holy Spirit, who dwells in you.* 1 Cor. 6:19.

60. This is the place of Divine indwelling and revelation; and thus the soul may also be damned if the fiery will breaks itself off from God’s love and Unity, and enters into its natural self-possession <note>original: "Eigenthum"; refers to the state of being centered on the self rather than God</note>, as into its evil properties; which is to be further meditated upon. O Zion, note this ground, and you are free from Babel <note>Babel here represents the confusion of worldly systems and false religious structures</note>.

61. With this fifth property, the second Principle <note>original: "Principium"; the second of Böhme's three spiritual realms</note>, namely the angelic world or the Thrones, is understood; for it is the movement of the Unity, where all properties of the fiery nature burn in love.

62. A likeness of this ground is seen in a lighted candle: For in the candle everything lies within one another, and yet no property is manifest before the other until it is lit; then one sees fire, oil, light, air, and water from the air; all four elements become there- <insert>in</insert>

63. In the same way, one must also contemplate the eternal ground, for temporal being has flowed out of the Eternal; therefore, one has the same properties as the other, except that one is eternal and the other is transitory; one is spiritual, and the other is bodily.

64. When the spiritual fire and light are ignited, as they have burned from all eternity, then the great mystery of Divine power and science <note>original: "Wissenschaft"; here meaning systematic knowledge or the divine "knowing" inherent in creation</note> is always and eternally revealed therein. For in the fire, all properties of the eternal nature become spiritual, and yet nature also remains within itself as it is. But the outgoing of the will becomes spiritual; for in the fire-crack <note>original: "Feuer-Schrack"; a sudden flash or terrifying burst of energy that marks the transition from darkness to light</note> or flash, the dark receptivity is consumed. From that consumption, the pure, clear fire-spirit goes forth, permeated with the light-spirit. In such an outgoing, three kinds of properties are understood: (1) the fiery will goes upward; and (2) forward, as in the middle, like a center of the fiery will-spirit, goes the oily spirit <note>The "oily spirit" refers to the "Oleum," a term used by Böhme to describe the gentle, nourishing essence of divine love and light</note>, as the essence <term>Ens: the fundamental being or "thing-ness" of a substance</term> of the Unity of God, which has entered into a being through the desire of nature; and (3) downward, as in a sinking, goes the water-spirit, as the meekness. And though it is all merely spirit and power, it stands thus in the figure of the revelation; not that a separation occurs, but it is only thus in the revelation.

65. This threefold revelation happens according to the Trinity, for the center in which it stands is the one God according to His revelation: Upward goes the fire-flaming love-spirit, and downward goes the meekness out of love, and in the middle stands the center, which is the circumference, as the Father or the whole God according to His revelation. And as it is to be recognized in Divine revelation, so it is also in eternal nature, according to nature's own characteristic; for nature is only a reflection <note>original: "Gegenwurf"; an opposition or "counter-throw," meaning nature acts as a mirror or objectified expression of the hidden God</note> of the Deity.

66. Thus we are further to recognize nature. For the flash of the fire's origin is a "crack," a salt-nitrous ground <note>original: "Salnitrischer Grund"; a term from alchemy referring to a volatile, explosive potentiality that triggers life and movement</note>, where nature goes out into infinite multiplicity, as into the multitude of powers, from which also the many kinds of angels and spirits, as well as colors and workings, have arisen, as well as—
