# XX. Key, or Explanation

also the four elements <note>original: "Elementa"</note> in the beginning of time; for the temperament of fire and light is the holy element, acting as the movement within the light of Unity. But through this Salnitric ground <note>The "Salnitric ground" refers to a spiritual saltpeter or explosive potentiality; the author notes this should be understood spiritually and not as the earthly chemical.</note> the four elements arise: namely, in the impression of the fiery Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius"; here used as a technical term for the active, creative spirit or "working word" of nature.</note>, the earth and stones; and in the fifth essence <term>Quintessence: the purest, most concentrated form of a substance</term> of the fiery Mercury, the fire and the heaven; and in the movement or outgoing, the air; and in the bursting forth of desire through the fire, the water.

67. The fiery Mercury is a dry water which has given birth to metals and stones; but that which was dispersed through the mortification <note>The alchemical process of "killing" or transforming a substance's outward form.</note> in the fire has given birth to water, and the impression has brought the coarse roughness into the earth, which is a coarse Salnitric, Saturnine Mercury <note>Saturnine refers to the property of Saturn: cold, dark, and contracting.</note>. By the word Mercury, you should here always understand in spirit the flowed-out, natural, working Word of God, which has been the Separator, divider, and former of all beings; and by the word Saturn, understand the impression <note>The "impression" is the force of contraction or solidification.</note>.

68. In the fifth property, which is the light, the eternal Unity is substantial, as a holy, spiritual fire, a holy light, a holy air—which is purely spirit. Likewise, a holy water, which is the flowing-out love of the Unity of God; likewise, a holy earth, which is purely power and operation. This fifth property is the true spiritual, angelic world of Divine Joys, which stands hidden within this visible world.

69. The sixth property of eternal nature is the sound, resonance, or understanding; for in the flash of fire, the properties all become audible. The fire is the mouth of the essence, and the light is the spirit, and the sound is the understanding, wherein the properties all understand one another.

70. According to the revelation of the Holy Trinity <note>original: "H. Dreyfaltigkeit"</note>, with the outflowing of the Unity, this sound or resonance is the Divine working Word, acting as the understanding in eternal nature, through which supernatural knowledge <note>original: "Wissenschaft"</note> reveals itself; and according to nature and the creature, it is the knowledge of God <note>original: "Erkenntniß GOttes"</note>, wherein the natural understanding recognizes God. For the natural understanding is a reflection and outflow from Divine understanding.

71. Within the natural understanding lie the five senses of a spiritual kind: And in the second property—that is, in motion, or the fiery Mercury <note>original: "Mercurio"; here referring to the alchemical principle of volatility and communication</note>—they exist in a natural way. The sixth property provides understanding through resonance, as in the speech of the Word; the other property <note>referring back to the second</note> acts as the guide, serving as the casing or instrument of speech and sound.

72. In the second property, the power is painful; in the sixth property, it is full of joy. Yet, there is no difference between the second and sixth properties other than light and darkness. They exist within one another like fire and light, and are distinguished only by that same degree.

73. The seventh property is the Essence <note>original: "Wesen"</note>, acting as a Subject <note>original: "Subjectum"; a foundational underlying substance</note> or casing for the other six, wherein they are all substantial, just as the soul is with the body. Primarily, according to the Light-World, this is to be understood as Paradise or a greening, active power; for every property creates for itself a Subject or counterpart through its own outflowing. In the seventh, all properties exist in Temperament <note>original: "Temperamento"; a state of perfect balance or harmony</note>, as in a single being. Just as they all spring from Unity, they all return again into One Ground; and though they work in diverse ways and qualities, here there is only one single being, whose power is called the Tincture <note>term: Tinctur; in Behmenist thought, the spiritual "dye" or essence that transforms and ennobles matter</note>, acting as a holy, penetrating essence.

74. Not that the seventh property is the Tincture itself, but rather it is the Body <note>original: "Corpus"</note> for it: The power of the fire and light is the Tincture within the substantial body, but the seventh property is the essence which the Tincture penetrates and sanctifies. Therefore, Paradise exists as a spiritual greening within the seventh property. Understand that according to the power of Divine Revelation it is so; but according to the quality of Nature, it is an essence of the attracted desire of all properties.

75. It is especially to be noted that the first and seventh properties are always counted as one, as are the second and sixth, and likewise the third and fifth. The fourth stands alone as the dividing mark <note>or "turning point"</note>; for there are only three properties of Nature, according to the revelation of the Holy Trinity of God.

76. As the first is Desire, which is attributed to God the Father—

77. The Second is dedicated to God the Son as the Divine Power; in the second number it is only a spirit, but in the sixth it is the intelligible power.

78. The Third is dedicated to God the Holy Spirit according to his revelation; in the beginning of the third property it is only a fire-spirit, but in the fifth property the great love is revealed therein.

79. Thus the outflow of Divine Revelation according to the three properties is natural in the first principle <note>original: "Principio"</note> before the light, and spiritual in the second principle within the light.

80. These then are the Seven Properties in a Single Ground, all seven of which are equally eternal without beginning, and none can be counted as the first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, or last; for they are equally eternal, without beginning, and thus have an eternal beginning out of the unity of God. One must only set such things down in a figurative way for the sake of understanding, showing how one is born from the other, for a greater understanding of what the Creator is, and so that one may contemplate the life and essence of the visible world.

81. This visible world sprouted forth from the aforementioned spiritual world, as from the out-flowing Divine power, and is an <term>Object</term> <note>original: "Objectum" or "Gegenwurf"; a mirror or counterpart</note> of the spiritual world: The spiritual world is the innermost ground of the visible world: the visible world stands within the spiritual.

82. This visible world is nothing other than an outflow of the seven properties, for it arose from the six working properties, and in the seventh, as in Paradise, it stands in rest; this is the eternal Sabbath and rest, wherein the working of the Divine power rests.

83. Moses says: God created heaven and earth along with all creatures in six days, and rested on the seventh day and commanded it for rest, Genesis 2: 1, 2. In these words the understanding lies secretly hidden: He could indeed have made all His works in one day; also, one cannot speak of any "day" before the sun existed, for in the Deep there is only a single day, but the understanding lies hidden in the words.

84. By the "day-works," he understands the creation or revelation of the seven properties, for he says: In the beginning God created heaven and earth. Genesis 1: 1. # XX. Key, or Explanation

The effect became clearer, which Moses calls Heaven; for the Scripture says: God dwells in Heaven; Psalm 2:4. And this fiery Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius"; here referring to the active, transformative principle in alchemy and nature, rather than the planet or metal alone</note> is the power of the firmament, acting as a reflection of the spiritual world in which God is manifest.

85. When this had happened, God said: Let there be light; and there was light. Genesis 1:3. There, the inner light broke through the fiery heaven, whereby a power of radiance arose in the fiery Mercury; and that was the light of external nature within the properties in which all growing life exists.

86. On the second day's work, God separated the watery and fiery Mercury from one another, and called the fiery one the firmament of heaven, which was formed from the midst of the water as the Mercury. There, the masculine and feminine kinds originated within the spirit of the external world—the masculine in the fiery Mercury, and the feminine in the watery Mercury.

87. Such a separation occurred everywhere through all things, and to this end: that the fiery Mercury should long again for the watery, and the watery for the fiery, so that a desire of love might exist between them in the light of nature, from which their union arose. Thus the fiery Mercury, as the out-spoken Word according to the fiery and watery nature of light, divided itself; and from this now comes the masculine and feminine kind of all things, both in living and growing things.

88. On the third day's work, the fiery and watery Mercury entered again into a conjunction <note>original: "Conjunction"; a term for the union of opposites</note> or mixing, and conceived one another; then the saltpeter <note>original: "Salniter"; used here to describe a volatile, fertile earth-spirit or mineral force</note>, acting as the separation within the earth, brought forth grass, herbs, and trees, and the first birthing between masculine and feminine kinds took place.

89. On the fourth day's work, the fiery Mercury brought forth its fruits, namely the quintessence <note>original: "fünfte Essentiam"; the "fifth essence" or purest underlying force of nature</note>, a higher life-force than the four elements, which is nevertheless within the elements. From this the stars were created; for just as the impression of desire brought the earth into a mass—which impression moves inward—so also the fiery Mercury was pressing outward from that impression,

90. On the fifth day's work, the <term>Spiritus Mundi</term> <note>Spirit of the World; the animating soul of the physical universe</note>, as the soul of the great world, revealed itself in the fifth essence <note>the quintessence or purest element</note>. Understand this as the life of the fiery and watery <term>Mercury</term> <note>In this context, Mercury refers to the "life-force" or "solvent" that drives growth and movement</note>; there God created all animals, fish, birds, and creeping things, each out of its own property of the divided Mercury.

91. There one sees how the eternal <term>Principles</term> <note>original: "Principia"</note> moved according to both evil and good, according to all seven properties, according to their outflowing and mixing. For there, good and evil creatures were created, all according to how Mercury—acting as the <term>Separator</term> <note>the force that divides unity into specific forms</note>—formed itself into a being <note>original: "Ens"</note>, as can be seen in the evil and good creatures. And there, all life arose in the light of nature, as in the love of nature; therefore all species love one another according to this outflowing love within their own property.

92. On the sixth day's work, God created Man; for on the sixth day's work, the understanding of life revealed itself out of the fiery Mercury, as if from the inward ground. God created him in His likeness, out of all three <term>Principles</term> into one image, and breathed into him the understanding, fiery Mercury—according to the inner and outer ground, as according to eternity and time—into a living, understanding soul. And within this soulful ground hovered the revelation of Divine Holiness, as the living, outflowing Word of God with the eternally-known <term>Idea</term>, which had been known in Divine Wisdom from eternity as a <term>Subject</term> <note>original: "Subjectum"</note> or form of Divine <term>Imagination</term>.

93. This [soul] was clothed with the essence of the heavenly world; thus it became an understanding spirit and temple of God, an image of Divine contemplation. This spirit was given to the soul as a spouse—just as fire and light are spouses, so it is to be understood here. That same Divine ground blossomed and permeated soul and body; that was the true Paradise within man, which he lost through sin when the ground of the dark world, with its false desire, gained dominion over him.

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the "Key" (Clavis), specifically addressing the spiritual significance of the Sabbath and the nature of the physical world's creation.</meta>

# XX. Key, or Explanation

94. On the seventh day, God rested from all His works which He had made, as Moses speaks in Genesis 2:2. Now, God certainly requires no rest, for He has worked from eternity and is a pure, active power; therefore, the true meaning lies hidden here in the word. For Moses says: God commanded the seventh day for rest. The seventh day was the true Paradise (understand this spiritually), namely the <term>Tincture</term> <note>In Böhme's alchemy, the Tincture is the spiritual life-force or "dye" that gives quality and beauty to matter</note> of Divine power, which is a <term>Temperamentum</term> <note>A perfect balance or "tempering" of opposing qualities</note>; this power penetrated through all properties and worked in the seventh, as if in the very essence of all the others.

95. The Tincture penetrated through the earth and all elements and "tinged" <note>imbued with spiritual quality</note> everything; there, Paradise was on earth and within man, for evil was still hidden, just as the night is hidden within the day. Likewise, the <term>Wrath of Nature</term> <note>The harsh, consuming fire of existence without the light of God</note> was hidden in the first <term>Principle</term> <note>The dark foundation of reality</note> until the Fall of Man. At that point, the Divine activity fled with the Tincture into its own Principle—into the inner ground of the Light-World—for the Wrath turned upward and gained the upper-regime. And that is the curse: that God cursed the earth. For His "cursing" is the fleeing of His activity <margin>or: then His fleeing or cursing is His active cursing</margin>. When God's power works in a thing alongside that thing's own life and spirit, and afterwards withdraws His activity from that thing, then it is cursed; for it then works only in its own will, and not in God's will.

## XI. Concerning the *Spiritus Mundi* <note>The Spirit of the World</note> and the Four Elements.

96. We can very well contemplate the hidden spiritual world by looking at the visible world; for we see that in the "Deep" of the world, fire, light, and air <margin>or: fire, air, and water</margin> are constantly being born, and that there is no rest or ceasing of such birthing, and it has been so from the beginning of this world. Yet, in the outer world, we can find no cause for what this foundation might be. Instead, Reason says: "God created it so," and there it stops. This is true in itself, but Reason does not know the Creator who creates in this way without ceasing—namely the true <term>Archaeus</term> <note>The master-chemist or vital force of nature</note> or <term>Separator</term> <note>The power that divides unity into various forms and qualities</note>, which is an outflowing...

from the invisible world, as the outflowing Word of God, which I indicate with the term "fiery Mercury" <note>original: "feurischer Mercurius"; in alchemy, Mercury represents the principle of volatility and the spirit of life</note>.

97. For what the invisible world is in a spiritual operation—where light and darkness are within one another, and neither grasps the other—that is what the visible world is in a substantial operation. For whatever powers are to be understood in the outflowing Word within the internal spiritual world, those same powers are understood in the visible world by the stars <note>original: "Gestirne"</note> and also by the elements; but in a different principle of a holy kind.

98. At the beginning of this time, they were breathed out from the internal ground and set into their own active being and life. Therefore, the outer world is called a "principle," as a subject of the inner world and a tool of the inner spiritual Master, which Master is God’s Word and Power.

99. And just as the inner Divine (spiritual) world has within itself an intelligent life from the outflowing of Divine Knowledge—in which the angels and souls are understood—so too does the outer world have a rational life within itself. This life stands in the outflowing powers of the inner world, and this outer life has no higher understanding than that in which it stands: namely, in the stars and in the four elements.

100. The *Spiritus Mundi* <note>The "Spirit of the World" or the animating soul of the universe</note> is hidden in the four elements like the soul in the body, and is nothing other than an outflowing and active power from the sun and the stars. Its housing, in which it works, is spiritual (or "Spirit") surrounded by the four elements. This (spiritual) housing is, firstly, a sharp magnetic power from the outflowing of the inner world, from the first property of eternal nature; this is the ground of all compositions and powers, as well as all formation and essence.

101. Secondly, it is the outflowing of the inner movement, which has flowed out from the second form of eternal nature and stands in a fiery manner, like a dry water-source, in which the ground of all metals and stones is understood, for they were created from it. This...

# XX. Key, or Explanation

<margin>The ground of movement</margin>
...ground I call the fiery Mercury <note>original: "Mercurium"; in alchemy, Mercury represents the principle of volatility and the "spirit" of a substance</note> in the spirit of this world, for it is the movement of all things; a separator of powers, a shaper of forms, and a foundation of the external life, according to movement and perception.

102. The third ground is the sensitivity in the movement of sharpness, acting as a spiritual sulfur-source <note>original: "Schwefel-Quall"</note> from the ground of the anxious will in the inner foundation; from this arises the spirit with the five senses (namely seeing, hearing, feeling, tasting, and smelling) as the true essential life, from which the fire, as the fourth form, becomes manifest.

103. These three properties were called by the ancient sages Salt, Mercury, and Sulfur <note>original: "Sal, Mercurius und Sulphur"</note>, according to their materials which are born from them in the four elements, in which such a spirit coagulates or makes itself substantial. Within this ground also lie the four elements, and they are not separated from it or anything special; they are merely the manifestation of such a spiritual ground, acting as a housing for the spirit in which such a spirit works.

104. The earth is the coarsest outflow of this subtle spirit; after the earth, water is the second; after water, air is the third; and after air, fire is the fourth. These have arisen from a single ground, namely from the Spirit of the World <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"</note>, which has its root in the inner world.

105. Now reason asks: For what purpose did the Creator make such a manifestation? Answer: There is no other cause than that the spiritual world might introduce itself into a visible, figurative form, so that the inner powers might become pictorial and formal. For this to happen, the spiritual essence had to introduce itself into a material ground in which it could build and form itself; and such a separation had to occur so that the separated parts would forever long for their first ground—the inner for the outer, and the outer for the inner.

106. So it is also with the four elements, which inwardly are only a single ground; there, each must long for the other and desire the other, and the inner ground...

in the other, for the inner element is decided within them; and the four elements <note>The classical elements: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire</note> are only properties of the divided element. 108. There is no thing in the essence of this world in which a formed shape does not stand according to the inner spiritual world, either according to the wrath <term>Grimm: the harsh, fiery, or "dark" quality of God's nature in Böhme's system</term> of the inner ground, or according to the (good) power; and yet, in the most poisonous power, in the inward ground, the greatest virtue from the inner world often lies within.

109. But where a dark life is in a thing, like a dark oil, there is little to hope for, because it is a foundation of wrath, like a false, evil, and reprehensible poison. But where the life stands in the poison <gloss>or: Spirit</gloss> and has a light radiance in the oil, as in the *Quinta Essentia* <note>The "Fifth Essence" or Quintessence; the purest, most medicinal part of a substance</note>, there Heaven stands revealed in Hell, and there great virtue lies hidden; understood by our own <note>A common phrase Böhme uses to address his initiated followers or "fellow-laborers" in the spirit</note>.

110. The entire visible world is a vain, spermatic, working ground; every being longs for the other, the upper for the lower, and the lower for the upper, for they are separated from one another. In such hunger, they receive one another in desire, as can be seen in the Earth: she is so hungry for the stars and the *Spiritus Mundi* <note>The "Spirit of the World," a mediating force between the physical and spiritual realms</note>, as for the spirit from which she sprouted in the beginning, that she has no rest from hunger. And this same hunger of the Earth is a consumption of the bodies <note>original: "Corporum"</note>, so that the Spirit may again be separated from the coarse elemental nature and enter again into its *Archaeus* <note>The hidden formative principle or "master workman" within nature</note>.

111. Furthermore, we see in such hunger the impregnation—

movement of the <term>Archeus</term><note>The 'Archeus' is a term used in Paracelsian alchemy to describe the internal 'master workman' or vital force that directs the growth and separation of matter.</note>, acting as the <term>Separator</term><note>original: "Separatoris"; the force that divides pure essence from dross.</note>, just as the lower Archeus of the earth draws the upper subtle Archeus from the stars into itself. 113. Herein now should man (as a noble image, which has its foundation in time and eternity) well consider himself, and not travel so blindly, seeking his eternal fatherland far away from him. It is within him, but covered by the coarseness of the elements and their strife. When the strife of the elements ceases in the dying of the coarse body, then the spiritual man becomes manifest, whether he was born in light or in darkness. Whichever power holds the government within him, in that the spiritual man remains eternally, either in the foundation of the wrath of God, or in His love.

114. For the outer visible man is not now the true image of God; he is only an image of the Archeus, and a housing for the spiritual man, in which the spiritual man grows like gold in the coarse stone and the herb from the wild earth, as the Scripture also says of it: *1 Corinthians 15:44. If we have a natural body, we also have a spiritual body; of whatever kind the natural is, such is also the spiritual.* But the outer, coarse, four-elemental body shall not inherit God’s kingdom, but rather this one, which is born from the single element, as from Divine revelation and operation: *John 1:13, chapter 3:5, 6, 7, chapter 6:63.* For it is not this one of the flesh nor of the will of man, but this one which is worked in this coarse [body] by the heavenly Archeus, for which purpose

the coarse [body] is only a housing and a tool. But when the shell perishes, then it will become manifest why we have all called ourselves human beings, and yet one part of us were scarcely animals—indeed, were even more evil than animals.

115. Thus we should rightly contemplate the *Spiritum* <note>original: "Spiritum"; the spirit or animating breath</note> of the outer world: namely, that it is a housing and a tool of the inner spiritual world, which is hidden within it, and which works through the outer world, and thus introduces itself into formed images.

116. And so also is man’s Reason <note>original: "Vernunft"; refers here to worldly, discursive logic</note> only a housing for the true Understanding <note>original: "Verstand"; refers to higher spiritual intuition or intellect</note> of Divine Knowledge; therefore, no one should trust too much in his reason and cleverness; it is only the outer stars according to their constellation, and it misleads him as often as it leads him to God’s Unity.

117. Reason should surrender itself entirely to God, so that the inward *Archaeus* <note>original: "Archæus"; a term from Paracelsian alchemy referring to the vital internal artisan or formative principle of life</note> may become manifest; this will work and give birth to a true spiritual, intelligent, God-like foundation, in which God’s Spirit becomes manifest, and which leads the understanding to God; then the spirit searches all things in such a foundation; yes, even the depths of the Deity, as St. Paul says in 1 Corinthians 2:10. I wanted to briefly outline this for the lovers [of truth], for them to meditate upon further.

### XII. There follows a brief explanation or formula of Divine Revelation.

118. God is the eternal, immeasurable, incomprehensible Unity, who reveals Himself in Himself from eternity to eternity, with the Trinity <note>original: "Dreyheit"</note>, and is a Father, Son, and Holy Spirit in three kinds of workings, as mentioned previously.

119. The first outflow and revelation of this Trinity is the Eternal Word, or the speaking-forth of Divine Power; the first spoken-forth being out of the Power is the Divine Wisdom as an essence, in which the Power works.

120. Out of Wisdom flows the power of the breathing-out, and goes into differentiation <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit"; the process by which the unified divine light divides into various qualities or forms</note> and formation, wherein the Divine Power becomes manifest in its virtue.

121. These same differentiated powers lead themselves into accept-

# XX. Key, or Explanation

...into a state of self-sensitivity, and from this sensitivity arises self-will and desire.

122. This self-will is the foundation of eternal nature; it leads itself through desire into various properties, reaching as far as the fire.

123. Within the desire, darkness arises, and within the fire, the eternal Unity becomes manifest along with the light, inside the fiery nature.

124. From this fiery and light-filled nature, type, and property, the angels and the souls of humanity have originated as a Divine revelation.

125. The power of the fire and light is called <term>Tincture</term> <note>In Böhme's thought, the Tincture is the spiritual "dye" or essence that transmits life and transformation</note>, and the movement of this power is called the holy or pure element.

126. The darkness becomes substantial within itself, and the light also becomes substantial within the fiery desire; these two constitute two <term>Principles</term> <note>original: "Principia"</note>: namely, in the darkness is God’s wrath, and in the light is God’s love. Each works within itself; and there is only a distinction like that between day and night, yet both are but a single foundation. Each is a cause of the other, so that the one may be manifested and known within the other, just as light comes forth from fire.

127. The visible world is the third <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principium"</note>, serving as the third foundation and beginning: this was breathed out from the inner foundation—that is, from the first two—and brought into creaturely form and kind.

128. The inward eternal working is hidden within the visible world; it is in all things and through all things, and yet remains incomprehensible to all things in their own power. The outer forces are merely the passive part, or the casing, within which the inner forces work. Common creatures are made only from the essence of the outer world, but man was created from time and eternity, out of the "Essence of all Essences," into an image of Divine revelation.

129. The eternal revelation of Divine light is called the Kingdom of Heaven, a dwelling place for the holy angels and souls. The fiery darkness is called Hell or the Wrath of God, wherein the devils dwell along with the damned souls. In the location of this world, Heaven and Hell are present everywhere, but according to the inward foundation.

140. The word Science <note>Scienz</note> is understood by me just as it is understood in the Latin language: however, I understand within it the true ground according to its *Sensus* <note>Sense or meaning</note>, which has fallen into a lack of understanding in Latin and indeed in all languages; for every word, in its composition, formation, and pronunciation, gives the true understanding of what that thing is which is so named.

141. You understand "Science" to mean a branch of knowledge or cognition: in German this is indeed spoken correctly, but not fully expressed.

142. Science is the root of understanding, as well as of sensibility: it is the root of the Center of the "grasping" of the Nothing into a Something; as where the Will of the *Ungrund* <note>The "Abyss" or "Groundless"; the primordial void before creation</note> draws itself into a Center of comprehensibility (that is, into the Word), thus the true understanding originates. It exists in the divisibility of Science, where the Will separates itself from the grasped compaction; thus, in that which is separated (where the divisibility grasps itself into being), one first understands the Essence.

# Of the Principal Points and Words
## XIV.

The root of the senses. Yet in the understanding, where it is called <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: "Scienz"; from the Latin 'scientia', meaning knowledge or a formative power of knowing</note>, it is not the sensuality itself, but the cause of sensuality. In the manner that the understanding takes hold in the <term>mind</term> <note>original: "Gemüthe"; refers to the seat of emotions and soul-disposition</note>, there must first be a cause which provides the mind, from which the understanding flows out into its contemplation. Thus, Scienz is the root of the fiery mind; and it is, in sum, the root of all spiritual beginnings, as the true root of the soul, and so forth through all life, for it is the ground of life from which it comes.

144. I have otherwise been able to give it no other name, because this one coincides so completely in the <term>sense</term> <note>original: "Sensu"; the inner meaning or sensory resonance of the word</note>: for it is the cause by which the Divine, unfathomable Will draws itself in, and takes hold in Nature, toward the distinct, recognizable, and sensitive life of understanding and discernment; for with the drawing-in of the Scienz, as the Will pulls the same into itself, the natural life and the Word of all lives originates.

145. The distinction arising from the fire is to be understood as follows: The eternal Scienz in the Will of the Father draws the Will (which is called Father) into itself, and encloses itself in a center of the Divine birth of the <term>Trinity</term> <note>original: "Dreyheit"</note>; and expresses itself with the Scienz into a Word of the understanding. And in the speaking, there is the distinctness within the Scienz; there, in every distinctness, is the desire for the enclosure of the expression; and the enclosure is substantial, and is called <term>Divine Essence</term> <note>original: "Göttliche Essenz"</note>.

146. Out of this Essence, the Word now expresses itself in the second distinction (as if from Nature): And in that same expression, as the creaturely will separates into its center into a sensuality, there the distinction from the fiery Scienz is understood; for from this is the soul and all angelic spirits.

147. The third distinction happens according to the outer nature of the spoken, formed Word, wherein lies the <term>animal Scienz</term> <note>original: "viehische Scienz"; referring to the instinctual or biological drive</note>; as is to be seen in the treatise on the <term>Election of Grace</term> <note>original: "Gnaden Wahl"; a reference to Boehme's work 'De Electione Gratiae'</note>, which is very sharp in its understanding, and is one of the clearest among my writings.

<summary>This page defines 'Scienz' as the foundational root of the soul and divine will, explaining how the Divine Essence differentiates itself into spiritual, angelic, and natural life. It highlights the process of the Divine Word expressing itself through various levels of existence.</summary>

<keywords>Scienz, Divine Will, Trinity, Divine Essence, Soul, Election of Grace, Jacob Boehme, spiritual origin, understanding</keywords>

### XIV.
# SPECIAL KEY,
## Or explanation of various words, partly the author's own and partly Paracelsian,
### Which the author shared specifically with his most trusted friends.

->Whereby the preceding table can be consulted at the same time.<-

->The following is from Mr. Johann Sigmund von Schweinitz’s own hand.<-

GOD is Nothing, compared to the creature, and is the <term>Ungrund</term> <note>The "Abyss" or "Groundless" — a term used by mystics like Jacob Böhme to describe the absolute nothingness out of which God and the universe emerge</note> and All.

1. Will (Father) from which proceeds <term>Scienz</term> <note>original: "Scienz" — here meaning a spiritual "knowing" or the root of the spirit's science and all powers</note>, the root of the knowledge of the spirit or of all powers.

2. Desire, Mind, or Heart of the Will (Son) is the outcome of the <term>Scienz</term>.

3. Desire is a state of being contained; within that which is contained, understand the Trinity, from which is breathed out:

4. The speaking Word in God (in potential) or the Divine Power.

5. Wisdom is the outcome of the Word, otherwise called the <term>Mysterium Magnum</term> <note>Latin: "Great Mystery" — the chaos or womb from which all things are created</note> or Divine Contemplation.

And thus far God is to be understood as to what He is without nature and creature, as a being generating within Himself.

### Follows the speaking-forth of the Word, as the sensitivity or perceptibility.

->I. PRINCIPLE.<-

TINCTURE is the speaking of the Word, (The Word in Action.) The first form of nature is Desire or the attractive power, which shadows itself within itself, and from which darkness arises. 1. Sharpness is a property of Desire, which impresses itself and makes itself hard. 2. Hardness is the second property arising from it. 3. Coldness is the third property. This Desire, with its three properties, is a mother of all Salts.

SALT. The second form is <term>Scienz</term> or the bitter sting of perceptibility or sensitivity, from which the mercurial life arises (the five senses).

MERCURY <note>original: "Mercurius"; here referring to the third property of nature: the restless, rotating motion of the mind.</note>. ### SECOND PRINCIPLE.
## The Divine Light-World or Angelic World.

The fifth form is the light or love-fire from the Divine Freedom, or the Holy Tincture <note>term: Tincture; in Böhme’s thought, this is the spiritual "dye" or essence that transforms and beautifies matter.</note>.

The sixth form is the resounding, the sound, the ringing, the manifest life, the Divine life, the spiritual mercurial life.

The seventh form is the bodily being of all forms in all three worlds; these are the seven forms from which comes the "welling up," as a movement of all seven forms, which is a root of the four elements; it is called the pure element, in which the budding or growth in the Divine Light-World arises, known as Paradise.

These seven forms with their properties and all their being, have breathed out in time the:

### THIRD PRINCIPLE.
## The outer visible world.

1. The Heaven or the incomprehensibility of the four elements; which has breathed out:

2. The Fifth Essence <note>original: "Quintam Essentiam"; the underlying spiritual quintessence behind physical matter.</note>, as the stars and growing life, from which come:

3. The four elements, from which the earthly creatures were created.

# XX. Key, or Explanation

GOD is the delight of eternity as the Abyss <note>original: "Ungrund"; refers to the bottomless, non-spatial void before creation</note>: This same delight contains within itself a will toward the revelation of that delight; and the will grasps itself within itself to become its own ground—that is the Center of the Deity. And it is a Chaos, like an eye of eternity, of the abyss and the ground. This same will is a desiring will, seeking to reveal itself in the highest kingdom of joy, as the power of Majesty, within that which the will has grasped. Now, however, no revelation can occur without an impression or movement. Thus, the impression is harsh <note>original: "herbe"; implies astringency, contraction, or sharpness</note>, which is the *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let there be"; the creative command</note> of all beings. It is the contracted, desiring will.

Will is the father of all being.

Delight is a longing to reveal itself, and is the Angelic World.

Desire produces the dark world and nature.

Chaos is the Abyss, from which the soul originates in the Fire-Eye. If the spirit is born out of the soul, it is the likeness and image of GOD, which possesses the Angelic World.

1. The impression gives birth to Harshness. This harshness, out of its own self-property, creates (♄) Saturn, (♂) Mars, and (☿) Mercury—that is, PHUR within SUL <note>Böhme’s alchemical shorthand for Sulphur; SUL is the divine light/oil, PHUR is the fiery, stinging motion</note>.

SUL is from GOD, or the revealed GOD Himself. PHUR creates the wheel of the senses: And the essence of being creates the bitter stinging, as the second form. Or even more clearly:

The 1st Form is SAL <note>Salt</note>, Saturn, the desire of the eternal Word (which is GOD); it is the beginning of eternal nature and is the grasping of the eternal Nothing into Something. It is the cause of all beings, including cold and heat, as well as water and air; the formation of powers, and a cause of taste: the Mother of all salts.

2. Bitter stinging, which draws the harshness into the impression.

This drawing is the bitter sting, (☿) Mercury, the mover toward life.

The second form is (☿) Mercury, the mobility of desire, acting as the attraction; it is the cause of all life and motion, as well as the senses and differentiation.

3. Thus it creates anguish, and the sting rages within that anguish:

# Of the Principal Points and Words

Thus the desire of the free delight separates back into itself, as if into nothingness, and has sharpened itself within the impression, and has become a fire—indeed, a fire of love.

The third form is $\triangle$ Sulphur <note>original: "Sulphur"; here referring to the alchemical principle of combustibility and soul-force</note> or ($\sigma^{\nearrow}$) Mars; it is anguish, acting as the sensitivity, a cause of the mind <note>original: "Gemüths"; the seat of emotions and soul-will</note> wherein the senses become active.

4. Fire. The first Principle: (1) Harshness, (2) Bitterness, (3) Anguish, these give the essence of fire, and the free delight gives the light. The Groundless <note>original: "Ungrund"; the absolute, undifferentiated divine nothingness from which all begins</note> ignites itself within the impression and creates the first Principle through movement. The light creates the second Principle through power and the kingdom of joy.

The fourth form creates the truly living spirit, half in the fire-world according to its essence, and half in the light-world according to its understanding. The power within the light is the understanding and the intelligent spirit.

->SULPHUR; To be considered thus:<-

PHUR is the mover of life and stands in the four above forms: (1) Harsh, (2) Bitter-sting, (3) Anguish, (4) Fire. (1) Harshness is called Saturn $\hbar$. (2) Bitter-sting is called Mercury $\$. (3) Anguish is called the torment of fire $\triangle$. (4) And in the Sulphuric torment the light becomes shining, as the Groundless becomes a radiance; and in that radiance the first three forms—Harsh, Bitter, Anguish—are transformed.

->$\triangle$ Fire, $\sigma^{\nearrow}$ Mars, and $\odot$ Sun.<-

The $\triangle$ Fire is a cause of the true spirit-life, where the holy powers of the free delight are liberated from the harsh roughness; for the $\triangle$ Fire swallows the dark being of the impression into its essence and carries it out into spiritual powers within itself.

### The FIRST PRINCIPLE

This is the most primordial origin in the seven tormenting-spirits <note>original: "Qual-Geister"; the seven source-qualities of nature that "labor" or "torment" to produce manifestation</note> of God, as one imagines it when the light was not within; therein is the sharpness—that is, the wrath and hellish torment and pain—which existed and came to be, and is that from which Lucifer became a devil. To this place all damned souls belong.

Between the First and Second Principle, the Natural Heaven is the distinction.

<meta>Continuity from page 113: The author continues the systematic explanation of the "Seven Forms" or properties of nature, moving from the harsh foundational qualities toward the spiritual and divine manifestations.</meta>

# XX. Key, or Explanation

spiritual love-desire, where the Holy Will of God has sharpened itself in the stern impression, and revealed itself through the <term>Fire (△)</term> <note>original: "Feuer"; in Boehme's alchemy, fire represents the turning point between the dark world of longing and the light world of love</note> with the power of omnipotence. This now leads itself out through the fire into the light, and has thus introduced itself into the powers of life and motion, into desire, wherein the Holy Birth and the joyful kingdom of the greatest love of God stands and is manifest.

The fifth form is the Light; (to be considered) as when a light rises, which made the second Principle, as the kingdom of joy: This is God Himself, the Heart of God, the Son, and the Kingdom of Heaven, and eternal heavenly bliss. The Light makes the second Principle with power and the kingdom of joy. Here the Holy Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son, and is the bliss, joy, and God Himself. This has separated and adorned everything in the angelic and sidereal <note>star-like or celestial</note> world. The fifth form is Love-Desire. In the radiance, the first three forms—namely Harshness, Bitterness, and Anguish—are transformed. Out of Harshness comes a love-desire, the fifth form. This love-desire is also an impression, like an awakened life; that is the living, intelligent <term>Mercury (☿)</term> <note>original: "Mercurius"; here representing the principle of communication, movement, and the senses</note> which gives the five senses and speech. The fifth form is the center of the desire of love, the Divine eternal <term>Fiat</term> <note>Latin: "Let it be done"; the creative command of God</note>: This takes hold of itself again in the powers of holiness and gentleness.

The sixth form: The origin of life <term>Jupiter (♃)</term>, sound, resonance, word. Subsequently, such a center and eternal Divine Fiat (as mentioned in the previous fifth form) is the out-speaking and re-captured, spoken and counter-speaking Word, according to heavenly properties; namely the resonance, sound, voice, tone; also in growing things, as in Paradise it is the Holy Spirit still now greening-forth in this world. The sixth form is the living, Divine, creaturely, human <term>Mercury (☿)</term>. And it is the sound of the Divine Word out of the Divine powers, which forms itself in the love-desire, and introduces itself into an audible word of all powers: Wherein the revelation of the Divine kingdom of joy stands in the free delight of the Wisdom of God. The sixth form has spoken out this world into the seventh form.

<summary>This page explains the fifth and sixth "forms" of divine manifestation, describing how the harsh forces of nature are transformed through spiritual fire into light, love, and the creative "Word" or sound associated with Mercury and Jupiter.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the seven properties of nature, specifically focusing on the transition from the spiritual forms to the physical and divine manifestation in the seventh form, leading into the creation of the Third Principle (the material world).</meta>

# Of the Principal Points and Words

This seventh form—this desire of <term>Mercury</term><note>original: "Mercurii"; in Böhme's alchemy, Mercury represents the "word" or the driving motion of life</note> (as <term>Saturn</term><note>original: "Saturnus"; representing the cold, contracting, and grounding force</note> was reported in the fifth form)—is a hunger, and draws into itself the power of the light, and in the desire of the <term>impression</term><note>The process by which spiritual desire "compacts" or "presses" itself into physical substance</note> makes it bodily; this is now the seventh form: a house of the spirit and a being. This seventh form is the essence of nature, <term>Luna</term><note>The Moon; representing the physical body or the "container" of the soul</note> and Saturn, and is the formed essence of power. What the first six forms are in the spirit, the seventh is in the comprehensible essence, acting as a casing for all the others: or as a body of the spirit, wherein the spirit works and plays with itself. It is also a food for the fire, from which the <term>fire-essence</term><note>original: "△ Feuer Essenz"</note> draws for its consumption, wherein it burns; and the seventh is the kingdom of the glory of God. This seventh form, or the revealed power of God, desires to behold itself: it grasps the heavenly Divine powers and itself again, and is the heavenly nature, the <term>Wisdom</term><note>Sophia; the divine mirror or feminine principle of God's self-reflection</note>, the angelic world, the <term>Great Mystery</term><note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; the chaotic "nothing" or "chaos" from which all things are manifested</note>. Thus have light and darkness played with one another. In the seventh form, God's Wisdom has been revealed from eternity, out of which He created all things. The creation of all things is the revealed, formed Wisdom, which in the human quality of the new birth is called Christ. The seventh form is the <term>Sabbath</term>, wherein the other six rest from all their workings; they give birth to themselves again and rest again. This is the eternal essence.

->The III. PRINCIPLE.<-

In the first impression, the first three forms are the dark world. In the second impression, these same three forms are the shine of the light and are the angelic world. What is hellish in the first dark impression is heavenly in the second impression. Out of these both—out of the desire of the light and the dark—the Third Principle is born, entirely according to the quality of the two inner worlds: for out of the quality of light and darkness, which are One and not two, this world was spoken forth, and grasped and formed in the <term>Word Fiat</term><note>original: "Verbo Fiat"; the creative "Let there be" from Genesis</note> (as the desire). The Third Principle is outside the heaven of nature, yet it <term>qualifies</term><note>original: "inqualirets"; works within or influences the properties of</note> within the same, and is the <term>sidereal spirit</term><note>The "starry" or astral spirit that governs the physical world and the elements</note> of God. Herein the sidereal air is the spirit, and is the animal kingdom, for the earthly belongs to it; this arose in the wrath of God when this world was created, and is this world.

<summary>This page describes the seventh form of nature as the physical "house" or body where the spiritual forces of the first six forms manifest as the Kingdom of Glory and Divine Wisdom. It further introduces the Third Principle, explaining how the material world was "spoken forth" from the union of light and dark desires through the creative Word.</summary>

<keywords>Seventh Form, Divine Wisdom, Sophia, Sabbath, Third Principle, Sidereal Spirit, Verbo Fiat, Mercury, Saturn, Luna, Impression</keywords>

# XX. Key, or Explanation

From this we must enter into the other middle Principle, so that we may become children of God: But if we are not partakers of the second Principle, then the sidereal spirit <note>The "sidereal spirit" refers to the astral or star-influenced mind that governs natural human reason without divine grace.</note> rules us in the light of nature, which is a subtle deception; thus we are heathens.

### This from Mr. Friedrich Krause’s, M. D. own hand.

->The Author’s Skeleton of the aforementioned, a key to his writings.<- <note>original: "Autoris Sceleton prædictorum"</note>

The Abyss <note>The "Ungrund" or "Groundless": the absolute, undifferentiated nothingness from which God emerges in Böhmean thought.</note>, Nothing. The unfathomable will of the Father proceeds from the Abyss.

The proceeding forth is the Spirit of the Will.

That which has proceeded forth is the "Found," acting as the Mirror.

The Will imagines within the Mirror of the Found, draws into itself, and impregnates itself; this is the Heart. Thus the Will dwells as Father, and the Heart as Son, and the proceeding forth as Spirit within the Mirror.

The Mirror is the Eye of the Mystery <note>original: "Mysterii"</note>.

The Spirit, which proceeds from the Will and the Heart, is the Seeing or the Essence within the Mirror. The Mirror is its luster with the wonders.

The Wonder is a flash with all colors, yet none of them are yet recognized individually. The Wonder is a desire for Nature, wherein it may manifest itself; and it receives Nature into the Desire.

The Desire imagines toward the Light of Freedom, and becomes manifest out of itself in the Fire: and within itself in the dark world.

The Fire becomes manifest in the Light within Freedom; and the Mystery with the Mirror of Wonders—which stands and also remains in the original state within Freedom—becomes manifest with the Light, and is called God, encompassed with Essentiality. The Fire of God lusts after Essence, and receives Essence.

In the Essence is the Tincture <note>A term from alchemy used here to describe the spiritual "dye" or life-force that transforms and enlivens matter.</note>.

The Tincture desires a Body, which is the Element.

The Element desires the Being <note>original: "Ens"</note>;

The Being is Paradise, or the substance of the essence in the first being. The Being of the Fire and of the Light is called the Fifth Essence <note>original: "Quinta Essentia"; the purest, most concentrated form of a substance.</note>.

<summary>This page outlines a mystical cosmology where the divine Trinity emerges from a "Groundless" abyss through a process of reflection and desire, eventually manifesting as light, fire, and the "Fifth Essence." It serves as a condensed "skeleton" or key to understanding the author's complex spiritual philosophy.</summary>
<keywords>Abyss, Trinity, Mirror of Wonders, Tincture, Fifth Essence, Friedrich Krause, Jacob Böhme, spiritual alchemy, sidereal spirit</keywords>

All of this desires being out of itself; that is the outer world, a complete likeness of the inner world.

### Another key of the author, from the hand of the same Mr. Friedrich Kraus, M. D. <note>Friedrich Kraus was a physician and associate of Böhme; this section provides his specific definitions of Böhme's complex terminology.</note>

**Abyss:** Eternal Will of the unfathomable Being.

**Mystery of the Trinity** with Wisdom outside of nature, is Will, Heart, Spirit, Mirror; it is not called God. But rather in the ignition of the Principle.

**Principle** is, according to the Father's nature, his property; in Majesty he is called God. For in Majesty is the Godhead, and is Spirit, Life, Power.

**Wisdom** is the housing of the Mystery outside of nature; it is like a glass or mirror of the Mystery. Here it is only the mirror of Wisdom, of wonders.

**Wisdom** is the revelation of the Godhead, and the cause of the Father's imagination toward the Son.

**Dark World** is the ground, the sinking.

**Fire-World**, Father. I. PRINCIPLE.

**Light-World**, Son. II. PRINCIPLE.

The **Holy Spirit** proceeds from the Father and Son.

**Wisdom** is the housing of these three. Here the Trinity dwells in Wisdom.

**Being** is Tincture <note>In alchemy and Böhme's thought, the 'Tincture' is the spiritual essence or life-force that transforms matter.</note>, the highest, and Tincture is Being.

**Essentiality** is dead in itself; Tincture is its life.

**Essentiality** is Divine, heavenly corporeality.

**Aquaster** <note>A Paracelsian term referring to a visionary or sidereal water, distinct from common water.</note> is heavenly essentiality, from heavenly water, wherein are <term>Mercury</term> <note>The principle of movement and spirit.</note>, <term>Sulphur</term> <note>The principle of light and growth.</note>, and <term>Salt</term> <note>The principle of physical substance and stability.</note>.

**Fire** is the true Principle; the strong, great, almighty life, [the Father (says Christ) is greater than I. John 14:28. For the fire is greater than the light] between all three worlds, giving torment <note>original: "Qual" - Böhme uses this to mean both 'torment' and 'source/quality' of life.</note> and essences to all three. From the torment arises the true intelligible life. What the fire gives, from that comes understanding. Fire is called Being, and gives Spirit. Fire gives wind: Light gives air.

**Essence** is the whole being within one another: it is the life of essentiality. Essence has no life nor being; it is like a mirror and the finding; it is more subtle than being; it draws into itself,

<meta>catchword: itself,</meta>

# XX. Key, or Explanation

itself, so that it becomes substantiality <note>original: "Wesenheit"</note>. Substantiality comes from the essence. From the sun’s essence comes water. *Es*—that is fire in the Language of Nature: *sentz* is the containment, the becoming-body, which holds the fire within itself. Essence is like a greening, a well-springing (like the life-spirits in the flesh), or rain, or small lives within Wisdom without the true Spirit. Yet it is a spirit from which the true Spirit and life arise, as if from its primary cause.

**Ens** <note>Latin: "being" or "entity"</note> is the greening.
**Iliaster** <note>A Paracelsian term for the prime matter of the universe</note> is the beginning of Nature.
**Cagastrum** <note>A term for corruptible or earthly matter</note> is the outward fire in the out-birth.
**Necrolice** is the dark world in the first three forms.
**Necromantice** is the fire-spirit, the Principle <note>original: "Principium"</note> with its "necromancy" in the Son, in the Light-World.

The **Heavenly Virgin** is a luster and mirror of the Holy Spirit. He is the "seeing" within Her. She does not see of herself: she is the wonder, the colors. The Holy Spirit is the power within her. Were she the power, she would be the one who gives birth.

**Iliaster** is the Mystery of the Divine Being outside of Nature, which contains within itself the will toward Nature. That which is contained within the will is the being of Nature with its forms and properties. That is the beginning and the dark *Fiat* <note>Latin: "let it be," the creative command</note>, and it stands in harshness. In itself, it is the dark world; in the out-birth of the third Principle, it is the Earth. The two forms, Good and Evil (regarding the dark, severe form), are born from it. But whatever the spirit-form is in itself is called **Cagastrum**, the center of Nature.

**Necrolice** are the forms before the fire and the Principle in the chamber of anguish.

**Necromantice** is the Principle; the true fire-world or soul, which brings forth the second center in the light. That is called Necromantice: that is the revelation of the *Iliaster*; where the *Iliastrum* becomes manifest in fire and light. That is the noble image after God, a little God (containing the Trinity <note>original: "Dreyzahl"</note>) or God Himself. It is also the Holy Trinity outside of form, insofar as the Principle is ignited. From the Trinity, the image is ignited by the Majestic Light into Divine understanding.

**Tincture** is the Virgin, as the life of Wisdom; it has a flying, spiritual life, and is the sharp spirit in Wisdom. According to its being, it is the Virginal Spirit: it is in the substantiality

# Of the Most Principal Points and Words

<note>original: "der vornehmsten Puncten u. Wörter"</note>

...presence, the other opener of Wisdom according to the Spirit of God: It is the luster of Wisdom: It is the body of the Holy Spirit or of the Deity. In her, the Holy Spirit opens the wonders; here she is dissolved: She belongs to the wonders.

**The Fifth Essence** <note>original: "Quinta Essentia"; the purest, most concentrated medicinal or spiritual force</note> is the quality of Paradise in the heavenly world: In the outer world, it is enclosed. When it is dissolved, it exists as a **Tincture** <note>a spiritualizing agent or "dye" that transforms matter</note>: Yet because it is in the fire, it is only half open: If it were entirely open, what should it not do? Outside the fire, the Tincture and that which is tinctured are enclosed again. In the New Man, it is open in the Fifth Essence, yet covered over by the Old Man; it can perform deeds and wonders, and move mountains.

The **Outer Mystery** (to which the stars belong) has all the power and being of the **Inner Mystery**. Only that it is an outgrowth, and is as a likeness: Into it belongs and enters everything that exists and can be named in this world: therein is Fire, Water, Earth, Air.

Air is the spirit of the Mystery.

**God’s Kingdom of Joy** is when one is set out of the chamber of anguish, through death, into freedom and into the Light.

**Zion** is in itself a paradisiacal being, and the form of Paradise: It is the budding forth of the paradisiacal being; which is hidden in the old Adamical man; and is understood in the New Man.

The government in Zion is the **Eternal Element**, in which everything lies. Zion greens forth from the being of the Element, as the essence from the earth: It is the greening of the new body in Christ.

**Wisdom**, however, is the eye of the Element and also of Zion, wherein all wonders are beheld; and out of the wonders comes the greening according to the form of the vision of that same mirror. Wisdom is not a being, nor does she take any being into herself; rather, she is the eye and understanding of the being: She dwells in the being; She is the wonder therein. Wisdom gives colors, or the formation of colors: Wisdom is the wonders which the Spirit beholds; she encompasses them all.

**Paradise** is substantiality: That is the true essence or substantiality.

The **Holy Element** is the life of that same being: It has that same life and being in itself, and is nearest to this world. The Element is substantiality and the life thereof.

<meta>This page continues the 'Clavis' or Key, providing definitions for the mystical terminology used throughout the author's work, specifically focusing on the relationship between the divine nature and the human soul.</meta>

# XX. Key, or Explanation of etc.

Light is Majesty. The Son is the Heart of the Light, and the power of Majesty.

The Father is the power of the fiery essences: He is the hidden Fire-World. This is not made manifest in the Kingdom of Heaven.

The Soul has the form of Nature: It is the Principle <note>original: "Principium"</note> of the Father: it possesses both the Dark-World and the Fire-World: It is these things itself. If the fire cannot burn, then the soul has no light and is a devil, whose fire also cannot burn.

The holy soul possesses the Spirit of Christ: This is the Holy Spirit, which proceeds out of the soul.

Turba <note>A Latin term meaning 'turmoil' or 'disturbance'; in this context, it refers to the breaking of divine order.</note> is: When the wrath opens itself in a thing, the good is destroyed: it is led into an anguish-torment in the property of the dark world. Turba is the will of wrath and anger, a disrupter: just as when sweetness becomes sour, like vinegar in milk, so that the milk is no longer what it was before.

Tincture <note>original: "Tinctura"; a term from alchemy referring to a transformative essence or spiritual 'dye' that ennobles matter.</note> is in all three Principles: It is the essentiality of the Holy Trinity. It is not a maker, but rather the life of the fire and light; an opener of all colors, leading everything to the highest degree in the Wonders. Just as the outward Tincture makes gold; yet it does not "make" it, it only reveals it. Tincture is the life of Wisdom: The Holy Spirit is the life of all. Power goes out from the Center of Nature; it is at first painful, then in the light it is changed into something lovely and friendly.

The Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; the chaos or "Great Void" from which all things are manifested.</note> outside of Nature is entirely one with Wisdom: it has all forms, such as the Element and Paradise: The Tincture is the highest. Everything proceeds from the Great Mystery. This is in all three Principles: In every thing, according to its property. Every thing is something secret: It has come out of the Great Mystery and must return into it again.

Thus Man performs his wonders in the elements, and in his mystery within him, and leads them into the root of the elements, and thus into the Mystery. When the elements pass away, the Mystery remains.

The soul is a fire along with this Mystery; therefore, man must give an account for it in the Mystery.

Sickness <note>original: "Sucht"; here likely referring to a 'craving' or 'drive' that initiates the process of nature.</note> is the original state of Nature.

Will is God the Father of Eternity.

->T H E  E N D.<-

It concludes the 'Key' by emphasizing man's responsibility for the spiritual 'wonders' he creates within the elements.</summary>
